# سجل الجلسات — SRU Employee Performance App

> **ما هذا الملف؟** السجل الزمني الكامل لكل ما بُني وتحقق منه في هذا المشروع منذ 2026-07-16، منقولًا حرفيًا من `CLAUDE.md` §2-C في 2026-10-07 دون أي تعديل في النص. كان يعيش هناك في ثلاثة أسطر بلغ أحدها 435 ألف حرف، فصار البحث والمراجعة في `CLAUDE.md` شبه مستحيلين واختلط التاريخ بالحالة الحالية.
>
> **كيف يُستخدم:** هذا مرجع تاريخي — لماذا اتُّخذ قرار، وكيف تحقق منه أحد، وما الثغرات التي اكتُشفت وأُصلحت. الحالة الحالية والقواعد الملزمة تبقى في `CLAUDE.md`، وسجل التسليم المختصر لكل تغيير في `HANDOVER.md`. **لا تُضف إلى هذا الملف إلا بنقل فقرات تاريخية من `CLAUDE.md` إليه**؛ التوثيق الجديد يذهب إلى `HANDOVER.md` كما تنص قاعدة PROJECT_STRICT.
>
> **تحذير عن تقادم المحتوى:** كثير من الفقرات تصف لقطة من `role_permissions` أو من حالة جدول بتاريخها. المصفوفة قابلة للتعديل من `/admin` وقت التشغيل، وقد ضُلِّل المشروع مرتين بلقطة موثّقة تقادمت — أعد الاستعلام من قاعدة البيانات قبل الاعتماد على أي «من يملك ماذا» هنا.

---

## الجزء الأول (2026-07-16 → 2026-08-04)

- **`.env.local` exists now with real Supabase credentials** (2026-07-16) — `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` (Publishable key), `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` (Secret key), and `DATABASE_URL` (Session pooler — `db.<ref>.supabase.co` direct-connection is IPv6-only and unreachable from this dev network; use the pooler host for any raw `psql`/migration work). Seven migrations under `supabase/migrations/` have been run and verified against this real database: `roles`/`role_permissions`/`user_roles` (12 seeded roles), the full competency framework (27 competencies, 108 behavioral-level rows), `org_units` (58 units, self-referencing tree), `profiles` (linked to `auth.users`, RESTRICT on delete, `job_title_id` deliberately FK-less until `job_titles` exists), the `check_vpra()`/`is_org_unit_in_scope()` SECURITY DEFINER functions (the central VPRA+scope check from `SRU_System_Design.md` §C — real logic, not the doc's illustrative sketch), — as of migration 6 — **32 real RLS policies replacing the zero-policy default-deny on all 9 tables**, gated by `check_vpra()`, and — as of migration 7 — **a 107-row `role_permissions` baseline matrix (12 roles x up to 12 process areas each)**. The matrix was a first-draft proposal (every cell `[استنتاج]`) that has since had its 4 least-documented roles (mentor, field_supervisor, strategy_admin, competencies_admin) reviewed and corrected with the project owner (migration 8 adds `strategy_admin.vacancies=view`; 108 rows total now). End-to-end behavior verified: an `employee`-role test user sees all 27 competencies but zero `org_units`/`roles` rows, while an `hr_admin`-role test user sees all 58 org units and all 12 roles, proving the full chain (`role_permissions` -> `check_vpra()` -> RLS) actually works with real data, not just structurally. That review also surfaced a real gap in `src/lib/vpra.ts`: `field_supervisor` held a flat `evaluation=prepare` grant but wasn't one of the 5 actors in the §4-A per-state lifecycle table, so its evaluation access would have bypassed the draft→...→finalized state gating that `supervisor` goes through. Fixed by adding `field_supervisor` as a 6th `EvaluationActorRole` mirroring `supervisor` at every state (explicit 2026-07-16 decision, not a CLAUDE.md transcription — §4-A's table still names only 5 in the doc text) — `vpra.test.ts` updated to match, 88 tests passing. `check_vpra()` execute is granted to `authenticated` only (a first attempt via `REVOKE ... FROM PUBLIC` alone did **not** actually block `anon` — this project has `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` granting `EXECUTE`/full table privileges (including TRUNCATE, which bypasses RLS — a known, deliberately-unaddressed tech-debt item) to `anon`/`authenticated`/`service_role` on everything new in `public`; any future function needs an explicit `REVOKE ... FROM anon`, not just `FROM PUBLIC`). Migration 6's policy design rests on several **[استنتاج]** business-rule assumptions not documented elsewhere (process_area per table, e.g. `org_units`/`profiles` → `employeeData` since no dedicated process area for org structure exists; write levels; which tables get a real DELETE policy vs. soft-delete-only) — flagged in the migration's own header, verified end-to-end via `SET ROLE authenticated` + simulated JWT inside a rolled-back transaction (process_area isolation, `all` vs `org_unit` scope enforcement via the org tree, and self-row visibility all confirmed working), but not signed off by the project owner at the time -- formally reviewed and approved 2026-07-19 (see the dated entry near the end of this section for details).

- **Auth is now wired end-to-end** (2026-07-16): `src/lib/supabase/{client,server}.ts` (`@supabase/ssr`), `src/proxy.ts` refreshes the Supabase session alongside next-intl's locale routing on every request (required per `@supabase/ssr`'s own docs — omitting it causes random logouts), and `src/app/[locale]/login/actions.ts` + `src/components/LoginForm.tsx` implement a real `signInWithPassword` flow (Zod-validated, generic error message, locale-aware redirect via `useActionState`). Verified live in-browser with a real (since-deleted) Supabase Auth user: wrong credentials show the real error message, correct credentials redirect to `/[locale]` and set a real `sb-<ref>-auth-token` session cookie. Fixed a real UI/design mismatch found while wiring this: the login form's first field was labeled "ID/Username" (`type="text"`) despite `SRU_System_Design.md` explicitly deciding on email+password auth — now `type="email"`, `messages/*.json`'s `LoginPage.idLabel/idPlaceholder` renamed to `emailLabel/emailPlaceholder`.

- **`auth.users`→`profiles` invite-linking trigger is now built and verified** (2026-07-16, migration 9): `profiles.id` was originally a direct FK to `auth.users.id` (migration 4), which made it impossible to create a `profiles` row before an invited employee's auth account existed — directly contradicting `SRU_System_Design.md` §C's documented invite flow (HR creates the profile first, invites later) and its bulk-import note ("import does not auto-create Auth accounts"). Fixed: `id` is now an independent surrogate key; added `email` (match key) and nullable `auth_user_id` (filled in by a new `SECURITY DEFINER` trigger, `link_profile_to_auth_user()`, on `auth.users` INSERT, matched by email); `profiles_select`'s self-visibility clause updated from `id = auth.uid()` to `auth_user_id = auth.uid()`. Verified with a real end-to-end sequence (profile created before any auth user existed, then a real Supabase Admin API user creation triggered the link, confirmed via `SET ROLE authenticated`, then `ON DELETE SET NULL` confirmed to preserve the profile when the auth account was deleted).

- **Invite Server Action + "add employee" screen are now built and verified** (2026-07-16): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/employees/new/{actions.ts,page.tsx}` + `src/components/EmployeeInviteForm.tsx` — creates a `profiles` row through the caller's own RLS-respecting client (`check_vpra('employeeData','prepare', orgUnitId)` enforced by Postgres itself, not just application code), then invites via `src/lib/supabase/admin.ts`'s service-role client (`auth.admin.inviteUserByEmail`), then writes an `audit_log` row either way (new minimal `audit_log` table, migration 10 — RLS-enabled/zero-policy like everything else, no retention job or viewer UI yet). Verified live: a real `hr_admin` test user (`all` scope) saw all 58 org units, created a real `profiles` row, and got a graceful "invite failed" message when Supabase itself rejected `@example.com` as a reserved test domain (confirmed independently via a raw `/auth/v1/invite` call — a different real domain hit Supabase's own send-rate-limit instead, proving the success path is structurally sound and today's failure was an external constraint, not a code defect); the resulting `audit_log` row had the correct `actor_id`/`action`/`entity_id`. A plain `employee`-role test user (no `employeeData` grant) saw a "no permission" message instead of the form, with zero org units leaked. All test users/rows cleaned up after.

- **Rate limiting on login and invite is now built and verified** (2026-07-16): `supabase/migrations/20260716000011_rate_limiting.sql` adds a self-contained Postgres fixed-window limiter (`rate_limit_attempts` table + `check_rate_limit()` SECURITY DEFINER function, no EXECUTE for `anon`/`authenticated` — called only via the service_role client in `src/lib/rate-limit.ts`) since no Upstash/Redis account exists for this project (CLAUDE.md §5-A names Upstash as an option, not a requirement) — correct for a single-instance deployment, explicitly flagged as not race-free across concurrent serverless instances without a real distributed store. Login is limited 5/15min per email + 20/15min per IP (`x-forwarded-for`, best-effort); the invite action is limited 20/hour per actor. Verified live: pre-filling the email bucket to its cap via SQL then submitting the real login form showed the correct "too many attempts" message instead of "invalid credentials," while a different email from the same browser/IP immediately succeeded normally (proving per-email isolation, not a global block).

- **Employees list screen is now built and verified** (2026-07-16): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/employees/page.tsx`, plus a new "Employees" `NavBar` item (`navItems.ts` now has 7 entries, not 6 — `NavBar.test.tsx` updated to match). Found and fixed a real bug during browser verification: the `org_units(name_ar)` embed (a many-to-one FK relationship) is actually returned by PostgREST as a single object, but supabase-js — with no generated `Database` types anywhere in this project — infers it as an array; indexing `[0]` to satisfy that wrong inferred type silently returned `undefined` at runtime (confirmed the real shape via a raw authenticated `curl` call to `/rest/v1/profiles?select=...,org_units(name_ar)`). Fixed with an explicit type cast matching the verified real shape, not the tool's incorrect inference — same pitfall will recur for any future embed query without generated types, so verify against the raw REST API rather than trusting `tsc`.

- **`job_families`/`job_titles` tables now exist** (2026-07-17, migration 12): schema only, deliberately zero seeded rows — unlike `org_units`/competencies (extracted from real uploaded source documents), no source file for job titles/career grades exists in this repo (the original `SalaryScale.xlsx` was deleted by the project owner before this session and never replaced); inventing placeholder rows would violate this project's no-fabricated-data discipline. This migration also completed two FKs deliberately deferred earlier: `profiles.job_title_id` -> `job_titles.id` (deferred since migration 4) and `competencies.job_family_id` -> `job_families.id` (deferred since migration 2). RLS uses real policies from creation (not the zero-policy-then-backfill pattern used for the first 9 tables) — `process_area = 'careerPath'`, a direct documented fit per CLAUDE.md §4, not an `[استنتاج]` guess. Verified end-to-end in a rolled-back transaction: `hr_admin` (careerPath=approve) could insert and see rows on both tables; `committee` (no careerPath grant) saw zero rows on both.

- **`career_path`/`salary_scale` tables now exist** (2026-07-17, migration 13): schema only, zero seeded rows, same reasoning as `job_titles` (no source file). `salary_scale` follows `SRU_System_Design.md` §B's `[معتمد]` decision (Steps A-G only, no allowances) and has a `CHECK` enforcing ascending steps (an inferred, undocumented constraint — the only sensible reading of "scale," but flagged as such). `career_path` blocks a job title pathing to itself. RLS: `career_path` uses `careerPath` (clean fit, like `job_titles`); `salary_scale`'s SELECT policy ORs `careerPath`/`employeeData` because `SRU_System_Design.md` §A's own route table states this exact dual-area rule for `/salary-scale` — a documented fact, not a guess (only the write-side gate, `careerPath` alone, is an inferred choice). Verified end-to-end in a rolled-back transaction, including the key case: a `committee`-role user (has `employeeData` but no `careerPath` grant) could see `salary_scale` rows via the OR but zero `career_path` rows — proving the dual-area logic actually discriminates, not just passes trivially. Both CHECK constraints (self-referencing path, descending salary steps) confirmed to reject invalid inserts.

- **Career path list screen is now built and verified** (2026-07-17): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/career-path/page.tsx`, plus a new "Career Path" `NavBar` item (`navItems.ts` now has 8 entries — `NavBar.test.tsx` updated to match). `career_path` has two FKs to the same `job_titles` table (`from_job_title_id`/`to_job_title_id`), which PostgREST can't auto-disambiguate — this time the embed query (`job_titles!from_job_title_id(...)`/`job_titles!to_job_title_id(...)`) was verified directly against the REST API *before* writing any code, avoiding a repeat of the `org_units` embed bug from the employees list page. Verified live: `hr_admin` saw a real career path row (job title names + grade levels + requirements) in both locales; `committee` (has `employeeData` but no `careerPath` grant) saw "no career paths yet" despite a real row existing — same result as migration 13's SQL-level RLS test, now confirmed through the actual UI.

- **Salary scale list screen is now built and verified** (2026-07-17): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/salary-scale/page.tsx`, plus a new "Salary Scale" `NavBar` item (now 9 entries). Single FK to `job_titles` (no dual-embed disambiguation needed, unlike `career_path`) — verified the embed shape against the REST API before writing code, same habit as the career path page.

- **Found and fixed a real RLS gap during live browser verification**: a `committee`-role user (has `employeeData` but no `careerPath`) could see a real `salary_scale` row's numbers (correct, via migration 13's OR-gate) but the embedded job title name rendered blank, because `job_titles`' own SELECT policy only checked `careerPath`, not `employeeData` — showing salary figures with zero context about whose job title they belong to isn't useful and has no real privacy rationale. Fixed in migration 14: `job_titles_select` now mirrors `salary_scale_select`'s exact `careerPath OR employeeData` condition (not extended to `job_families`, which isn't directly embedded next to any `employeeData`-gated view yet). Reverified live: the same `committee` user immediately saw the correct job title name after the fix, and the career path screen was confirmed unaffected (still zero rows for `committee`, since `career_path_select` itself still requires `careerPath` specifically).

- **Password reset UI is now built and verified** (2026-07-17): `src/app/[locale]/reset-password/page.tsx` + `src/components/ResetPasswordForm.tsx` — discovered missing when bootstrapping the project's first real `super_admin` account (a genuine production account for the project owner, created directly via `service_role`/psql since no admin existed yet to use `/employees/new`). The recovery email's link redirected to the home page with no form to act on the `#access_token=...` in the URL hash — this app had no password-reset page at all. Fixed: the new page relies on `@supabase/ssr`'s `createBrowserClient` having `detectSessionInUrl: true` by default (parses the hash client-side, since fragments never reach the server) and calls `supabase.auth.updateUser({ password })` directly. Verified live: old password rejected after reset, new password worked.

- **Root cause of the VPS deployment issues found and fixed** (2026-07-17): the browser console on the real deployed site (`https://my-path.aboazzam.art`) showed `Uncaught Error: @supabase/ssr: Your project's URL and API key are required to create a Supabase client!` — `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` env vars are inlined into the client bundle at `npm run build` time specifically, not at PM2 runtime; if they were only set for the running process and not present during the build itself, the bundle permanently bakes in `undefined` until rebuilt. This explains the reset-password page flashing and disappearing (uncaught client-side error unmounting the React tree) and plausibly the broader "some links don't work" report too. Can't fix this from here (no VPS access) — gave the project owner exact steps (copy the real `.env.local` to the VPS, `npm run build`, `pm2 restart`); not yet confirmed applied. **[تصحيح 2026-08-19 — هذه الجملة تقادمت]**: يوجد الآن وصول SSH فعلي للخادم من هذه البيئة (`ssh -i ~/.ssh/vps_key root@63.250.42.222`، اسم المضيف `server1.aboazzam.art`)، والأهم أن **النشر صار تلقائيًا**: عملية `webhook` على الخادم تشغّل `/var/www/my-path/deploy.sh` عند كل دمج إلى `main` (git pull ← `npm ci` ← `next build` ← `pm2 restart sru_hr_performance`، والتطبيق في `/var/www/my-path` خلف `https://my-path.aboazzam.art`)، وتنتهي بسطر `===== deploy finished OK: <ts> =====` في `/var/www/my-path/deploy.log`. فلا حاجة لإعطاء صاحب المشروع خطوات نشر يدوية بعد الآن — الصحيح بعد أي دمج هو التحقق فقط: `git log --oneline -1` داخل `/var/www/my-path` مقابل `origin/main`، و`tail deploy.log`، ثم إثبات أن البناء الجاري يحوي التغيير فعلًا بالبحث عن نصّ جديد داخل `.next` لا بالاكتفاء بمعرّف الالتزام. (`deploy.lock` الباقي على القرص طبيعي: `deploy.sh` يستخدمه مع `flock -w 300` لتسلسل النشرات المتزامنة، وليس قفلًا عالقًا فلا يُحذف. ويبقى صحيحًا أن متغيرات `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` تُحقن وقت البناء، فأي تغيير فيها يحتاج بناءً جديدًا لا مجرد `pm2 restart`.) **Separately, a real security gap was found and fixed**: `/admin`, `/competencies`, `/org-units`, and the home page had **no login check at all** (built before real auth existed, never retrofitted like the newer pages) — any unauthenticated visitor could view `/admin`'s full role/VPRA matrix. Fixed architecturally, not per-page: the auth check now lives once in `src/app/[locale]/(app)/layout.tsx`, covering every current and future page under `(app)/` automatically; the four newer pages' redundant per-page checks were removed since the layout now covers them. Verified live: unauthenticated visitors to `/admin`, `/competencies`, and `/` all redirect to `/login`; an authenticated user still sees `/admin` normally. (`/evaluations`, `/calibration`, `/promotions` showing 404 is correct — those pages were never built, not a bug.) `role_permissions` is seeded and was formally reviewed and approved by the project owner on 2026-07-19 (see the dated entry near the end of this section), real seed data for all four job/career/salary tables (needs a source file from the project owner), no bulk Excel import, no audit-log viewer. `src/lib/vpra.ts` (12 process areas, 5 VPRA levels, 12 roles, full evaluation-lifecycle table + transitions, unit-tested) and `src/lib/data/{competencies,org-units}.ts` remain the source of truth the migrations were generated from — keep them in sync if either changes.

- **`TopBar`'s login button is now real, not decorative** (2026-07-18, PR #9): it always rendered the static "login" label with no `onClick` at all, even though `(app)/layout.tsx` gates every page under `(app)/` on an already-authenticated user — reported live by the project owner after logging into production as the real `super_admin`. Fixed: the layout now fetches the caller's own `profiles.full_name_ar`/`full_name_en` (self-row, visible regardless of VPRA) and passes it to `TopBar` as `userName`; the button calls `supabase.auth.signOut()` (browser client) and redirects to `/login`. Verified live locally against the real Supabase project: logged in as the real super_admin, confirmed the name rendered instead of "تسجيل الدخول", clicked it, confirmed an actual sign-out + redirect back to the login form.

- **First slice of the evaluations module: `evaluation_cycles` + `evaluations` tables** (2026-07-18, migration 15, `20260718000001_evaluation_cycles_and_evaluations.sql`) — scope explicitly agreed with the project owner (asked directly, chose "cycles + evaluations skeleton" over a full screen) to avoid a single oversized change; `goal_library`/`goals`/`bau_tasks`/`feedback_360`/`evaluation_scores` and any UI/Server Action are deliberately out of scope. `evaluations.state` uses the exact 7 values from `vpra.ts`'s `EvaluationState`; `UNIQUE(employee_id, cycle_id)` (CLAUDE.md §5-A rule 10's own named example) is a partial unique index (`WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`) so a soft-deleted row doesn't block a replacement.

- **Caught and fixed a real RLS design flaw before it ever reached git**: the first draft gated the non-self-row branch of `evaluations`' SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE policies at `check_vpra('evaluation','prepare',...)` — but `employee` and `supervisor` hold the *identical* flat `prepare` grant on `evaluation` in the seeded matrix, and `check_vpra()` has no way to tell which role actually satisfied the check. This is precisely the risk this same file already flagged in its 2026-07-16 log (an `employee` role assigned with `scope_type='all'` — called out there as the likely default for an individual assignment — would satisfy the check exactly like a real supervisor, letting any employee read/write any other employee's evaluation row). Caught by re-reading that exact warning before considering the migration done, not by a failing test. Fixed by requiring `'approve'` instead — only `hr_admin` reaches `approve` on `evaluation` today, so the non-self branch is now unambiguously HR-only; this deliberately under-grants supervisor/manager/committee access to their reports' evaluations until either a `profiles.supervisor_id`-style relationship column or a full state-aware policy (mirroring `getEvaluationStatePermission`) exists — flagged as an explicit follow-up in the migration header, not silently deferred. Verified end-to-end in rolled-back transactions against the real database both before and after the fix: pre-fix, a simulated `employee`-role user (`scope_type='all'`) could see another employee's evaluation row (1 row); post-fix, the same scenario returns 0 rows, the user can still create/see their own row, and an `UPDATE` targeting the other employee's row silently affects 0 rows. No permanent data was written to the real database by any of this session's verification.

- **`goal_library` table now exists** (2026-07-18, migration 16, `20260718000002_goal_library.sql`): schema only, zero seeded rows (no source file, same reasoning as job_titles/career_path/salary_scale). Column set is transcribed from `SRU_System_Design.md`'s own "Goals / BAU" ERD sketch (line 169) rather than invented, with two `[استنتاج]` adjustments flagged in the migration header (`description` split into `description_ar`/`description_en`; `default_weight` given a `(0,100]` CHECK). That same doc section also documents `cycle_type`/`status` on `evaluation_cycles` and `eval_type`/`created_by`/`updated_at`/a separate `evaluation_scores` table that migration 15 (earlier the same day) did not implement, because that source wasn't found before writing migration 15 — flagged as a real documentation gap to reconcile before building any UI/action on top of `evaluation_cycles`/`evaluations`, not silently fixed retroactively here. `process_area = 'goalsLibrary'` (direct CLAUDE.md §4 fact); RLS mirrors job_titles/competencies exactly (flat `check_vpra` is safe here since this is shared reference data with no per-row ownership column, unlike `evaluations`) — SELECT at `view`, INSERT/UPDATE at `prepare`, no DELETE policy. Verified in rolled-back transactions: the weight CHECK constraint rejects an out-of-range value and accepts NULL/valid values; a real `strategy_admin` test user (the sole `approve`-level owner) could insert and see a row; a real `committee` test user (no `goalsLibrary` grant) saw zero rows and was rejected attempting to insert.

- **`goals` table now exists** (2026-07-18, migration 17, `20260718000003_goals.sql`): per-employee goal assignment, consuming `goal_library` as an optional template. Column set transcribed from the same `SRU_System_Design.md` ERD line as `goal_library`, same `_ar`/`_en` split adjustments, plus a new CHECK requiring exactly one of `goal_library_id`/`custom_title_ar` (never both, never neither). `status` deliberately has no CHECK enum (no documented vocabulary exists, unlike `evaluations.state`). Because this table has `employee_id` (unlike `goal_library`), the exact ambiguity risk fixed in `evaluations` had to be re-checked, not assumed away — the good news is `goalAssignment`'s seeded levels avoid it structurally: `employee` holds only `'view'` on `goalAssignment` (not `'prepare'`, unlike its `evaluation`/`bauTasks` grants), while `supervisor`/`field_supervisor` hold `'prepare'` and `manager` holds `'approve'`. Gating the non-self branch at `'prepare'` therefore cleanly excludes `employee` regardless of scope, with no ambiguity about which role satisfied it — unlike `evaluation`, where `employee` and `supervisor` shared the identical level. Trade-off: oversight roles capped at `'view'` (super_admin/ceo/cxo/hr_admin/strategy_admin) also don't clear `'prepare'`, so they lose broad RLS visibility for now, same deliberate under-provisioning as `evaluations`. INSERT/UPDATE have no self-row bypass at all — `employee` never reaches `'prepare'` on `goalAssignment`, correctly reflecting that goals are assigned by a supervisor, not self-authored (unlike `evaluation`, where employee explicitly prepares their own draft). Verified in rolled-back transactions: the title-source CHECK rejects both-set and neither-set; the weight CHECK rejects out-of-range; a real `supervisor` test user created and saw a goal for a different employee; a real plain `employee` test user saw zero rows for that other employee's goal but did see their own (self-row SELECT bypass), and was rejected outright trying to insert a goal even for themselves.

- **`bau_tasks` table now exists** (2026-07-18, migration 18, `20260718000004_bau_tasks.sql`): per-employee business-as-usual tasks, no library/template table (unlike goals). Column set transcribed from the same ERD line, same `_ar`/`_en` and weight-range adjustments; `status` again has no CHECK enum.

- **This is the `evaluations`-style ambiguity risk, not the `goals`-style safe case** — checked the seeded matrix explicitly rather than assuming: `employee` holds `'prepare'` on `bauTasks`, the SAME level as `supervisor`/`field_supervisor` (unlike `goalAssignment`, where `employee` was capped at `'view'`, one tier below). Gating the non-self branch at `'prepare'` would have repeated the exact evaluations hole. Fixed identically: self-row bypass (employee legitimately prepares their own BAU tasks — that's why their flat grant is `'prepare'` here, unlike `goalAssignment`) plus `check_vpra('bauTasks','approve',...)` for the non-self branch, which only `manager` reaches today. Deliberately under-grants `supervisor`/`field_supervisor` direct RLS access to their reports' BAU tasks, same documented follow-up debt as `evaluations`. Verified in rolled-back transactions: the weight CHECK rejects out-of-range; a real `employee` test user assigned `scope_type='all'` (the exact scenario the fix targets) saw zero rows for another employee's task despite sharing supervisor's flat level, but created/saw their own task, and was rejected inserting for someone else; a real `manager` test user (approve) saw and created tasks for a different employee.

- **`feedback_360` table now exists** (2026-07-18, migration 19, `20260718000005_feedback_360.sql`): the most explicitly documented table so far — its anonymity strategy is marked **`[معتمد]`** (approved, not inferred) in `SRU_System_Design.md`. Column set transcribed verbatim (`cycle_id`, `target_employee_id`, `evaluator_relation` enum, `evaluator_id`, `is_anonymous`, `scores` JSONB, `comments`, `submitted_at`). `comments` deliberately is NOT split into `_ar`/`_en` like `goals`/`goal_library`'s templated text — it's free-form prose one real evaluator types once in one language, not curated bilingual reference content; splitting it would be a category error.

- **The real challenge: hiding `evaluator_id` from the target employee cannot be done with RLS alone** (RLS filters rows, not columns, and the target legitimately needs to see the rest of their own row). Implemented with real Postgres column-level privileges, independent of RLS.

- **Found and fixed a genuine mechanism bug while verifying, not assumed**: a bare `REVOKE SELECT (evaluator_id) ... FROM authenticated` does nothing here, because this project's `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` (the same root cause as migration 5's anon-EXECUTE issue) grants `authenticated` full TABLE-level SELECT on every new table, and a table-level grant overrides a column-specific REVOKE. Fixed by revoking SELECT on the whole table first, then re-granting SELECT to `authenticated` on an explicit column list that omits `evaluator_id`. Verified directly: before the fix, `SELECT evaluator_id FROM feedback_360` as `authenticated` succeeded; after it, the same query fails with "permission denied for table feedback_360" (not a column-specific message, in this REVOKE-then-GRANT shape), while the granted columns select normally. The actual "reveal evaluator identity" action (super_admin-only, audit-logged) is a deliberate follow-up RPC, not part of this migration. Row-level RLS re-applies the exact `evaluations` fix: `process_area = 'evaluation'` (no dedicated area exists, but `SRU_System_Design.md` explicitly groups 360 feedback under the Evaluation module), and since `employee`/`supervisor` share the same flat `'prepare'` level there, the oversight branch gates at `'approve'` (hr_admin-only). SELECT allows self-as-target OR self-as-evaluator OR the approve branch; INSERT/UPDATE are deliberately MORE restrictive than the flat VPRA model — only `evaluator_id = caller` can write, no approve-level bypass, since impersonating who gave feedback would defeat the whole audit/dispute purpose. Verified in rolled-back transactions: the column lock failed before the fix and succeeded after; the unique-submission constraint rejects a duplicate (cycle, target, evaluator, relation); a real evaluator created and saw their own submission; the target employee saw their own feedback's comments but was blocked from reading `evaluator_id` on that same row; a `committee` test user (no approve) saw zero rows and couldn't insert impersonating another evaluator; an `hr_admin` test user (approve) saw the row via the oversight branch.

- **`evaluation_scores` table now exists** (2026-07-18, migration 20, `20260718000006_evaluation_scores.sql`): the last table in the documented evaluations ERD, linking one score to either a competency or a goal within one evaluation. `comment` is single-column (not `_ar`/`_en`), same reasoning as `feedback_360.comments`. `score` deliberately has NO range CHECK, unlike every `weight` column elsewhere — at least three plausible readings exist (1-4 matching competency behavioral levels, 1-5 Likert, 0-100 percentage) with no document to disambiguate, so a guessed range is flagged as an open point rather than invented.

- **Surfaced a real structural gap rather than working around it silently**: `SRU_System_Design.md`'s model has `evaluations.eval_type` (self/supervisor/peer/customer) distinguishing whose assessment a row represents, but the already-merged `evaluations` table doesn't have that column (a gap already flagged in migration 16's header, not fixed retroactively here either). Without it, there's no way to know whether a given score row is meant to be self-authored or supervisor-authored. Conservative resolution: SELECT allows the employee to read their own evaluation's scores, but INSERT/UPDATE have NO self-row bypass at all -- only `check_vpra('evaluation','approve',...)` (hr_admin-only) can write, until `eval_type` is resolved. `competency_id`/`goal_id` are mutually exclusive via the same XOR CHECK pattern as `goals`. Partial unique indexes (not a single multi-column UNIQUE) prevent double-scoring the same competency or goal within one evaluation -- a plain compound UNIQUE would silently miss duplicates whenever one of the two nullable columns is NULL, the same NULL-uniqueness gotcha this project already hit twice before (`org_units`' single-root constraint, `user_roles`' scope uniqueness). Verified in rolled-back transactions: both CHECK/uniqueness cases rejected correctly; a real `employee` test user read their own score but their UPDATE affected 0 rows (value unchanged); a `committee` test user (no approve) saw zero rows; an `hr_admin` test user (approve) read and successfully updated the score.

- **Evaluation state-transition Server Action built and verified** (2026-07-18): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/evaluations/[id]/actions.ts` (`transitionEvaluation`) — never accepts a client-supplied current or target state (CLAUDE.md §4-A's non-negotiable rule), reads the true state itself, and uses `src/lib/vpra.ts`'s already-unit-tested `canAdvanceEvaluationState`/`transitionEvaluationState` as the sole state-machine authority. Deliberately does not try to bypass or re-derive `evaluations`' existing RLS (migration 15) — both the read and the write go through the caller's own RLS-respecting client, so that policy remains the actual "can you touch this row" gate; the action only layers the state-machine check on top.

- **Found and fixed a real bug while verifying, not assumed**: the first design read the caller's role via a client-side embed (`user_roles.select('roles(role_code)')`), which silently fails for almost every role — `user_roles_select`'s RLS does grant unconditional self-row visibility, but `roles_select` (migration 6) requires a `userManagement` grant with no self-role exemption, so a plain employee's own `user_roles` row joins to nothing. Verified directly via SQL simulation: the exact join returned 0 rows for a real `employee`-role test user, while the same join for `super_admin` succeeded. Fixed with a new migration (`20260718000007_get_my_role_codes_function.sql`) adding `get_my_role_codes()`, a `SECURITY DEFINER` function matching `check_vpra()`'s established pattern — a narrow, purpose-built RLS bypass to answer "what are my own roles," not a general workaround; `EXECUTE` granted to `authenticated` only, explicitly revoked from `anon` (the same lesson as migration 5's anon-EXECUTE issue). Verified the fix directly: the same employee test user that failed the raw join got back `('employee')` from the new function. Known, documented limitation of the action itself (not a bug): since `evaluations`' RLS only allows non-self writes at `approve` (hr_admin-only), the action works today for exactly two cases — an employee submitting their own draft, and hr_admin's final `committee_reviewed -> approved` — while supervisor/manager/committee transitions are correctly rejected as "forbidden" until a supervisor-relationship mechanism exists; the action does not attempt to work around that gap. `approved -> finalized` is unreachable for any role by design (`vpra.ts` models it as system-triggered). Verified end-to-end in rolled-back transactions: the full sequence (read state -> read roles via the RPC -> guarded update -> audit_log write) succeeded for a real employee's draft-to-submitted transition with a matching audit entry; the same next transition was correctly blocked (0 rows) for a real supervisor test user; hr_admin's oversight read succeeded.

- **Goal library list screen is now built and verified** (2026-07-18): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/goals/library/page.tsx`, on the documented route `/[locale]/goals/library`, plus a new "Goal Library" `NavBar` item between Salary Scale and Evaluations (`navItems.ts` now has 10 entries — `NavBar.test.tsx` updated to match). Read-only list, same scope as the career-path/salary-scale screens (no add/edit form yet). Single nullable FK to `job_families` — verified the embed shape against the REST API with a real temporary row before writing code, same habit as career_path/salary_scale; goals with no `job_family_id` (general/all-families) render a clear label instead of a blank cell.

- **Noted but not fixed, since it isn't a live bug today**: `job_families_select`'s RLS only checks `careerPath`, not `goalsLibrary` (the same class of gap fixed for `job_titles_select` in migration 14) — but every role holding any `goalsLibrary` grant in the current seeded matrix also holds a `careerPath` grant, so the blank-job-family-name symptom can't currently reproduce; incidental to today's matrix, not a structural guarantee, flagged in HANDOVER for whoever next changes the role_permissions matrix. Verified live in the local browser (same real Supabase project): the empty state rendered correctly with zero rows; two real temporary rows (one with a job family, one general) rendered correctly in both locales, with weight formatted as a percentage and the description shown under the title. Test data cleaned up after (`goal_library`/`job_families` back to zero rows).

- **Assign goal screen and first `goals`-writing Server Action are now built and verified** (2026-07-18): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/goals/assign/{page.tsx,actions.ts}` + `src/components/AssignGoalForm.tsx`, on the documented route `/[locale]/goals/assign`, reached via a new button on the Goal Library page (no separate NavBar item, same pattern as `employees/new`). Form: employee select, cycle select, then either a goal-library dropdown or a custom-title field — exactly one, matching the DB's own `goals_title_source` CHECK, validated in the Zod schema too (`refine`) not just relied on at the DB layer. Mutual exclusivity in the UI uses direct DOM refs rather than React state, avoiding a real `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` ESLint violation from resetting state inside the success effect. The `assignGoal` action inserts through the caller's own RLS-respecting client (not service_role) — real authorization is `check_vpra('goalAssignment','prepare', org_unit_id)` enforced by Postgres itself (migration 17), not application code — then writes an `audit_log` row via the admin client on success. Verified live against the real database, not just SQL simulation: (1) logged in as the real `super_admin` (holds `goalAssignment=view` only, not `prepare`) and submitted the full form — got the real "no permission" message through the actual RLS path, confirmed `goals` stayed at zero rows; (2) created a real temporary Supabase Auth user with a real `supervisor` role via the Admin API, logged in, submitted a goal for a different employee — got the real success message, and confirmed directly in the database that both the `goals` row (correct employee/cycle/title/weight) and a matching `audit_log` entry (`action='goal_assigned'`, correct actor) were created. All test data (auth user, profiles, cycle, goal, audit row) deleted afterward.

- **BAU tasks assign screen is now built and verified** (2026-07-18): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/bau-tasks/{page.tsx,actions.ts}` + `src/components/BauTaskForm.tsx`, on the one documented route `/[locale]/bau-tasks` (unlike goals, there's no separate library/bank table or route for BAU tasks). New "BAU Tasks" `NavBar` item between Goal Library and Evaluations (`navItems.ts` now has 11 entries — `NavBar.test.tsx` updated) — added directly as a nav item rather than a button off an existing list page, since (unlike goal assignment) no BAU-tasks list page exists yet to attach one to.

- **This form differs from goal assignment in a real, security-relevant way, confirmed live rather than assumed**: `bau_tasks`' RLS (migration 18) is `self-row OR check_vpra('bauTasks','approve', org_unit_id)` — unlike `goals`, which has no self-row bypass at all — so ANY authenticated user can successfully assign a BAU task to themselves regardless of role (matching `employee`'s real `bauTasks=prepare` grant, since routine tasks are meant to be self-reported, unlike evaluation goals which are assigned by a supervisor), while assigning to a DIFFERENT employee still requires `approve` (manager-only). Verified live against the real database: logged in as the real `super_admin` (`bauTasks=view` only) and successfully self-assigned a task via the self-row RLS bypass despite not clearing `approve`; the same account then correctly failed to assign a task to a different real employee, with the database confirming exactly one row (the successful self-assignment) and a matching `audit_log` entry, and zero trace of the rejected attempt. All test data deleted afterward.

- **Evaluation cycles list screen is now built and verified** (2026-07-18): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/evaluations/page.tsx` — the first real content behind the `NavBar`'s "Evaluations" link, which has pointed at `/evaluations` since early in this session but genuinely 404'd until now (previously documented as expected behavior, not a bug, each time this section was updated). No `navItems.ts`/message changes needed for navigation — the link was already correctly wired, just waiting on a page. Scope: a read-only list of `evaluation_cycles` (name, start date, end date) — no create/edit form (that needs `hr_admin`-only `approve` level, not requested here). This is distinct from `SRU_System_Design.md`'s separately documented "evaluations list" route (individual employee evaluation records in the `evaluations` table) — the user specifically asked for "evaluation cycles," so this targets `evaluation_cycles`, not `evaluations`. RLS: `check_vpra('evaluation','view')`, which most roles hold since cycles are university-wide metadata, not org-unit scoped. Verified live in the local browser against the real database: the empty state rendered correctly; two real temporary cycles rendered correctly in the right order (newest first) in both locales — `name_en` was fetched but deliberately not displayed, matching every other list screen's convention of keeping domain names Arabic-only. Test data cleaned up after.

- **Evaluation detail screen is now built and verified** (2026-07-18): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/evaluations/[id]/page.tsx` + `src/components/EvaluationStateAction.tsx` — the first UI to actually consume the state-transition Server Action built in #16, which had no consumer until now. Shows employee name/number, cycle name, current state (`evaluationStateLabels` from `vpra.ts`, same convention as `AdminPage`: fixed Arabic lifecycle vocabulary regardless of locale, not translatable UI chrome), an "advance to [next state]" button, and the employee's goals/BAU tasks for that cycle (joined by `employee_id`+`cycle_id`, since neither table has a direct FK to `evaluations`). `profiles`/`evaluation_cycles` embeds verified as single objects against the REST API with a real temporary row before writing code. Noted an architectural point during review: goals/BAU-tasks visibility here is fully independent of the evaluation row's own visibility — e.g. `hr_admin` (approve on `evaluation`) sees the evaluation header but may see empty goals/tasks lists if they don't separately clear `goalAssignment`/`bauTasks`' own bar (they don't, today) — correct per each table's own RLS, not a bug, but worth remembering. The transition button is a Client Component calling `transitionEvaluation` directly via `useTransition` (a plain function, not FormData-based), then `router.refresh()` on success to re-fetch and display the new state immediately. Verified live against the real database with two complete scenarios: logged in as the real `super_admin`, viewed a real evaluation with a real goal/task, attempted to advance it -- got the real "no permission" message because `super_admin` isn't one of the six roles modeled in the evaluation lifecycle table (a genuinely useful negative test, not an error); then created a real temporary `employee`-role Supabase Auth user with their own real evaluation, logged in, and successfully advanced draft -> submitted -- the displayed state and next-transition target updated immediately without a manual reload, and the database confirmed both the updated row and a matching `audit_log` entry. All test data deleted afterward.

- **"Create Evaluation" from the evaluation cycles screen is now built and verified** (2026-07-18): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/evaluations/new/{page.tsx,actions.ts}` + `src/components/CreateEvaluationForm.tsx`, closing the gap flagged in the previous log entry ("no Server Action to create a new evaluations row at all"). A new "Create Evaluation" button per row on the cycles list (`/evaluations`) opens `/evaluations/new?cycleId=<id>`, with the cycle pre-selected via Next 16's async `searchParams`. The `createEvaluation` action validates only `employeeId`/`cycleId` (state always starts at `draft`), inserts through the caller's RLS-respecting client (the same `evaluations` policy from #10: self-row or `hr_admin`-level approve), then **redirects directly** to the new evaluation's detail page (`redirect({href, locale})`, `locale` bound client-side same as `login`) rather than showing an inline success message — a deliberate small deviation from the `goals`/`bau-tasks` pattern, since jumping straight to the created record is the more useful destination here. The existing `evaluations_employee_cycle_active_uidx` unique constraint is handled explicitly: a duplicate (employee, cycle) insert returns a clear "an evaluation already exists" message instead of a generic error. Verified live against the real database: created a real evaluation for the real `super_admin` via the actual button-driven flow, confirmed the redirect landed on the new record's detail page with `state=draft` in the database; then attempted the identical (employee, cycle) creation again and confirmed the real duplicate error message with exactly one row still present. All test data deleted afterward.

- **"My Evaluations" screen is now built and verified** (2026-07-18): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/evaluations/mine/page.tsx`, closing the repeatedly-flagged "no natural navigation path" gap. A new "My Evaluations" button on the cycles list header opens `/evaluations/mine`, listing the current user's own evaluations (cycle name, state, a "View" link to `/evaluations/[id]`) across all cycles.

- **Real security decision, verified rather than assumed**: relying on `evaluations`' default RLS alone (self-row OR `check_vpra('evaluation','approve',...)`) would have leaked EVERY evaluation in the system to any `approve`-level user (i.e. `hr_admin`) on this specific screen, since that RLS branch can't distinguish "show me my own evaluations" from "show me any evaluation my role permits." Fixed with an explicit `.eq('employee_id', <my own profile id>)` filter in the query itself, on top of RLS, so this screen specifically only ever shows rows where the caller is the employee, regardless of their role's broader access. Verified live against the real database with a scenario built specifically to prove the fix: created a real cycle and two real evaluations (one for `super_admin` themselves, one for a completely different employee); a real temporary `hr_admin` test user (holds `approve` on `evaluation` — exactly the scenario the fix targets) saw zero evaluations on this screen despite technically having approve-level access to both; logging in as the real `super_admin` showed exactly their own one evaluation (not the other employee's), and the "View" link correctly navigated to the right evaluation's detail page. All test data deleted afterward.

- **Direct-supervisor relationship mechanism built at last, plus "My Team's Evaluations" screen** (2026-07-18, migration `20260718000008_supervisor_relationship.sql`): closes the biggest architectural gap flagged repeatedly since the `evaluations` migration and re-raised in every subsequent slice (`goals`, `bau_tasks`). Added `profiles.supervisor_id` -- a self-referencing, nullable FK, one level only (not a full hierarchy walk like `org_units.parent_id`), with a CHECK preventing self-supervision. `[استنتاج]`, genuinely undocumented: `SRU_System_Design.md`'s own `profiles` ERD has no supervisor/manager column at all. Scope deliberately narrow: this migration only widens `evaluations_select` (read access); it does not touch `evaluations_insert/update` (state transitions) or `goals`/`bau_tasks`' RLS -- extending the same mechanism there is a natural next step, not done here. The new RLS branch (`emp.supervisor_id = caller's own profile id`) is a real FK match, not a flat role level, so it doesn't suffer the `employee`/`supervisor`-share-`prepare` ambiguity that forced the original non-self branch to `approve`-only; `check_vpra('evaluation','view',...)` is layered on top anyway as defense in depth. Verified in rolled-back transactions: the CHECK rejects self-supervision; a real `supervisor` test user with a real direct report (real `supervisor_id` link) who has a real evaluation could now see that evaluation (blocked before this migration), while an unrelated employee's evaluation stayed invisible. Also added `src/app/[locale]/(app)/evaluations/team/page.tsx` ("My Team's Evaluations"), reached via a new button next to "My Evaluations" on the cycles list -- same explicit-filter discipline as "My Evaluations" (results scoped to `employee_id IN (my actual direct reports via supervisor_id)`, not left to RLS alone, since an `approve`-level user would otherwise see everyone here too). Verified live in the local browser against the real database: a real temporary `supervisor` test user with a real direct report and a real evaluation saw that report's evaluation correctly on this screen after logging in. All test data deleted afterward. Known follow-up, not done here: same mechanism for `goals`/`bau_tasks` and for evaluation state transitions; no broader "manages this whole org unit" concept (only single-level direct reports); no UI yet to assign `supervisor_id` to a real employee (needs direct DB access or a future edit screen).

- **Extended the supervisor relationship to `goals`/`bau_tasks`, and caught two real bugs in the mechanism itself while doing it** (2026-07-18, migration `20260718000009_extend_supervisor_relationship_goals_bau_tasks.sql`). Asked to extend the just-merged `supervisor_id` pattern to two more tables; while writing the FIRST test (a real `org_unit`-scoped supervisor, not `'all'`-scope, whose report has no `org_unit_id` at all), the test failed instead of passing.

- **Bug #1, affecting the already-merged `evaluations_select` branch, not just this new work**: that branch's `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM profiles emp WHERE emp.id = <target> AND emp.supervisor_id = (SELECT id FROM profiles WHERE auth_user_id = auth.uid()))` runs the inner `profiles emp` lookup under `profiles_select`'s own RLS (self-row or `employeeData` view) -- so proving "this employee is my report" first requires being ALLOWED to read their profile row at all, which an `org_unit`-scoped supervisor whose scope doesn't cover the report may not have. Verified directly: a real `org_unit`-scoped supervisor test user couldn't even `SELECT` their real direct report's bare profile row (0 rows). The original migration's own test used `scope_type='all'`, which also grants unconditional `employeeData` view, accidentally masking this. Fixed with `is_my_direct_report()`, a `SECURITY DEFINER` function matching `check_vpra()`/`get_my_role_codes()`'s established pattern, bypassing `profiles_select`'s RLS specifically for this one relationship question; `evaluations_select` was redefined to use it, and `goals_select`/`bau_tasks_select` were built with it from the start.

- **Bug #2, caught before this migration was ever committed**: the first draft additionally required `check_vpra(<area>,'view', target_org_unit)` as "defense in depth" on top of `is_my_direct_report()` -- but `check_vpra()` always evaluates scope, and for `'org_unit'`-scoped roles requires a non-NULL, in-scope target, so the exact same org-unit-mismatch scenario broke this "extra" check too, silently defeating the fix a second time. Caught by re-running the identical adversarial test immediately after the first fix rather than assuming success. Resolved by dropping the extra check entirely: `supervisor_id` can only be set via a `profiles` UPDATE that itself requires `employeeData`-`prepare`, so the relationship's existence already is the authorization fact -- the same trust model this schema already uses for every self-row bypass. Verified in rolled-back transactions with the full adversarial scenario across all three tables at once: the `org_unit`-scoped supervisor saw their report's evaluation, goal, and BAU task (all three blocked before the second fix), while an unrelated employee's rows stayed invisible in every case; a separate regression check confirmed `hr_admin`'s `approve` branch and self-row branches were unaffected. All test data rolled back.

- **Extended the supervisor relationship to write access (INSERT/UPDATE) on `evaluations`/`goals`/`bau_tasks`** (2026-07-18, migration `20260718000010_supervisor_write_access.sql`), completing what both prior migrations explicitly deferred. Uses `is_my_direct_report()` with no additional `check_vpra` layered on top, for the same reason established fixing migration 24's second bug: `supervisor_id` can only be set via an already-authorized administrative action, so the relationship itself is the authorization fact.

- **Practical effect**: this is what makes the already-built `transitionEvaluation` Server Action (previously with zero real consumers able to exercise it) actually work for a real supervisor advancing their direct report's evaluation at the `submitted` state -- nothing in the action or in `vpra.ts` changed; the RLS write gate was the only blocker, and `vpra.ts`'s `canAdvanceEvaluationState` remains the sole authority on which transition is valid. Does not unlock `manager`/`committee` transitions -- those roles act one level removed from a direct report (org-unit-wide oversight, not a 1:1 relationship), which `supervisor_id` doesn't model; that remains a separate, undesigned follow-up. Verified in rolled-back transactions with the same adversarial scenario (org_unit-scoped supervisor, scope not covering the report): the supervisor created a real goal and BAU task for their report, and advanced their report's evaluation from `submitted` to `supervisor_reviewed` via the real code path; the identical actions against an unrelated employee were all correctly rejected (RLS violation on insert, zero rows affected on update). A separate regression check confirmed a plain employee still cannot self-author a goal, while self-creating their own BAU task and evaluation still work exactly as before.

- **Assign Supervisor screen is now built and verified** (2026-07-18): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/employees/assign-supervisor/{page.tsx,actions.ts}` + `src/components/AssignSupervisorForm.tsx`, closing the last explicitly-flagged gap from the supervisor-relationship work ("no UI yet to assign supervisor_id"). New button on the Employees list header next to "Add Employee". Form: pick the employee, pick their direct supervisor (same full employee list for both), save. `assignSupervisor` validates with Zod (including a `refine` blocking self-supervision, mirroring the `profiles_supervisor_not_self` DB constraint but checked at the application layer too) then updates `profiles.supervisor_id` through the caller's RLS-respecting client -- real authorization is `check_vpra('employeeData','prepare', org_unit_id)` on `profiles_update`, the same level the existing employee-invite screen requires, met only by `hr_admin` today. Writes an `audit_log` row (`action='supervisor_assigned'`) via the admin client on success. Verified live against the real database: logged in as the real `super_admin` (`employeeData=view` only) and got the real "no permission" message with `supervisor_id` confirmed still NULL; created a real temporary `hr_admin` test user, logged in, successfully assigned a supervisor with the database confirming both the updated `supervisor_id` and a matching `audit_log` entry; then attempted to set an employee as their own supervisor with the same account and got the real validation error before the database was touched, with the prior valid assignment confirmed unchanged. All test data deleted afterward.

- **Manager org-unit oversight on `evaluations` is now built and verified** (2026-07-18, migration `20260718000011_manager_org_unit_oversight.sql`), closing the last gap explicitly flagged throughout the `supervisor_id` work: "manager/committee act one level removed from a direct report (org-unit-wide oversight), which `supervisor_id` doesn't model." **Key finding before writing any code**: this needed no new schema or function at all -- the existing `check_vpra()`/`user_roles.scope_type='org_unit'` mechanism (built since migration 4) already models exactly this concept. The only gap was that `evaluations`' non-self branches required `'approve'` (hr_admin-only) instead of `'recommend'`, the level `manager`/`committee` actually hold. Lowering the bar on `evaluations_select`/`evaluations_update` to `'recommend'` is the entire fix. This does NOT reopen the `employee`/`supervisor` ambiguity that forced the original `'approve'`-only gate: that risk was about an individual role sharing a level with a genuine oversight role; at `'recommend'`, only `manager` and `committee` qualify, both inherently oversight roles (a manager legitimately org-unit-scoped, a committee legitimately university-wide, matching its real function). `goals`/`bau_tasks` needed no change -- `manager` already holds `'approve'` on both, meeting their existing bars. `evaluations_insert` was deliberately left untouched (still self-row or hr_admin-only) -- creating a new evaluation is a distinct administrative action from overseeing an existing one. Verified in rolled-back transactions with an adversarial scenario: caught and corrected a flawed first attempt where the two "different" org units chosen were actually parent/child (checked `org_units.parent_id` directly rather than trusting the first result), then re-ran with two genuinely unrelated units under different branches of the tree. A real `manager` test user scoped to one org unit saw and advanced an in-unit employee's evaluation from `supervisor_reviewed` to `manager_recommended`, while an out-of-unit employee's evaluation stayed completely invisible and unmodifiable. A separate check confirmed `committee` (scope `'all'`) correctly gets broad visibility, matching its intended university-wide review function.

- **Resolved the `eval_type` gap on `evaluations` -- a real decision confirmed with the project owner, not assumed** (2026-07-19, migration `20260718000012_evaluation_eval_type.sql`). `SRU_System_Design.md` had documented `evaluations.eval_type ENUM('self','supervisor','peer','customer')` since migration 15, but those same four values already exist on `feedback_360.evaluator_relation` (marked `[معتمد]`), while CLAUDE.md §5-A rule 10 names "one evaluation per employee per cycle" as its own literal uniqueness example -- a real tension worth resolving explicitly rather than guessing. Asked directly: `eval_type` allows MULTIPLE evaluation rows per employee per cycle, one per perspective, meaning the existing `(employee_id, cycle_id)` unique index was now too strict. Added `eval_type` (new enum, NOT NULL, no default -- every future insert must specify it explicitly) and replaced the unique index with `(employee_id, cycle_id, eval_type)`. The §4-A lifecycle state machine applies uniformly to every eval_type (CLAUDE.md documents one table, not a per-perspective one) -- flagged as inferred, not assumed to need a lighter workflow for peer/customer evaluations. Added `EvalType`/`evalTypes`/`evalTypeLabels` to `vpra.ts` (same fixed-Arabic-vocabulary convention as `evaluationStateLabels`, +1 Vitest test, 89 total) and updated every screen this change touches: the create-evaluation form gained a required eval_type select, the detail page displays it in the header, and both "My Evaluations" and "My Team's Evaluations" gained an eval_type column to distinguish the now-possible multiple rows per cycle. Verified live against the real database: created two real evaluations for the same employee/cycle with different eval_types (self, then supervisor) through the actual form -- both succeeded and displayed correctly distinguished in the list and detail views; a third attempt at a duplicate (employee, cycle, self) was correctly rejected with the right error message. Hit and resolved an unrelated dev-server issue during verification (stale Turbopack cache causing spurious 404s even on the home page) by clearing `.next` and restarting -- not a code defect.

- **Resolved the `score` range/meaning gap on `evaluation_scores` -- a real decision confirmed with the project owner, same pattern as `eval_type`** (2026-07-19, migration `20260719000001_evaluation_scores_range.sql`). This gap had been flagged since the table's creation (migration 20, `20260718000006_evaluation_scores.sql`): "at least three plausible readings exist (1-4 matching competency behavioral levels, 1-5 Likert, 0-100 percentage) with no document to disambiguate." Asked directly via `AskUserQuestion`: `score` is a 0-100 percentage, applied uniformly whichever of `competency_id`/`goal_id` is set (the table has no column distinguishing the two cases' scale). Added `CHECK (score IS NULL OR (score >= 0 AND score <= 100))` -- `NULL` still explicitly allowed (a score row can exist before scoring happens), bounds inclusive (0 and 100 both valid, the natural reading of "percentage," flagged as `[استنتاج]` since not separately confirmed). Table was confirmed empty in production before applying (`SELECT count(*) FROM evaluation_scores` -> 0), so a plain `ALTER TABLE ... ADD CONSTRAINT` needed no backfill/validation-against-existing-rows step.

- **Did not touch this table's RLS**, despite noticing while reading its policies that they still use the older `check_vpra('evaluation','approve',...)`-only pattern (no `is_my_direct_report()`, no `'recommend'`-level manager-oversight branch) that predates migrations 9-11's fixes to `evaluations`/`goals`/`bau_tasks` -- a real, currently-unaddressed gap, flagged here as a follow-up rather than fixed opportunistically inside an unrelated, narrowly-scoped migration (per PROJECT_STRICT.md's "patch not rewrite -- no changes outside Scope even if you notice a problem" rule). Verified in a rolled-back transaction using real FK-chained rows (a real existing profile and competency, a temporary cycle/evaluation created and rolled back): `score=101` and `score=-1` both correctly rejected with `check_violation`; `score=0` and `score=85.5` both correctly accepted. Confirmed production `evaluation_scores` still has zero rows and the constraint is live after applying. No `vpra.ts`/UI changes needed -- no UI exists yet for entering/viewing scores, and this is a pure DB-level constraint with no application-layer counterpart to keep in sync.

- **Extended the direct-supervisor relationship to `evaluation_scores`** (2026-07-19, migration `20260719000002_evaluation_scores_direct_report.sql`), closing the follow-up flagged in the previous entry: this table's RLS still used the older `check_vpra('evaluation','approve',...)`-only pattern, predating migrations 9-11's `is_my_direct_report()` fixes to `evaluations`/`goals`/`bau_tasks`. Mirrors migrations 9/10 exactly: added `OR is_my_direct_report(<employee_id>)` to SELECT, INSERT, and UPDATE, where `<employee_id>` is derived via the same `evaluations e JOIN profiles p` subquery this table's policies already used for `check_vpra`'s org_unit_id lookup (the table has no `employee_id` column of its own). Deliberately did NOT lower the `check_vpra` bar from `'approve'` to `'recommend'` the way migration 11 did for `evaluations` -- that was a distinct decision tied to `manager`/`committee` both holding `'recommend'` there, not bundled into this migration; flagged as a separate, still-open follow-up. Did not add a self-row bypass to INSERT/UPDATE (none existed before, none requested here) and left the existing self-row SELECT bypass untouched. Verified in rolled-back transactions with the same adversarial scenario as migrations 9-11 (real `org_unit`-scoped supervisor whose scope does NOT cover their report's org unit, via two genuinely unrelated org units confirmed different-parent beforehand): the supervisor could SELECT, UPDATE, and INSERT `evaluation_scores` rows on their real direct report's evaluation, while the identical actions against an unrelated employee's evaluation were rejected (0 rows / insufficient_privilege). A separate regression check in a second rolled-back transaction confirmed the pre-existing self-row SELECT bypass (a plain employee reading their own score) and the `check_vpra('approve')` branch (a real `hr_admin` scope-all test user reading and updating any score) both still work unchanged. Production `evaluation_scores` confirmed to remain at 0 rows throughout, and all temporary test profiles/auth users were created and rolled back, never committed.

- **Lowered the `check_vpra` bar on `evaluation_scores` from `'approve'` to `'recommend'`** (2026-07-19, migration `20260719000003_evaluation_scores_manager_oversight.sql`), resolving the follow-up flagged in both of the two preceding entries -- mirrors migration 11's identical change to `evaluations` exactly, and for the same reason: only `manager`/`committee` hold `'recommend'` on `evaluation` today, both inherently oversight roles, so this doesn't reopen the earlier employee/supervisor ambiguity. Same scope decision as migration 11 too: only SELECT and UPDATE were lowered; `evaluation_scores_insert` was deliberately left untouched (still `'approve'` OR `is_my_direct_report()`) -- migration 11 reasoned that creating a new record is a distinct administrative action from overseeing an existing one, and the same distinction was applied here (scoring a competency/goal for the first time vs. reviewing/adjusting an existing score) rather than separately re-confirmed with the project owner. Verified in a rolled-back transaction: a real `manager` test user scoped to one org unit could SELECT and UPDATE an in-scope employee's evaluation_scores row via the new `'recommend'` branch (blocked before this migration), while an out-of-scope employee's row stayed invisible/unmodifiable, and the SAME manager's attempt to INSERT a new score row was correctly rejected (confirming INSERT truly stayed `'approve'`-only). A real `committee` test user (scope `'all'`) correctly saw both org units' rows via the same `'recommend'` branch, matching its intended university-wide function. Production `evaluation_scores` confirmed to remain at 0 rows throughout.

- **First UI screen for entering evaluation scores is now built and verified** (2026-07-19): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/evaluations/[id]/scores/{page.tsx,actions.ts}` + `src/components/EvaluationScoresForm.tsx`, reached via a new "Enter Scores" button on the evaluation detail page. Renders one row per competency in the full framework (all 27 -- every role holds at least `view` on `competencyFramework`, checked directly in `role_permissions`, so this never renders empty for anyone who can reach the page at all) plus one row per the employee's own `goals` for that evaluation's cycle, each with a 0-100 score input and a free-text comment, prefilled from any existing `evaluation_scores` row.

- **Deliberately did not filter competencies by the employee's job family** -- `competencies.job_family_id` exists (nullable = general) but CLAUDE.md/SRU_System_Design.md don't document a rule for filtering evaluations by it, so showing the full framework was chosen over inventing one; flagged as a follow-up. The set of scorable subjects is never trusted from the client -- the Server Action re-fetches `competencies`/`goals` itself and only reads form fields named after IDs it just fetched, so a tampered request can only ever affect rows for subjects that genuinely apply to this evaluation.

- **Deliberately does NOT use `.upsert()`**: `evaluation_scores`' uniqueness is two PARTIAL indexes (`WHERE competency_id IS NOT NULL` / `WHERE goal_id IS NOT NULL`), and PostgREST's `on_conflict` inference can't carry a WHERE predicate, so a plain upsert would fail with "no unique or exclusion constraint matching the ON CONFLICT specification" -- worked around with an explicit select-then-insert-or-update per row (more round trips, acceptable at this table's row counts) rather than reaching for a raw-SQL RPC. A row with no score AND no comment is skipped entirely (not written as an all-NULL row) unless a prior row already exists, in which case leaving both fields blank on save genuinely clears it -- trusting the prefilled form state, same as every other edit-in-place screen in this app. All writes go through the caller's own RLS-respecting client, so real authorization is `evaluation_scores`' existing INSERT/UPDATE policies (20260719000002/000003), not application code. Verified live against the real database with a real temporary `hr_admin` test user and a real self-evaluation with one goal: submitted a score+comment for one competency and the one goal -- confirmed in the database that exactly 2 rows were created (not 28 all-null rows for the untouched competencies) and a matching `audit_log` entry (`evaluation_scores_saved`, `competencies_saved:1, goals_saved:1`); reloaded the page and confirmed both values came back prefilled; changed the competency score and re-submitted, confirmed the SAME row was updated (still exactly 2 rows total, not a duplicate) -- proving the select-then-insert-or-update logic actually distinguishes create from update, not just happens to work once. Hit and resolved the same stale-Turbopack-cache 404 issue documented earlier in this file (cleared `.next` and restarted, not a code defect). All test data (auth user, profile, cycle, evaluation, goal, scores, audit row) deleted afterward -- confirmed zero rows remaining in every touched table.

- **First UI screen for entering 360 feedback is now built and verified** (2026-07-19): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/feedback-360/{page.tsx,actions.ts}` + `src/components/Feedback360Form.tsx`, reached via a new "360 Feedback" `NavBar` item (`navItems.ts` now has 12 entries -- `NavBar.test.tsx` updated to match). Submits one `feedback_360` row (migration 20260718000005): pick a relation (self/supervisor/peer/customer), a target employee, a cycle, an optional 0-100 overall score, an optional free-text comment, and an anonymity checkbox (defaults checked, matching the DB's own `DEFAULT true`). `evaluator_relation` reuses `evalTypes`/`evalTypeLabels` directly from `vpra.ts` rather than a new label map -- the two enums share the exact same four values by documented design (`[معتمد]`), not coincidence. `scores` is a schemaless JSONB column with no documented question set anywhere; `[استنتاج]`: this form captures a single `overall_score` (0-100, mirroring the percentage convention just resolved for `evaluation_scores.score`) rather than inventing an undocumented multi-dimension rubric -- easy to extend later since JSONB has no CHECK to migrate. `evaluator_id` is NEVER taken from the form -- the action looks up the caller's own profile id itself, matching `feedback_360_insert`'s WITH CHECK (`evaluator_id = caller's own profile`), so impersonating another evaluator is structurally impossible, not just discouraged in the UI. A UX safeguard (not a DB constraint) rejects a 'self' relation targeting someone else or a non-self relation targeting the caller, mirroring the spirit of `profiles_supervisor_not_self`.

- **Found and fixed a real bug live during verification, not assumed**: the first draft auto-selected AND disabled the target `<select>` when relation='self' -- but a disabled form control is excluded from `FormData` entirely on submit, so `targetEmployeeId` silently vanished and every 'self' submission failed Zod validation (`invalid_input`), confirmed directly via the actual submission failing and a `FormData` dump showing the field missing. Fixed by auto-selecting the value without disabling the field, relying on the server-side self/target check as the real safety net instead of a client-side lock. Also noted, not fixed here: `profiles_select`'s RLS means a plain `employee` role (no `employeeData` grant) sees only themselves in the target dropdown -- they can still submit 'self' feedback but can't pick a colleague, same class of limitation already documented for `AssignGoalPage`; flagged as a follow-up, not a bug introduced by this screen. Verified live against the real database with a real temporary `hr_admin` test user and a real colleague profile: submitted real 'peer' feedback about the colleague with a score and comment -- confirmed the exact row (JSONB `{"overall_score":78}`, comment, `is_anonymous=true`, `submitted_at` set); resubmitting the identical (cycle, target, evaluator, relation) combination was correctly rejected with the real duplicate-submission error, confirmed still exactly 1 row afterward; selecting 'self' correctly auto-filled and (post-fix) correctly submitted a real self-feedback row. All test data (auth user, two profiles, cycle, feedback rows) deleted afterward -- confirmed zero rows remaining in every touched table.

- **First slice of the calibration engine: `calibration_sessions` + `calibration_results` tables** (2026-07-19, migration `20260719000004_calibration.sql`) -- CLAUDE.md §6's "Calibration & Rewards" module, scoped to schema + RLS only, mirroring exactly how `evaluation_cycles`/`evaluations` started (migration 15) before UI/Server Actions followed as separate slices.

- **Resolves CLAUDE.md §1's "Bell Curve / Forced Distribution" language in favor of `SRU_System_Design.md`'s already-dated, explicitly confirmed decision** ("Calibration: بدون توزيع إجباري (guided, not forced)"; "`calibration_sessions.mode = 'guided'` فقط -- حسب إجابتك" -- a direct project-owner answer from an earlier session, not re-asked here since it's already a settled fact, not an open question). `calibration_mode` is a Postgres ENUM with the single literal `'guided'`, matching the doc's ERD exactly rather than a free-form column. Column set transcribed verbatim from that same ERD; `status` is TEXT with no CHECK enum (no documented vocabulary, same precedent as `goals.status`/`bau_tasks.status`, defaults `'draft'`); `original_rating`/`calibrated_rating` get the identical 0-100 percentage CHECK just resolved for `evaluation_scores.score` (20260719000001) -- `[استنتاج]`, consistent with that established app-wide convention rather than a freshly guessed scale, not separately re-confirmed for this specific column.

- **RLS designed directly against the real seeded `role_permissions` matrix for `process_area='calibration'`, checked before writing any policy**: `hr_admin=approve`, `committee=recommend`, six other roles (ceo/cxo/manager/strategy_admin/competencies_admin/super_admin)=`view`, and critically -- unlike `evaluations`/`bau_tasks` -- **no individual/self role** (`employee`, `supervisor`, `field_supervisor`, `mentor`) holds any grant on `calibration` at all, so there is no risk of the employee/supervisor-share-a-level ambiguity that forced those tables' non-self write bar higher; gating writes at `'recommend'` is safe here since only the two genuinely administrative/oversight roles qualify. `calibration_sessions`: SELECT at `'view'`, INSERT at `'approve'` (hr_admin only -- creating a new session is a distinct administrative action, same distinction migration 11 drew), UPDATE at `'recommend'` (hr_admin + committee reviewing/progressing an existing session). `calibration_results` has no `org_unit_id` of its own -- derived via a join to `calibration_sessions`, same pattern `evaluation_scores` uses via `evaluations`; SELECT adds a self-row bypass (`[استنتاج]`, not separately documented for this table but the established default posture everywhere else in this schema) so an employee can see their own calibrated rating; INSERT stays `'approve'`-only (hr_admin seeds initial ratings); UPDATE at `'recommend'` with no self-row write bypass (an employee cannot calibrate their own rating). Partial unique index `(session_id, employee_id) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`, same NULL-safety reasoning as `evaluations`' own unique index. Verified in rolled-back transactions with real FK-chained data and a full adversarial matrix: a real `hr_admin` test user created a session and seeded a result; a real `committee` test user (scope `all`) could SELECT/UPDATE both tables but was correctly rejected on INSERT; a real `manager` test user (view-only, org_unit-scoped) could SELECT both tables in-scope but every UPDATE attempt affected 0 rows; a plain `employee` test user saw zero session rows but saw their OWN calibration result via the self-row bypass, with their own UPDATE attempt on it correctly blocked; a second scenario confirmed a manager scoped to one org unit sees zero rows for a session created in a genuinely unrelated org unit; the rating-range CHECK rejected an out-of-bounds value. Production `calibration_sessions`/`calibration_results` confirmed to remain at 0 rows throughout. No UI/Server Action yet -- a natural next slice, not built here.

- **UI for creating calibration sessions is now built and verified** (2026-07-19): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/calibration/{page.tsx}` (list) + `calibration/new/{page.tsx,actions.ts}` + `src/components/CreateCalibrationSessionForm.tsx`. `NavBar` already had a "المعايرة" item pointing at `/calibration` since early in the project (before real auth existed) that had genuinely 404'd until now -- same "link wired before the page existed" situation documented for `/evaluations` earlier in this file, not a bug introduced here. The list page shows existing sessions (cycle name, org unit name, status, notes) via `calibration_sessions_select`'s own RLS; a "New Session" button opens `/calibration/new`. The create form picks a cycle and org unit (both fetched through the caller's own RLS-respecting client, same "seeing an option here doesn't guarantee the insert succeeds" caveat as every other create screen) plus optional notes; `mode`/`status` are never exposed in the form -- they default to `'guided'`/`'draft'` at the DB level, matching the confirmed single-value mode decision. `createCalibrationSession` inserts through the caller's own RLS-respecting client -- real authorization is `calibration_sessions_insert`'s `check_vpra('calibration','approve', orgUnitId)`, `hr_admin`-only per the real seeded matrix -- then redirects to `/calibration` on success (same pattern as `createEvaluation`). Verified the `evaluation_cycles`/`org_units` embed shapes directly against the REST API with a real temporary row before writing the list page's query, same habit as every other embed query in this project. Verified live against the real database: a real temporary `hr_admin` test user (scope `all`) created a real session for a real cycle/org unit with notes -- confirmed the exact row (`mode='guided'`, `status='draft'`, correct cycle/org unit/notes) and the redirect landing back on the list page showing it correctly rendered.

- **Incidental finding, not a calibration bug, not fixed here**: while testing a real `manager` test user scoped to a single `org_unit` against `/calibration/new`, discovered that `evaluation_cycles_select`'s RLS calls `check_vpra('evaluation','view')` with NO org-unit argument at all -- since `check_vpra()`'s `org_unit`-scope branch requires a non-NULL target to pass, an `org_unit`-scoped role can never see ANY row in `evaluation_cycles` regardless of their `evaluation` grant, only `scope_type='all'` roles can.
