This predates calibration entirely (it's `evaluation_cycles`' own migration-15-era policy) and is orthogonal to calibration's own RLS (already fully verified correct in migration 20260719000004's adversarial matrix) -- flagged here as a real, separate follow-up rather than fixed opportunistically inside this narrowly-scoped UI PR. All test data (two temporary auth users/profiles, one cycle, one session) deleted afterward -- confirmed zero rows remaining in every touched table.

- **UI for entering calibrated ratings is now built and verified** (2026-07-19): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/calibration/[id]/{page.tsx,actions.ts}` + `src/components/CalibrationResultsForm.tsx`, reached via a new "إدخال الدرجات" button per row on the calibration sessions list. Lists every employee in the session's own `org_unit_id` (via `profiles`, RLS-scoped through `employeeData=view`, which `hr_admin`/`committee` both already hold alongside their `calibration` grant) with an original rating, calibrated rating, and justification input each, prefilled from any existing `calibration_results` row. The rated-employee set is never trusted from the client -- the Server Action re-fetches `profiles` filtered to the session's `org_unit_id` itself and only reads form fields named after IDs it just fetched, same discipline as `saveEvaluationScores`.

- **Deliberately does NOT use `.upsert()`**, for the identical reason as `evaluation_scores`: `calibration_results`' uniqueness is a PARTIAL index (`WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`), which PostgREST's `on_conflict` inference can't target -- handled with an explicit select-then-insert-or-update per row instead. A row with no original/calibrated rating AND no justification is skipped entirely (not written as an all-NULL row) unless a prior row already exists, in which case clearing all three fields on save genuinely clears it. All writes go through the caller's own RLS-respecting client, so real authorization is `calibration_results`' existing INSERT/UPDATE policies (20260719000004), not application code. Verified live against the real database with a real temporary `hr_admin` test user (scope `all`), a real org unit with two employee profiles plus the tester's own profile, and one real session: entered a rating+justification for one employee and a rating only for a second, leaving the tester's own row untouched -- confirmed in the database that exactly 2 rows were created (not 3 all-null rows) with the correct values; reloaded the page and confirmed both rows came back prefilled; changed one calibrated rating and re-submitted, confirmed the SAME row was updated (still exactly 2 rows total, not a duplicate) -- proving the select-then-insert-or-update logic actually distinguishes create from update. All test data (auth user, three profiles, cycle, session, results, audit row) deleted afterward -- confirmed zero rows remaining in every touched table.

- **`promotions` table + RLS, plus the reviewing/approving UI, built and verified together** (2026-07-19, migration `20260719000005_promotions.sql`) -- CLAUDE.md §6 "Promotions & Rewards"; the requested screen ("reviewing/approving promotions") had nothing to act on without the schema existing first, same "schema is a prerequisite" situation as calibration. Column set transcribed from `SRU_System_Design.md`'s ERD (`promotions(id, employee_id, cycle_id, from_job_title_id, to_job_title_id, status, approved_by)`). `status` is TEXT with no CHECK enum (no documented vocabulary, same precedent as `goals.status`/`calibration_sessions.status`, defaults `'pending'`); `from_job_title_id` is nullable (an employee may have no `job_titles` row yet), `to_job_title_id` required, with a CHECK blocking a "promotion" to the same title (mirrors `career_path`'s self-path block).

- **RLS designed directly against the real seeded `role_permissions` matrix for `process_area='promotions'`, checked before writing any policy**: `ceo=approve`, `cxo`/`hr_admin`/`manager`=`recommend`, five other roles=`view`, and no individual/self role holds any grant at all -- same clean situation as `calibration`. A single UPDATE policy at `'recommend'` covers both the initial proposal edits (manager/cxo/hr_admin) and `ceo`'s final approve/reject decision, since `'approve'` automatically satisfies the `'recommend'` bar via the VPRA rank ordering -- no second policy needed. `promotions` has no `org_unit_id` of its own -- derived via a join to `profiles.org_unit_id` for the employee being promoted, same pattern as `evaluation_scores`/`calibration_results`; SELECT adds a self-row bypass (`[استنتاج]`, consistent with the established default posture everywhere else in this schema) so an employee can see their own promotion record; INSERT/UPDATE have no self-row bypass (an employee cannot propose or approve their own promotion). UI: `src/app/[locale]/(app)/promotions/{page.tsx,actions.ts}` (list + `reviewPromotion` approve/reject action) + `promotions/new/{page.tsx,actions.ts}` (`proposePromotion` create form) + `src/components/{ProposePromotionForm,PromotionReviewActions}.tsx`, reached via the `NavBar`'s pre-existing "الترقيات" item (pointed at `/promotions` since early in the project, 404'd until now -- same "link wired before the page existed" situation as `/evaluations`/`/calibration`). The list shows Approve/Reject buttons only on `'pending'` rows; `reviewPromotion` sets `approved_by` to the caller's own profile id when approving, never taken from the client, and does not layer any extra role-specific gate on top of `promotions_update`'s own RLS (no documented distinct approval tier exists for promotions the way §4-A models one for evaluations, so none is invented). Verified `job_titles`' dual-FK-to-itself-style embed (here, `profiles`+two `job_titles` FKs on one table) directly against the REST API with real temporary rows before writing the list query -- confirmed PostgREST's ambiguous-relationship error first, then the correct explicit-hint syntax (`profiles!promotions_employee_id_fkey`, `job_titles!from_job_title_id`, `job_titles!to_job_title_id`), same habit as `career_path`. Verified live against the real database end-to-end: a real temporary `hr_admin` test user created a real promotion proposal (real employee, cycle, from/to job titles) -- confirmed the redirect landed back on the list showing it as `'pending'` with Approve/Reject buttons; clicking Approve set `status='approved'` and `approved_by` to the reviewer's own profile id, with a matching `audit_log` entry (`promotion_reviewed`, before/after `status`); a real temporary `committee` test user (view-only per the real matrix) saw the same pending promotion and its buttons rendered, but clicking Approve was correctly rejected with the real "forbidden" message and the status/approved_by confirmed unchanged in the database. Migration itself separately verified in rolled-back transactions beforehand with a full adversarial matrix (manager/hr_admin/cxo-style recommend create+update, ceo approve update, committee view-only blocked, employee self-row visibility, title-change CHECK). All test data (auth users, profiles, job family/titles, cycle, promotion, audit row) deleted afterward -- confirmed zero rows remaining in every touched table.

- **`rewards` table + RLS, plus the entering-rewards UI, built and verified together** (2026-07-19, migration `20260719000006_rewards.sql`) -- CLAUDE.md §6 "Promotions & Rewards"; same "schema is a prerequisite" situation as `promotions`/`calibration_sessions` before their own UIs, but this slice only builds entry (create + list), not a review/approve flow -- that wasn't asked for here, unlike `promotions`. Column set transcribed from `SRU_System_Design.md`'s ERD (`rewards(id, employee_id, cycle_id, reward_type, amount, status, approved_by)`). `reward_type`/`status` are TEXT with no CHECK enum (no documented vocabulary, same precedent as `promotions.status`), `status` defaults `'pending'`. `amount` deliberately does NOT get the 0-100 percentage CHECK used everywhere else in this schema -- it's a monetary figure with no natural upper bound, so only a non-negative CHECK (`amount IS NULL OR amount >= 0`) is added; no currency column exists and none is invented.

- **`process_area` reuses `'promotions'`** -- CLAUDE.md's 12 listed process areas have no dedicated one for rewards, and `SRU_System_Design.md`/CLAUDE.md §2 both document "Promotions & Rewards" as ONE bundled module, so this mirrors the same reasoning already used for `feedback_360` reusing `'evaluation'` and `org_units`/`profiles` reusing `'employeeData'` -- not a fresh invention. RLS therefore mirrors `promotions` exactly against the same already-verified matrix (`ceo=approve`, `cxo`/`hr_admin`/`manager`=`recommend`, no individual role holds any grant): self-row SELECT bypass, INSERT/UPDATE gated at `'recommend'` with no self-row bypass. UI: `src/app/[locale]/(app)/rewards/{page.tsx}` (list) + `rewards/new/{page.tsx,actions.ts}` (`enterReward` create form) + `src/components/EnterRewardForm.tsx`, reached via a new "المكافآت" `NavBar` item (`navItems.ts` now has 13 entries -- `NavBar.test.tsx` updated to match; unlike `promotions`, this route had no pre-existing dangling link, since `rewards` wasn't in the original nav table at all).

- **Found and fixed the same dual-FK-to-`profiles` ambiguity `promotions` had** (`employee_id` + `approved_by` both reference `profiles`) -- verified the exact failing PostgREST error first, then the correct explicit-hint query (`profiles!rewards_employee_id_fkey`) against the REST API with a real temporary row before writing the list page, same habit as `promotions`/`career_path`. Verified live against the real database: a real temporary `hr_admin` test user entered a real reward (real employee, cycle, type, amount) -- confirmed the redirect landed on the list showing employee/cycle/type/amount/status correctly (`status='pending'`); a real temporary `committee` test user (view-only per the real matrix, though they could still see the create form's employee/cycle dropdowns via their separate `employeeData` grant) had their submission correctly rejected with the real "forbidden" message, confirmed the reward count stayed at exactly 1 in the database. Migration itself separately verified beforehand in rolled-back transactions with the same adversarial matrix as `promotions` (manager/hr_admin/cxo-style recommend create+update, committee view-only blocked, employee self-row visibility, non-negative-amount CHECK). All test data (auth users, profiles, cycle, reward) deleted afterward -- confirmed zero rows remaining in every touched table.

- **UI for reviewing/approving rewards is now built and verified** (2026-07-19): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/rewards/actions.ts` (`reviewReward`) + `src/components/RewardReviewActions.tsx`, wired into the rewards list's new "إجراءات" column -- Approve/Reject buttons render only on `'pending'` rows, exactly mirroring `reviewPromotion`/`PromotionReviewActions` (same flat pending -> approved|rejected decision, no multi-step lifecycle invented; `rewards_update`'s existing `check_vpra('promotions','recommend', ...)` RLS -- 20260719000006 -- is the real authorization boundary, not application code; `approved_by` set to the caller's own profile id when approving, never from the client; no extra role-specific gate layered on top since no distinct approval tier is documented for rewards). Verified live against the real database: a real temporary `hr_admin` test user approved a real pending reward -- confirmed `status='approved'`, `approved_by` set to the reviewer's own profile id, and a matching `audit_log` entry (`reward_reviewed`, before/after `status`); a real temporary `committee` test user (view-only per the real matrix) saw the same pending reward and its buttons, but clicking Approve was correctly rejected with the real "forbidden" message, confirmed status/approved_by unchanged in the database afterward. All test data (auth users, profiles, cycle, reward, audit row) deleted afterward -- confirmed zero rows remaining in every touched table.

- **`vacancies` table + RLS, plus the list/create UI, built and verified together** (2026-07-19, migration `20260719000007_vacancies.sql`) -- CLAUDE.md §4's `vacancies` process area (also SRU_System_Design.md §B line 209), the last of the 12 process areas that had neither schema nor UI until now. Column set transcribed from the doc's ERD (`vacancies(id, job_title_id, org_unit_id, status, requirements)`); `requirements` split into `requirements_ar`/`requirements_en` (`[استنتاج]`, matching `career_path.requirements_ar`/`requirements_en`'s curated-content precedent, not `feedback_360.comments`'s free-form-prose precedent); `status` TEXT with no CHECK enum, defaults `'open'` (same precedent as `promotions.status`/`rewards.status`).

- **RLS checked directly against the real seeded `role_permissions` matrix for `process_area='vacancies'` before writing any policy**: `hr_admin=approve`, `manager=recommend`, and -- notably different from `promotions`/`rewards`/`calibration` -- `employee` genuinely holds `'view'` here too (internal job postings are meant to be visible to all staff, not just oversight roles); no role holds `'prepare'`, and only `hr_admin`/`manager` clear `'recommend'`+, so gating writes there is still safe (employee's `'view'` doesn't reach that bar). SELECT at `'view'` (broad), INSERT at `'approve'` (hr_admin only, creating a new posting is a distinct administrative action), UPDATE at `'recommend'` (hr_admin + manager, e.g. closing a vacancy in their own org unit). No self-row concept applies (vacancies aren't owned by an individual employee, same as `calibration_sessions`). UI: `src/app/[locale]/(app)/vacancies/{page.tsx}` (list) + `vacancies/new/{page.tsx,actions.ts}` (`createVacancy` form) + `src/components/CreateVacancyForm.tsx`, reached via a new "الشواغر" `NavBar` item (`navItems.ts` now has 14 entries -- `NavBar.test.tsx` updated). Verified the `job_titles`/`org_units` embed shapes directly against the REST API with a real temporary row before writing the list query -- both are single, unambiguous FKs here (unlike `promotions`/`rewards`' dual FKs to `profiles`).

- **Found and fixed a real RLS gap live during verification, not assumed**: a real `employee` test user (genuinely holding `vacancies=view`) could see a real vacancy row but its embedded org unit name rendered blank, because `org_units_select` only checked `employeeData`, not `vacancies` -- defeating the entire point of vacancies being visible to all staff. Fixed in a same-day follow-up migration (`20260719000008_org_units_vacancies_visibility.sql`), mirroring migration 14's exact `job_titles_select`/`salary_scale_select` fix shape: `org_units_select` now also accepts `check_vpra('vacancies','view', id)`. Reverified live: the same employee test user immediately saw the correct org unit name after the fix. Verified live against the real database end-to-end: a real temporary `hr_admin` test user created a real vacancy (real job title/org unit/requirements) -- confirmed the redirect landed on the list showing it correctly (`status='open'`); the same real temporary `employee` test user could view the vacancy (confirming the broad view grant) but was correctly rejected with the real "forbidden" message attempting to create one, with the vacancy count confirmed to stay at exactly 1 in the database. Migration itself separately verified beforehand in rolled-back transactions with a full adversarial matrix (hr_admin approve create, manager recommend update but not insert, employee view-only blocked on writes, cross-org-unit isolation for a scoped manager). All test data (auth users, profiles, job family/title, vacancy) deleted afterward -- confirmed zero rows remaining in every touched table.

- **The "assign supervisor" screen is now a real management screen, not a blind one-off form** (2026-07-19): the original `/employees/assign-supervisor` (built when `supervisor_id` was first added) only let the caller pick one employee and one supervisor with no visibility into who was already assigned to whom, and couldn't clear an assignment at all. Rebuilt as a bulk-editable table (`src/app/[locale]/(app)/employees/assign-supervisor/{page.tsx,actions.ts}` + `src/components/ManageSupervisorsForm.tsx`, replacing `AssignSupervisorForm.tsx` outright -- deleted, not kept alongside, since nothing referenced it anymore): one row per employee, each with a supervisor `<select>` (including a real "-- بلا مشرف --" / "no supervisor" option to clear it) prefilled from `profiles.supervisor_id`, excluding that row's own id from its own options (no client-side self-supervision possibility, on top of the existing `profiles_supervisor_not_self` DB CHECK). The managed-employee set is re-derived server-side from `profiles` itself, never trusted from the client, mirroring `saveEvaluationScores`/`saveCalibrationResults`'s discipline.

- **Deliberately does NOT use a blind per-row write**: the action re-fetches every employee's CURRENT `supervisor_id` first and only issues an UPDATE (and an `audit_log` row) for employees whose assignment actually changed, avoiding both a flood of no-op writes and a noisy audit trail when most rows are left untouched in a large bulk save. Authorization is unchanged from the original screen -- `profiles_update`'s `check_vpra('employeeData','prepare', org_unit_id)`, `hr_admin`-only per the seeded matrix. Verified live against the real database: a real temporary `hr_admin` test user saw a real pre-existing assignment correctly prefilled, cleared it and assigned a different employee to a different supervisor in the same submit -- confirmed in the database that exactly those 2 employees changed (not all 5 in the test set) with correct before/after `audit_log` entries; a real temporary `manager` test user (view-only on `employeeData`) could see the full table (including the just-made real assignment) but had an attempted change correctly rejected with the real "forbidden" message, confirmed the target employee's `supervisor_id` unchanged in the database afterward. All test data (auth users, profiles, audit rows) deleted afterward -- confirmed zero rows remaining in every touched table.

- **`feedback_360` evaluator-identity-reveal RPC is now built and verified** (2026-07-19, migration `20260719000009_reveal_feedback_360_evaluator.sql`), closing the follow-up flagged since `feedback_360`'s own creation (migration 20260718000005): `evaluator_id` is stored for audit/dispute purposes but hidden from everyone via column-level privileges, and this had remained a deliberate, un-built follow-up RPC until now.

- **Implements `SRU_System_Design.md`'s exact `[معتمد]` confirmed policy, not an inferred one**: identity is never shown via any RLS policy or view -- it appears only to `super_admin`, through a documented procedure logged to its own separate `audit_log` entry ("كشف هوية مقيّم — سبب: ..."). `reveal_feedback_360_evaluator(p_feedback_id, p_reason)` is a `SECURITY DEFINER` function matching the established pattern (`check_vpra()`/`get_my_role_codes()`/`is_my_direct_report()`) -- running as the function owner, it bypasses both RLS and the column-level lock, but only after (1) requiring a non-empty `p_reason`, (2) confirming the caller holds `super_admin` specifically via `get_my_role_codes()` (not `check_vpra()` -- this is a one-off super_admin-only power, no dedicated process area exists or is invented for it), and (3) confirming the target row exists. Any failure `RAISE`s rather than quietly returning nothing -- revealing PII deserves a loud failure, not a silent one. On success, writes the `audit_log` row (`action='feedback_360_evaluator_revealed'`, `after_data` carrying both the reason and the revealed `evaluator_id`) *before* returning the identity, so the audit trail exists independent of what the caller does next; returns only the evaluator's `profiles` identity (id/employee_number/full_name_ar), not their whole row. `EXECUTE` revoked from `PUBLIC`/`anon`, granted to `authenticated` only -- same established lesson as every other `SECURITY DEFINER` function in this project -- with the function's own internal role check (not the GRANT) doing the real restricting to `super_admin`. Verified in a rolled-back transaction with a full adversarial matrix against a real feedback_360 row: `anon` correctly denied at the GRANT level (no EXECUTE at all); a real `hr_admin` test user (not `super_admin`) correctly raised `insufficient_privilege` with zero `audit_log` rows written; a real `super_admin` test user with a blank/whitespace-only reason correctly raised "reason is required" with zero `audit_log` rows written; the same `super_admin` with a real reason on the real feedback row got back the true evaluator identity (matching the seeded evaluator exactly) and exactly one new `audit_log` row recording both the reason and the revealed id. No UI built in this migration -- the task asked specifically for the RPC; a super_admin-only "reveal" button/screen is a natural, separate follow-up, not assumed here. All test data (auth users, profiles, cycle, feedback row) rolled back -- confirmed zero rows remaining in every touched table.

- **The `role_permissions` matrix (108 rows, 12 roles x 12 process areas) is now formally reviewed and signed off by the project owner** (2026-07-19), closing the item flagged as an open follow-up since migration 6/7 ("first-draft proposal, every cell `[استنتاج]`") and repeated at every subsequent mention in this file. The full matrix was pulled directly from the live database (not from `src/lib/vpra.ts`'s seed data or any static file, to review what's actually enforced), rendered as a full role x process-area grid with VPRA levels color-coded, and presented to the project owner for direct review -- not decided unilaterally. Six structural observations were surfaced alongside the matrix for explicit confirmation rather than silently assumed correct: `hr_admin` is the sole `approve`-level role in 6 of 12 areas (evaluation, calibration, vacancies, careerPath, employeeData, and jointly with `super_admin` on defaultTemplates/userManagement); `strategy_admin` -- not `hr_admin` or `super_admin` -- is the sole approver for `goalsLibrary`; `ceo` is the sole approver for `promotions` (with `cxo`/`hr_admin`/`manager` at `recommend`); `employee` holds `prepare` only on `evaluation`/`bauTasks` and `view` elsewhere, with `none` on calibration/promotions/userManagement; `committee` holds `recommend` (not `approve`) on both `evaluation` and `calibration`, matching its collective-review, non-executive function; and `super_admin` deliberately does NOT hold blanket `approve` everywhere -- only on `defaultTemplates` and `userManagement`, a documented design choice (CLAUDE.md's own §4-A comment: "no role gets an undocumented implicit bypass") rather than an oversight. The project owner reviewed the full matrix and all six observations and approved the matrix as currently seeded, with no changes requested. This is a policy/business decision, not a code change -- no migration, no `vpra.ts` change, and no seed-data change resulted from this review; the sign-off is recorded here as the authoritative confirmation that the already-live 108-row matrix reflects actual intent, closing the "not yet signed off" caveat wherever it appeared earlier in this file.

- **"Needs My Review" evaluation queue is now built and verified** (2026-07-19): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/evaluations/review/page.tsx`, reached via a new button on the evaluation cycles list header alongside "My Evaluations"/"My Team's Evaluations". Closes a real gap: "My Team's Evaluations" only covers direct reports via `supervisor_id`, with no screen at all for the broader org-unit/university-wide oversight `evaluations_select` already grants `manager`/`committee`/`hr_admin` (migration 11's `'recommend'`-level branch, `is_my_direct_report()`, and the `'approve'` branch) -- those roles had no way to discover what needs their action without already knowing an evaluation's ID. This screen lists every evaluation visible via `evaluations_select` EXCLUDING the caller's own row (`.neq('employee_id', myProfile.id)` -- that's "My Evaluations"' job, not this one's), then filters in-memory to rows where `canAdvanceEvaluationState(state, role)` is true for at least one of the caller's real roles (fetched via the existing `get_my_role_codes()` RPC) -- i.e. genuinely actionable right now, not merely visible. Each row links to the existing evaluation detail page rather than duplicating the state-transition UI, since that page's `EvaluationStateAction` component already implements the actual advance action correctly.

- **Found and fixed a real crash live while verifying, not assumed**: clicking through to the detail page for an evaluation whose `evaluation_cycles` row an org_unit-scoped `manager` can't see (the pre-existing `evaluation_cycles_select` gap already documented in migration 20260719000004 -- `check_vpra('evaluation','view')` with no org-unit argument, so `org_unit`-scoped roles can never see any cycle row) threw an unhandled `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'name_ar')` and 500'd the entire page -- the detail page had assumed the `profiles`/`evaluation_cycles` embeds are always non-null. Fixed with a minimal null-safe rendering fix on that same page (`cycle?.name_ar ?? "—"`, `employee?.full_name_ar ?? "—"`), not by touching the underlying `evaluation_cycles_select` RLS gap itself, which stays a separate, already-flagged follow-up per PROJECT_STRICT.md's patch-not-rewrite rule. Verified live against the real database: a real temporary `manager` test user (org_unit-scoped) with a real in-scope employee's evaluation at `supervisor_reviewed` saw it correctly in the queue (with the cycle name rendering "—" as expected, the exact known gap, not a new bug); clicked through, hit the crash, confirmed the exact stack trace in the dev server logs, applied the null-safety fix, and reverified -- the detail page rendered correctly and the same manager successfully advanced the evaluation to `manager_recommended` through the real `transitionEvaluation` action; reloading the queue afterward correctly showed it empty, since `manager_recommended` needs `committee` next, not `manager`. All test data (auth user, two profiles, cycle, evaluation) deleted afterward.

- **Promotions & Rewards approval history screen built and verified, plus a new narrowly-scoped `audit_log` RLS policy** (2026-07-19, migration `20260719000010_audit_log_promotions_rewards_history.sql`): `src/app/[locale]/(app)/promotions/history/page.tsx`, reached via a new "سجل الاعتماد" button on both the promotions and rewards list pages. `audit_log` had been RLS-enabled with ZERO policies since its own creation (migration 10) -- a deliberate default-deny with "no viewer UI yet" flagged as a future follow-up. This migration does NOT open `audit_log` broadly (a general audit-log viewer for every action type is a separate, bigger, more sensitive decision, not asked for here) -- it adds exactly one SELECT policy restricted to `entity IN ('promotions','rewards')`, mirroring `promotions_select`/`rewards_select`'s own real org-unit scoping (joins to the promotion/reward's employee to resolve `org_unit_id`, then `check_vpra('promotions','view', org_unit_id)`) rather than a flattened global check; every other action type (`evaluation_state_transition`, `goal_assigned`, `supervisor_assigned`, `feedback_360_evaluator_revealed`, ...) stays completely unreadable through RLS, unchanged. No self-row bypass was added -- revealing "who rejected my promotion/reward" to the employee themselves wasn't asked for and stays an oversight-role-only screen, matching who already sees the org-wide list pages. The page reads `audit_log` for `action IN ('promotion_reviewed','reward_reviewed')`, then separately fetches the referenced `promotions`/`rewards` rows and the reviewing profiles (via `actor_id` -> `profiles.auth_user_id`) and merges them in application code into one unified table (date, type, employee, details, before->after decision, reviewer) -- necessary because the two entity types have different shapes and Supabase can't natively discriminated-union a join across them.

- **This is the one place in the app where a REJECTED promotion/reward's reviewer identity is visible at all** -- `reviewPromotion`/`reviewReward` only set `approved_by` on approval, never on rejection, so without this screen there was no way to see who rejected something, only the audit log's `actor_id` carries that. Verified live against the real database with a full adversarial matrix built directly (not through the UI, to save time re-testing already-verified review actions): a real temporary `committee` test user (scope `all`) saw all 3 history entries (an approved promotion, an approved reward, and -- critically -- a rejected promotion with no `approved_by` on the row itself) with correct employee names, details, decisions, and reviewer name; a real temporary `manager` test user (org_unit-scoped) saw only the 2 entries for their in-scope employee, correctly excluding the unrelated org unit's rejected promotion.

- **Surfaced a significant, broader systemic finding while verifying, not fixed here**: for the org_unit-scoped manager, the promotion's "to job title" detail and the committee reviewer's name both rendered as "—" instead of crashing (this page already degrades gracefully, unlike the earlier evaluation-detail crash). Investigating why revealed that `job_titles_select`'s RLS (`check_vpra('careerPath','view') OR check_vpra('employeeData','view')`) passes NO org-unit target at all -- and since `check_vpra()`'s `org_unit` scope branch requires a non-NULL target to pass, this means **any** `org_unit`-scoped role can never see job_titles at all, regardless of their real grant -- the exact same structural pattern already documented for `evaluation_cycles_select`, but this one likely also affects every other org-unit-agnostic catalog-style process area check with no target (`competencyFramework`, `goalsLibrary`, possibly others) -- university-wide reference data that legitimately shouldn't require org-unit scoping to view at all. This is a materially bigger, cross-cutting design question than the narrow one-off fixes made for `org_units_select`/`vacancies` or `job_titles_select`/`salary_scale` earlier -- it touches how `check_vpra()`'s scope model should treat organization-wide catalog data for every `org_unit`-scoped role across the whole app, not a single table's policy. Deliberately NOT fixed inside this narrowly-scoped history-screen migration; flagged here as the most significant open RLS follow-up in the project, needing an explicit design decision (very likely: any process-area check with no target parameter should treat `org_unit`-scoped roles as automatically satisfying scope, since there's no per-row unit to check against) before being addressed project-wide. The separate profiles-visibility case observed in the same test (the committee reviewer's own profile, which happened to have no `org_unit_id` set, was invisible to the org_unit-scoped manager) is a distinct, narrower, and likely-correct consequence of `profiles_select`'s existing real per-row org-unit scoping -- not conflated with the job_titles finding above. All test data (auth users, profiles, job family/titles, cycle, promotions, reward, audit rows) deleted afterward -- confirmed zero rows remaining in every touched table.

- **"Forgot password" screen is now built and verified** (2026-07-19), closing a real production incident reported directly by the project owner: a password-reset recovery link was manually triggered from the Supabase Dashboard (Auth -> Users -> Send recovery) for the real `m.almohaimeed@sr.edu.sa` account, and clicking it landed on the home page instead of `/reset-password`. Root cause, confirmed by grepping this entire codebase: there is NO call to `resetPasswordForEmail` anywhere in the app -- every password reset until now was Dashboard-triggered, which redirects using the Supabase project's configured Auth "Site URL" (not any `redirectTo` the app controls) since no in-app "forgot password" flow existed to pass one explicitly; this project's Site URL evidently doesn't point at `/reset-password`, so the recovery token landed on the home page unconsumed. Built `src/app/[locale]/forgot-password/{page.tsx,actions.ts}` + `src/components/ForgotPasswordForm.tsx`, plus a new "forgot password?" link on `LoginForm.tsx` (outside the `(app)/` route group, same as `login`/`reset-password`, so no auth gate applies). `requestPasswordReset` (the new Server Action) calls `supabase.auth.resetPasswordForEmail(email, { redirectTo })` with an EXPLICIT `redirectTo` computed from the request's own `x-forwarded-host`/`host` and `x-forwarded-proto` headers (no `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` exists in this project) -- trusting the deployment's reverse proxy to set these honestly, the same trust boundary this codebase already accepts for `x-forwarded-for` in `login`'s rate limiting -- so the destination is now correct regardless of the Dashboard's Site URL setting. Zod-validates the email and rate-limits via the existing `checkRateLimit` (3/15min per email -- stricter than login's 5, since this triggers a real email send; 10/15min per IP), reusing `check_rate_limit()` from migration `20260716000011_rate_limiting.sql` with new `forgot_password:email:`/`forgot_password:ip:` bucket keys -- no new migration needed.

- **Deliberately always returns the same `{ status: "sent" }` result** once past validation/rate-limiting, regardless of whether the email actually belongs to a real account or Supabase itself returned an error -- revealing account existence through this form's response would be a real email-enumeration leak, the same "one generic outcome" precedent `login`'s own undifferentiated `invalid_credentials` state already follows. Verified live in the local browser: submitted a non-existent test email (`not-a-real-test-user@example.internal`, chosen specifically so no real inbox would receive anything during verification) -- got the correct generic "if this email is linked to an account..." success message, not an error, confirming no enumeration leak; a temporary debug log (added and removed after the check) confirmed the computed `redirectTo` resolved to exactly `http://localhost:3000/ar/reset-password` in the dev environment, proving the mechanism computes the right destination rather than guessing; submitting a 4th time within 15 minutes correctly triggered the real rate-limit message instead of a 4th send. English locale (`/en/forgot-password`) rendered correctly with a working locale-aware "Back to login" link (`/en/login`). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (89 tests) all passed clean with no new failures. No migration or database change in this PR -- purely an application-layer fix reusing existing rate-limiting infrastructure. Fixed the systemic `check_vpra()` catalog-table scope gap, the project's most-flagged open follow-up** (2026-07-19, migration `20260719000011_check_vpra_global_catalog_fix.sql`) -- first surfaced for `evaluation_cycles` during the calibration work, found again for `job_titles` during the promotions/rewards history screen, each time deliberately deferred per PROJECT_STRICT.md's "patch not rewrite" rule rather than fixed inline. Root cause, confirmed by pulling the live `pg_policies` table and `check_vpra()`'s exact definition before writing anything: every RLS policy on a university-wide catalog/reference table (no per-row `org_unit_id` of its own) calls `check_vpra(process_area, level)` with only 2 arguments, relying on the 3rd parameter's `DEFAULT NULL` -- but `check_vpra()`'s `org_unit` scope branch requires `p_target_org_unit IS NOT NULL` to pass, so ANY `org_unit`-scoped role (manager, supervisor, field_supervisor, ...) could NEVER see these tables at all regardless of their real VPRA grant; only `scope_type='all'` roles ever could. Audited and confirmed this affects 13 tables end to end: `career_path`, `competencies`, `competency_domains`, `competency_levels`, `competency_pillars`, `evaluation_cycles`, `goal_library`, `job_families`, `job_titles`, `role_permissions`, `roles`, `salary_scale`, `user_roles` (~45 individual policy clauses total).

- **Deliberately did NOT fix this by changing `check_vpra()`'s own NULL-handling** -- `profiles.org_unit_id` is genuinely nullable (a real possible state, e.g. an unassigned bootstrap account), and every PER-ROW org-scoped table (`evaluations`, `goals`, `bau_tasks`, `promotions`, `rewards`, `vacancies`, `calibration_sessions`, `profiles`, `org_units`, ...) calls `check_vpra()` with a 3rd argument resolved from a real employee's `org_unit_id`, which can legitimately be NULL for that specific row; treating a NULL target as "scope passes" would have let an `org_unit`-scoped manager see every unassigned employee's evaluations/goals/promotions -- a real regression, not a fix. Instead added a new, distinctly-named function `check_vpra_global(process_area, level)` -- identical VPRA-level comparison logic, but no scope/org_unit condition at all, since catalog tables have no per-row org unit to scope against -- and swapped every bare 2-argument `check_vpra(...)` call across all 13 tables' policies (DROP + CREATE, same USING/WITH CHECK shape, e.g. `job_titles_select`'s/`salary_scale_select`'s `careerPath OR employeeData` OR-structure and `user_roles_select`'s self-row bypass both preserved exactly) to call `check_vpra_global(...)` instead.

- **A same-name 2-argument overload of `check_vpra` was deliberately rejected** (Postgres would consider it ambiguous against the existing 3-argument version's default).

- **The originally-planned safety rail -- removing `check_vpra`'s 3rd-argument `DEFAULT NULL` so a future accidental 2-argument call fails loudly instead of silently reintroducing this bug -- was attempted, then deliberately dropped after a real failure surfaced applying it**: Postgres refused the plain `CREATE OR REPLACE` ("cannot remove parameter defaults from existing function"), and the only alternative (`DROP FUNCTION ... CASCADE`) would have silently dropped every one of the ~15 unrelated real RLS policies that call the 3-argument form (evaluations, goals, bau_tasks, promotions, rewards, vacancies, calibration_*, profiles, org_units, audit_log), forcing this migration to also redefine all of them just to keep the safety rail -- a blast radius far beyond "catalog tables" and a direct PROJECT_STRICT.md scope violation; `check_vpra()` itself was left completely untouched, and the safety rail is flagged as its own separate, not-yet-built follow-up. Verified in a rolled-back transaction with real temporary rows (all 5 previously-empty catalog tables -- `job_titles`, `career_path`, `goal_library`, `job_families`, `salary_scale`, `evaluation_cycles` -- had zero production rows, so temp rows were inserted first) and a real `org_unit`-scoped `manager` test user: previously would have seen 0 rows on every one of these tables regardless of grant; after the fix, correctly saw rows on exactly the tables matching their real seeded grants (`careerPath=view` -> job_titles/career_path/job_families/salary_scale; `goalsLibrary=view` -> goal_library; `evaluation=recommend` -> evaluation_cycles) and correctly still saw 0 on `roles`/`role_permissions` (`userManagement=none`). A direct side-by-side call confirmed the exact mechanism: `check_vpra('evaluation','view')` (old 2-arg form) returned `false` for this manager while `check_vpra_global('evaluation','view')` returned `true` on the identical grant. A `committee` test user correctly saw 0 rows on `goal_library`/`job_families` (no qualifying grant) and correctly DID see `job_titles` via its legitimate `employeeData=view` grant (the existing OR-branch, not a leak). Regression-checked the untouched per-row path directly: a real `org_unit`-scoped manager still correctly saw an in-scope employee's `evaluations` row and correctly saw ZERO rows for an employee in a genuinely unrelated org-unit branch (verified via `is_org_unit_in_scope()` directly, after an initial test-setup mistake accidentally picked the tree's actual root as one "unrelated" unit -- everything is trivially in scope of the root, not a bug). All test data (auth users, profiles, job families/titles, career_path/salary_scale/goal_library/evaluation_cycles rows, evaluations) rolled back -- production catalog tables confirmed to remain exactly as before (5 of them still at 0 rows).

- **Worked around a genuine, still-unresolved Supabase-side password-reset redirect bug with a client-side fix** (2026-07-20): the project owner reported a real recovery link landing on the login page instead of `/reset-password` even after the `forgot-password` screen's explicit `redirectTo` was built (an earlier entry in this file). Methodically diagnosed via the Admin API's `generate_link` endpoint rather than guessing: confirmed the project's Site URL (`https://my-path.aboazzam.art`, no path) and a `https://my-path.aboazzam.art/**` wildcard entry in Redirect URLs are both correctly configured on the Supabase dashboard side (screenshots reviewed directly) -- yet EVERY `redirect_to` value tested, including a completely unrelated trivial path like `/foo`, gets silently stripped down to the bare Site URL. This isn't a wildcard-syntax mismatch with our specific path -- it's that no path is being honored at all, across `recovery` and `magiclink` types alike, and survived a hard dashboard refresh plus removing/re-adding the Redirect URLs entry. This looks like a genuine Supabase platform-side issue outside what's fixable from the dashboard or from this codebase's Supabase configuration; not pursued further here since a robust application-level workaround exists.

- **The fix**: `src/components/RecoveryHashRedirect.tsx`, a client component mounted once in `src/app/[locale]/layout.tsx` (so it runs on every page, not just login) -- Supabase's recovery link always appends the session tokens as a URL hash fragment (`#access_token=...&type=recovery&...`), and hash fragments are never sent to the server and survive every redirect the browser follows, regardless of where Supabase's broken `redirect_to` handling lands the user. On mount, it checks `window.location.hash` for `type=recovery` and -- unless already on `/reset-password` -- does a `window.location.replace` to `/${locale}/reset-password` with the hash preserved intact, sidestepping the platform bug entirely rather than depending on it ever being fixed. Verified live in the local dev browser: manually navigating to `/ar/login` with a realistic (fake) recovery hash attached correctly redirected to `/ar/reset-password` with the exact same hash preserved (confirmed via `window.location.href` inspection, not just visual page content) -- `ResetPasswordForm`'s own existing invalid-token handling then correctly reported the fake token as invalid, proving the hash truly reached and was processed by the reset-password page rather than silently dropped. A plain `/ar/login` load with no hash was reverified to stay put with no unwanted redirect (no regression to the normal login flow). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (89 tests) all passed clean.

- **Found and fixed the REAL reason recovery links never worked, superseding the redirect_to investigation as the actual blocker** (2026-07-20): after the redirect fix shipped, the project owner reported still landing on the login page after clicking a real reset email. Root-caused methodically rather than guessing again: generated real recovery links via the Admin API and fed them through actual browser navigations (not same-tab hash-only changes, which don't remount React and silently test stale state -- a real testing-methodology mistake caught and corrected mid-investigation by re-running with a genuinely fresh tab each time). Confirmed via `auth: { debug: true }` on the Supabase client that `_initialize()` was throwing `AuthPKCEGrantCodeExchangeError: Not a valid PKCE flow url.` on every single recovery link, before ever calling `/auth/v1/user` (confirmed separately via `read_network_requests` showing zero requests to that endpoint despite a genuinely valid, freshly-generated token). Root cause, found by reading `node_modules/@supabase/ssr/dist/main/createBrowserClient.js` directly: it spreads `options.auth` and THEN hardcodes `flowType: "pkce"` unconditionally afterward in the same object literal -- silently discarding any `flowType: "implicit"` override passed in, confirmed by reproducing the identical error even after explicitly setting it. Recovery links deliver `access_token`/`refresh_token` directly in the hash (the implicit-grant shape), permanently mismatched against the client's hardcoded PKCE flow -- this bug has nothing to do with `redirect_to`, `RecoveryHashRedirect`, or Supabase's dashboard config at all; it would have blocked every recovery link regardless of where it landed.

- **Fix**: `ResetPasswordForm.tsx` no longer relies on `@supabase/ssr`'s automatic `detectSessionInUrl` hash-parsing at all -- it parses `window.location.hash` itself and calls `supabase.auth.setSession({ access_token, refresh_token })` directly, which has no flow-type dependency whatsoever (verified by reading `GoTrueClient.js`'s `_setSession`, which just needs the two tokens). `src/lib/supabase/client.ts` documents the dead-end discovery so no one repeats it. Verified end-to-end with a real, freshly-generated recovery link for a real temporary test user: the actual password-set form rendered (not "invalid link"), submitting a new password succeeded, and the browser landed on `/ar` and *stayed there* -- proving a genuine session was established and persisted, not just a cosmetic form render, since `(app)/layout.tsx`'s auth gate would have bounced an unauthenticated visitor straight back to `/login`. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (89 tests) all passed clean. Test user and all generated tokens/scratch files deleted afterward.

- **Found and fixed the actual reason real (not admin-API-generated) recovery emails still failed, superseding both the redirect_to and the browser-client PKCE fixes** (2026-07-20): the project owner tested with a real production account (`m.almohaimeed@sr.edu.sa`) and reported the same "lands on login" symptom even clicking the link immediately -- ruling out email-scanner prefetch or genuine time-based expiry as the cause. The real recovery link the user received had a token prefixed `pkce_...`, completely different from every admin-API-generated test link used so far (which are always plain, unprefixed tokens since the Admin API's `generate_link` doesn't go through either `@supabase/ssr` wrapper) -- this was the missing piece the entire investigation had been blind to, since every prior test happened to route around the exact code path that actually breaks. Root cause: `src/app/[locale]/forgot-password/actions.ts`'s `requestPasswordReset` called `resetPasswordForEmail` through `@/lib/supabase/server`'s `createClient()` (`@supabase/ssr`'s `createServerClient`), which -- exactly like `createBrowserClient`, fixed earlier the same day -- unconditionally hardcodes `flowType: "pkce"`. A PKCE-flow recovery token's completion fundamentally requires a `code_verifier` stored as a cookie on the app's OWN domain, but the email link points at `supabase.co/auth/v1/verify` -- a different domain that can never receive that cookie -- so the exchange is structurally impossible, not a race with a scanner or a stale link; confirmed by curl-testing a real, freshly-issued link (via a real live email request through the actual form, not the Admin API) the instant it was generated and getting `error_code=otp_expired` immediately, before the user had any chance to click it themselves.

- **Fix**: swapped `createClient()` for a plain `@supabase/supabase-js` client constructed directly with `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` and explicit `persistSession: false`/`autoRefreshToken: false`, bypassing `@supabase/ssr` entirely for this one call -- its own default `flowType` is `"implicit"`, matching what `ResetPasswordForm`'s `setSession()`-based handling already expects, and costing nothing since this one-shot unauthenticated call never needed cookie/session handling in the first place. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (89 tests) all passed clean.

- **Real `job_titles`/`job_families`/`salary_scale` data now populated (2026-07-20, migration `20260720000001_job_titles_career_path_salary_scale_data.sql`)**, closing the "no source file" gap flagged since migrations 12/13 created these tables schema-only. Source: three real Excel files the project owner provided (`Career path.xlsx`, `SalaryScale.xlsx`, and a newer cleaner `Salary Scale.xlsx`) — not fabricated, per this project's no-invented-reference-data discipline. Extraction used Node.js + the `xlsx` (SheetJS) npm package rather than the usual Python/pandas approach, since this machine has no working Python install (only the Windows Store stub alias) — a genuine environment limitation, flagged rather than silently worked around. Deliberately excluded several sheets containing real employee PII (`Staff(Original)`, `Cost Implication`, `Sheet1` — names, salaries, birthdates) that were present in the source workbook but categorically out of scope for a "job titles / salary scale reference data" import. Two real schema gaps were found and fixed to fit the real data, not invented ahead of time: `job_title_category` widened from 2 to 5 enum values (`leadership`/`academic`/`admin`/`technical`/`labor` — the real data has 4 admin-track categories the project owner confirmed directly plus the existing academic track, not the 2 originally guessed), and `job_titles.grade_level`'s CHECK widened from 1-14 to 1-16 (the real data has 16 distinct grade levels C1-C5/A1-A5/T1-T3/B1-B3, not the originally-assumed 14-level career ladder from CLAUDE.md §3's illustrative text). `salary_scale` gained nullable `step_h`/`step_i` columns (academic's real scale has 9 steps A-I; admin's has 7, A-G) with an updated conditional ascending-order CHECK. Academic titles have no explicit grade-level column in the source, so `job_titles.grade_level` was assigned by ranking on real midpoint salary (`step_e`) descending — flagged as `[استنتاج]`, the only sensible reading available, not separately confirmed with the project owner. `job_titles.job_family_id` is NOT NULL, so two placeholder families were added for titles with no specific family in the source: "عام" (admin generic per-level titles) and "الأكاديمي" (all academic titles) — also `[استنتاج]`. One genuine data duplicate was found and resolved: "مقدم خدمة" (Service Provider) appeared twice under family "المرافق" at two different grade levels (a `UNIQUE(job_family_id, name_ar)` violation) — kept the grade-2 occurrence (better contextual fit alongside other general service roles), dropped the grade-4 one, flagged as an isolated low-risk judgment call rather than escalated. `career_path` population is deliberately out of scope here, deferred to a later round per the project owner's own instruction ("لدينا أربع فئات..." conversation) — the existing `/career-path` screen correctly shows its empty state, unaffected by this migration. Schema changes were applied to production first (verified safe since target tables were empty), then the data insert was dry-run in a rolled-back transaction with verification `SELECT`s (confirming 25 families / 272 job_titles / 272 salary_scale rows and correct category/grade distribution) before being committed for real. Verified live in-browser afterward with a temporary `hr_admin` test user (created via the Admin API, deleted after): `/salary-scale` correctly rendered real job titles, grades, and salary steps with proper Arabic-Indic digit formatting and the correct non-null `annual_increase_cap` only on grade-16 rows; `/career-path` correctly showed its "no career paths yet" empty state. Hit and resolved the same stale-Turbopack-cache spurious-404 issue documented earlier in this file (cleared `.next`, restarted) before this verification could proceed. `npx tsc --noEmit` and the full `vitest run` suite (89 tests) passed clean (no `vpra.ts` or application-code changes were needed — this was a pure data + schema-fitting migration).

- **`career_path` data now populated (2026-07-20, migration `20260720000002_career_path_data.sql`)**, closing the item explicitly deferred in the previous entry. Source: the same `Career path.xlsx`'s "Career Path" sheet — a career-ladder matrix (one job title per grade level per job-family column), not an explicit from→to list, and an older/draft version of the matrix already superseded by "Positios" for the job_titles import itself (its family-column headers don't even name-match the 25 `job_families` rows already in the database). Rather than trust this sheet's own (pre-widening) level numbers or its column-header family names, pairs were derived by matching job title **name text** against the already-imported `job_titles` table (name → id/grade_level/family), pairing same-name matches at adjacent `grade_level`s (N → N+1) within each sheet column, positionally for multi-title cells (e.g. "أ.استراتيجية، أ.إدارة مشاريع" ↔ "أ.أول.استراتيجية، أ.أول.إدارة مشاريع"). Any cell-pair with a different title count, an unmatched name, an ambiguous (non-unique) name, or a resulting grade gap ≠ 1 was skipped outright rather than guessed — confirmed directly with the project owner in plain conversation (not `AskUserQuestion`, consistent with their earlier preference in this same session) before writing anything. Result: 104 confident pairs (89 from the specialty-track columns + 15 from the flat "عام" generic ladder, grade 1→16, built directly from the already-known DB rows rather than reparsing the sheet, with qualification text still pulled from the sheet's per-level column) out of ~190 candidate adjacent-level cell-pairs; 84 skipped (29 count-mismatch, 25 from-not-found, 28 to-not-found, 2 grade-gap-mismatch) due to genuine wording drift/typos in this older sheet (e.g. "أستشاري مرافق" vs the authoritative "استشاري مرافق", multi-title cells that don't align 1:1 across levels) — not fixed by fuzzy-matching, since guessing at HR promotion-path data carries real risk if wrong; the skip list is preserved in session scratch for a possible follow-up round. `requirements_ar` is the target level's "المؤهل" (qualification) text from the same sheet, present on 102 of 104 rows. Verified in a rolled-back transaction first (104 rows, spot-checked ordering/grades), then applied for real and confirmed live in-browser with a temporary `hr_admin` test user (created and deleted via the Admin API): `/career-path` now correctly lists real promotion pairs with grade levels and requirements instead of its former empty state. `npx tsc --noEmit` and the full `vitest run` suite (89 tests) passed clean — no schema or application-code change needed, since `career_path`'s table/RLS already existed from migration 13.

- **`career_path` extended pass — reviewed the 84 skipped pairs for manual resolution, per the project owner's own follow-up request** (2026-07-20, migration `20260720000003_career_path_data_extended.sql`). Found two real, narrow bugs in the matching script itself, not new guesses: (1) a parser bug where a cell's embedded newline before a bare parenthetical (e.g. "أ.أول.تغذية\n(تسجيل الهيئة)") split the parenthetical into its own fake title instead of treating it as a continuation of the preceding one; (2) a confirmed wording difference between this older sheet and the newer "Positios" sheet `job_titles` was built from — "(تسجيل الهيئة)" vs "(ترخيص الهيئة)" for the same health-sciences licensing note, verified by reading both sources directly, not assumed. Also generalized the matcher to handle single-role fan-out/fan-in (e.g. one grade-10 "قائد فريق خدمة العملاء" branching to two distinct grade-11 targets; four distinct grade-9 call-center roles converging to that same grade-10 title) instead of rejecting these as a count mismatch — each resulting pair is still independently verified against `job_titles`, so this isn't a loosened check, just a better-shaped one. Net result: 15 additional confident pairs (104 → 119 total; the migration resends all 119 with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so the original 104 are a no-op).

- **The remaining ~97 candidates were deliberately NOT resolved and are not silently guessed at**: investigation showed the overwhelming majority (61 distinct title strings — mostly the "استشاري" tier at grade 11 across nearly every specialty track, and several specific "رئيس قسم X" titles at grade 12) reference job titles that simply do not exist anywhere in the already-imported 272-row `job_titles` table — a real, structural gap in the earlier admin extraction (built from the newer "Positios" sheet, which apparently consolidated or omitted this tier for most tracks), not a text-matching problem this migration can fix. Resolving that would mean inserting roughly 40+ new `job_titles` rows (with their own `salary_scale` entries) — a separate, materially larger decision, flagged back to the project owner rather than made unilaterally inside a "review the skip list" task. A handful of remaining skips are genuine many-to-many ambiguities (e.g. the technical/data track's grade 8→9 transition: 7 source titles vs 9 target titles with no reliable way to infer which maps to which) correctly left unresolved. Verified via the same dry-run-then-apply discipline: rolled-back transaction confirmed exactly 15 new inserts against the pre-existing 104, then applied for real (`career_path` now at 119 rows). `npx tsc --noEmit` and the full `vitest run` suite (89 tests) passed clean; no application-code or schema change was needed.

- **Added the ~40 job titles found genuinely missing while reviewing the skip list, per the project owner's explicit follow-up request** (2026-07-20, migrations `20260720000004_missing_job_titles.sql` + `20260720000005_career_path_data_after_missing_titles.sql`). Before inserting anything, re-walked the entire "Career Path" sheet (not just the skip-list entries) checking each title by **name existence anywhere in `job_titles`**, not name+grade — the earlier name+grade check was itself producing false positives, since several titles genuinely exist already but at a grade the old sheet's row position doesn't predict (confirmed real drift: the secretarial ladder, the engineering ladder now split into its own `الهندسية` family, and a few leadership titles are all 1 grade off between this old sheet and the newer source already used for `job_titles`). That full walk found 92 true "name doesn't exist anywhere" gaps, still not accepted at face value: 59 were added after manual review; the rest were explicitly excluded and documented in the migration header rather than guessed at, because each is a materially different, bigger decision — the entire "التشغيل الطبي" (Medical Operations) track (no such `job_family` exists at all), the "وظائف الاتصال المؤسسي" grade-11 cell whose split is genuinely ambiguous plus its wholly-absent "مصور"/"كتابة محتوى" sub-tracks, a near-duplicate ("استشاري مصادر التعلم" vs. the already-existing "استشاري مصادر تعلم", differing only by the definite article), and several facilities/trades titles the original admin import had already deliberately consolidated into combined rows (e.g. "مشرف خدمات - مشرف أمن - كبير سائقين") that re-adding separately would fragment. The 59 added rows are mostly the grade-11 "استشاري" tier for ~15 tracks that already had a clean grade 8-10 ladder, plus a few entirely-missing 8-11 sub-track ladders within families that already had their grade-13 leadership titles (business development, and a "أداء"/performance sub-track under Strategy), plus 3 standalone leadership titles.

- **Salary lookup was direct, not a new source dig**: this schema's `salary_scale` is per-grade-level, not per-title (already established fact from the original import — every admin/leadership title at a given grade shares identical step figures), so each new title's salary rows were copied from the already-verified per-grade figures already sitting in the database, generated via a single SQL `INSERT ... RETURNING` CTE joined against a grade→salary VALUES list, not re-parsed from Excel. Verified in a rolled-back transaction first (all 59 rows validated for family existence, no name collision, and salary-data availability *before* any SQL was even generated, catching problems at the JS validation layer rather than at the database), then applied for real (`job_titles`/`salary_scale` now at 331 rows each). Re-ran the `career_path` matcher against the now-larger `job_titles` table and it picked up 27 additional pairs it couldn't see before (119 → 146 total, again via `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` so the prior 119 are a no-op) — e.g. the call-center ladder's fan-out to "مدير تجربة العملاء" (previously missing target), the newly-added grade-11 "استشاري" tier connecting to its grade-12 targets, and four allied-health grade-8→9 pairs. Verified live in-browser with a temporary `hr_admin` test user (created and deleted via the Admin API): `/salary-scale` still renders correctly, `/career-path` shows the new pairs with correct grades and requirements. `npx tsc --noEmit` and the full `vitest run` suite (89 tests) passed clean.

- **Created the "التشغيل الطبي" (Medical Operations) `job_family`, the last documented gap from the missing-titles review** (2026-07-20, migrations `20260720000006_medical_operations_family.sql` + `20260720000007_career_path_after_medical_ops.sql`), per the project owner's explicit follow-up request. Added its 5-tier ladder (grades 8/9/10/11 admin + grade 13 leadership) exactly as it appears in the source sheet's own column, with salary rows copied from the existing per-grade figures, same validated-before-any-SQL approach as the earlier missing-titles migration (25 → 26 `job_families`, 331 → 336 `job_titles`/`salary_scale`). Grade-12 "رئيس قسم" for this track was deliberately left out again — same reasoning as every other track: the sheet gives only the bare generic label, not a specific name, and it doesn't exact-match the existing generic "رئيس قسم/رئيس مكتب" title's wording.

- **Found and fixed a real bug in the `career_path` matcher itself while re-running it against the new titles, not a data problem**: several sheet columns (Medical Operations chief among them, but also the Strategy family's "أداء"/performance sub-track and the Innovation family's "تطوير أعمال"/"تحليل أعمال" sub-tracks) spell the tier prefix with spaces — "أ. أول تشغيل طبي" / "أ. م. تشغيل طبي" — instead of the already-established "أ.أول.X"/"أ.م.X" convention every inserted `job_titles` row actually uses; the matcher's `applyKnownWordingFix` only handled the health-sciences "(تسجيل الهيئة)" wording difference, not this spacing pattern, silently leaving 9 real pairs unmatched. Fixed by porting the same spacing-normalization already used in the missing-titles finder script into the `career_path` matcher too. Verified the fix doesn't touch already-correctly-formatted names (e.g. "أ.م.استراتيجية" is untouched, confirmed by regex walkthrough before running). Re-running the matcher surfaced exactly 9 new pairs — the 3 Medical Operations transitions, 2 in the Institutional Excellence sub-track, 1 in "أداء", 2 in "تطوير أعمال"/"تحليل أعمال", and 1 connecting "مطور تطبيقات" to the previously-orphaned "أ.أول.تخطيط موارد المنشأة" (ERP planning) row — `career_path` now at 155 rows (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` again made the prior 146 a no-op). Verified in rolled-back transactions before both real applies, then live in-browser with a temporary `hr_admin` test user (created and deleted via the Admin API): `/career-path` shows the full Medical Operations ladder with correct grades/requirements, `/salary-scale` includes the new titles correctly. `npx tsc --noEmit` and the full `vitest run` suite (89 tests) passed clean.

- **Fixed three real issues in the add-employee form, reported live by the project owner testing as the real super_admin** (2026-07-21, migration `20260721000001_super_admin_employee_data_and_pending_roles.sql`): (1) a garbled hire-date placeholder — the native `<input type="date">` inherited `dir="rtl"` from the page, which some browsers render as reversed day/month/year segments; fixed with an explicit `dir="ltr"` on that one field, same approach already used for the English name input. (2) `super_admin` couldn't add employees at all — confirmed this was the deliberately-designed, already-reviewed-and-signed-off (2026-07-19) matrix, not a bug: only `hr_admin` held `employeeData=approve`, `super_admin` held only `view`. The project owner explicitly asked to widen it specifically because no `hr_admin` user exists yet in production and `super_admin` needs to create the first one — raised to `approve`, matching `hr_admin` exactly (a real policy change, confirmed with the project owner, not assumed). (3) the form had no way to assign a system role at all — a genuine feature gap, closed by adding a role `<select>` and a scope picker (all org units, or several specific ones via checkboxes — the project owner explicitly asked for multi-org-unit support, not a single choice).

- **The real architectural problem this surfaced**: role assignment can't go directly into `user_roles` at invite time, because `user_roles.user_id` references `auth.users(id)`, but an invited employee has no `auth.users` row yet — `profiles.auth_user_id` is only backfilled later by `link_profile_to_auth_user()` (20260716000009) once the invite is accepted. Fixed with a new holding table, `pending_role_assignments` (keyed by `profile_id`, which exists immediately, with the same `scope_type`/`org_unit_id` shape and partial-unique-index pattern as `user_roles` itself — a role scoped to several org units is just several pending rows), and extended `link_profile_to_auth_user()` to promote any pending rows into real `user_roles` rows the moment the auth user links up, then delete them (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on the promotion INSERT as a safety net). Verified the exact mechanism in a rolled-back transaction first (a real `DO` block inserting two org-unit-scoped pending rows, then a raw `auth.users` INSERT to simulate invite acceptance, confirming both rows promoted to `user_roles` and the pending table cleared) before applying for real. Then verified end-to-end through the actual UI, not just simulated: a real temporary `hr_admin` test user submitted the real form with `hr_admin` role scoped to two specific org units — confirmed live that `profiles.auth_user_id` linked and exactly 2 `user_roles` rows were created with the correct role and both org units, immediately (not eventually), since Supabase's `inviteUserByEmail` creates the real `auth.users` row right away rather than waiting for the invite email to be clicked. All test data (test user, invited profile, auth user, user_roles rows) deleted afterward — confirmed zero rows remaining in every touched table. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (89 tests) all passed clean.

- **Fixed invite-acceptance links landing on an error instead of a set-password form** (2026-07-22): the project owner reported clicking "accept invite" in a real email led to `/login#error=access_denied&error_code=otp_expired` instead of a password-entry page. Root-caused methodically rather than guessing: generated a fresh invite link via the Admin API and curl-tested it immediately — it verified correctly at Supabase and produced a valid `access_token`/`refresh_token` hash (`type=invite`), proving the token-generation side is not broken. The real bug was downstream: `RecoveryHashRedirect` only recognized `type=recovery` in the hash, and `ResetPasswordForm`'s guard explicitly rejected anything but `type=recovery` — despite its own doc comment already claiming to handle invite links (a real discrepancy between the comment and the code, not previously caught). An invite link that verifies successfully was landing on the bare domain with a valid, unconsumed session that nothing in the app ever read, silently stranding the invited user with no way to set an initial password. Since `setSession()` doesn't care which OAuth flow produced the tokens, accepting `type=invite` alongside `type=recovery` in both places was the entire fix — no new page needed. Verified end-to-end with a real freshly-generated invite token pair replayed against the local app (extracted from the real `Location` header after curl-verifying the link, since invite links always redirect to the production domain regardless of where they're generated): `RecoveryHashRedirect` correctly forwarded to `/reset-password`, the form rendered (not "invalid link"), and submitting a new password produced a real, persisted logged-in session (confirmed by the nav shell rendering with a working "تسجيل الخروج" button, not a bounce back to `/login`). The specific link the project owner reported as expired was a separate, already-used token (most likely consumed by real time elapsed or an email security scanner prefetching it) — unrelated to this code bug, and expected to work now that the destination page can actually complete a valid invite link. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (89 tests) all passed clean.

- **NavBar now filters tabs by the caller's actual permission — general fix, not employee-specific** (2026-07-22, migration `20260722000001_get_my_permissions_function.sql`), per the project owner's explicit request after reporting that `NavBar` renders all 14 tabs unconditionally for every logged-in user — confirmed directly in the code (zero filtering existed). The project owner specifically asked for a general solution applying to every role, not a one-off "employee" list. Added `get_my_permissions()` (same SECURITY DEFINER pattern as `get_my_role_codes()` — reading `role_permissions`/`roles` directly requires a `userManagement` grant most roles, including plain `employee`, don't hold) returning the caller's own highest `vpra_level` per `process_area`; `MAX()` works directly on the `vpra_level` enum since Postgres orders enums by declaration order (none<view<prepare<recommend<approve), confirmed via `\dT+` before relying on it.

- **Each tab's minimum level is a deliberate product choice, not a mechanical ">none" derivation**: direct inspection confirmed `employee` genuinely holds `view` on `goalsLibrary`/`careerPath`/`vacancies`, so a naive ">none" filter would have left "بنك الأهداف" (Goal Library) and "سلم الرواتب" (Salary Scale) visible — exactly the tabs the project owner named as examples to hide. Fix: each item in `navItems.ts` declares an explicit `(processArea, minLevel)` — Goal Library requires `goalsLibrary=prepare` (shows only for `strategy_admin`, the actual sole approver, hidden even from `hr_admin`/`employee` who share the identical `view` level); BAU Tasks requires `bauTasks=prepare` (shows for `manager`/`supervisor`/`field_supervisor`/`employee`, the roles that actually do BAU-task work, hidden from `hr_admin`/`ceo`/etc. who hold `view` only); Salary Scale checks `employeeData` specifically rather than its page's real `careerPath OR employeeData` OR-gate, since full company salary figures are more sensitive than a promotion-path reference. `vacancies` and `career-path` were deliberately left at `view` — vacancies are already documented as visible to all staff, and career-path is reasonable for anyone to browse for their own progression. Verified in a rolled-back transaction first (simulated a real `employee` role via the RPC, confirmed `anon` explicitly denied), then live in-browser with two real temporary test accounts: an `employee`-role account showed exactly 7 tabs (Home, Career Path, BAU Tasks, Evaluations, Feedback 360, Competencies, Vacancies) and correctly hid every tab named by the project owner (Employees, Salary Scale, Goal Library) plus Calibration/Promotions/Rewards/Admin; an `hr_admin`-role account showed 12 tabs (everything except Goal Library and BAU Tasks, exactly matching its real `view`-only grants on those two areas).

- **Chased down a verification false alarm, not a real bug**: the Admin tab appeared missing from the accessibility-tree read for the `hr_admin` test account; temporary debug logging proved both the permissions data and the filtered items list were fully correct — the link was genuinely present in the DOM (confirmed via a direct JS query over `nav.sru-navbar` anchors), just scrolled out of the accessibility snapshot by the navbar's own horizontal overflow, not a code defect. Debug logging removed once confirmed. All temporary test data deleted afterward. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (93 tests — 4 new, covering `visibleNavItems` including the real `employee` permission set verbatim) all passed clean.

- **Not yet resolved this round, pending the project owner's answers**: a "My Profile" self-service screen (no avatar column or Storage bucket exists yet), what exactly "goals cascaded from strategy" / "competencies cascaded from manager" should look like as dedicated employee-facing pages, and whether `employee`'s `calibration` grant (currently `none`, a decision already reviewed and signed off 2026-07-19) should actually change.

- **First self-service employee screen: "الملف الشخصي" (My Profile)** (2026-07-22), closing the profile/goals/career-path questions from the previous entry. The project owner's answers: profile stays read-only for now (no avatar column or Storage bucket exists, and no editable field was named); goals and competencies should show only what's cascaded to the employee specifically; career path should be scoped to the employee's own job title, not the full company matrix. Built as a single page (`/profile`) with three sections rather than three new nav tabs — deliberately avoids re-adding the nav clutter the previous entry's fix just removed.

- **Goals section**: `goals` WHERE `employee_id` = caller's own profile id, explicit filter matching the established `/evaluations/mine` discipline (not left to `goals_select`'s broader RLS branches, which would otherwise leak org-unit-scoped or direct-report access into what's supposed to be a "mine only" view).

- **Career path section**: `career_path` WHERE `from_job_title_id` OR `to_job_title_id` = caller's own `job_title_id` — same explicit-filter discipline, even though `career_path_select`'s RLS (`careerPath` view) would already let the caller read the whole table; empty state shown if the profile has no `job_title_id` set.

- **Competencies were deliberately NOT built as a fourth section**: direct inspection of the real `competencies` table found `job_family_id` populated on 0 of 27 rows — the schema supports per-job-family competency scoping, but no real data uses it yet, so a "competencies cascaded from your job family" view would be functionally identical to the existing full-framework `/competencies` page today; flagged to the project owner as a data gap rather than building a no-op filter. Access point: `TopBar`'s username display is now a real `<Link>` to `/profile` (previously plain text bundled into the same button as sign-out); sign-out is now a separate icon-only button so the two actions don't share one click target. Verified live end-to-end with a real temporary `employee` test account (job title deliberately set to one with a known real `career_path` entry, plus one real temporary goal assigned): all three sections rendered the correct real data (own info, the one assigned goal, and exactly the one career-path row touching that job title); the TopBar profile link and the separated logout button both worked correctly. All test data (auth user, profile, evaluation cycle, goal) deleted afterward — confirmed zero rows remaining. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (93 tests) all passed clean.

- **Expanded to 5 sections, and the calibration question closed** (2026-07-22): the project owner explicitly declined raising `employee`'s `calibration` grant after an explanation of what the module does (an org-unit-wide rating-consistency review HR/committee runs after evaluations, not an individual-facing action) — it stays `none`, no change. Also asked for two more sections: "مهامي التشغيلية" (My Routine Tasks) and "جداراتي" (My Competencies), making 5 total. Routine Tasks mirrors the Goals section's discipline exactly — `bau_tasks` filtered to `employee_id` = caller's own profile id, not left to `bau_tasks_select`'s broader OR-branches (org-unit approve, direct supervisor) which would otherwise leak into a "mine only" view. Competencies reuses the same static data source as the existing `/competencies` page (`src/lib/data/competencies.ts`) rather than querying the DB table directly, and is **not actually personalized** — flagged explicitly in the UI copy and in a code comment, since the real `competencies` table has `job_family_id` populated on 0 of 27 rows, so there is no real per-job-family data to cascade from; showing the full institutional framework is the honest option, not a fabricated filter. Verified live end-to-end with a second real temporary `employee` account (same job title with a known `career_path` row, plus a real goal and a real BAU task assigned): all 5 sections rendered correctly. Hit one unrelated transient error while setting up test data (`auth.admin.createUser` failed once with a JWT `kid`-mismatch error, code `bad_jwt`) — confirmed not a real environment problem by immediately retrying the identical call, which succeeded, and by confirming `auth.admin.listUsers` worked fine throughout; not investigated further since it didn't reproduce. All test data deleted afterward — confirmed zero rows remaining. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (89 tests — this branch doesn't include the separately-tracked NavBar-filtering test additions) all passed clean.

- **Avatar dropdown menu in TopBar, extended /profile with supervisor lookup + an interim performance-level dashboard** (2026-07-22): the project owner reported the employee experience was missing a discoverable entry point (TopBar showed no name at all for the account they tested with, and asked for a user-photo icon whose click opens a menu with My data/My competencies/My KPIs/My routine tasks/My performance level). Root-caused the missing-name/empty-nav report first rather than assuming a code bug: the account in question (m@leafyland.co) was a bare auth.users identity created earlier this session purely to test the invite-link fix, with no profiles/user_roles row behind it at all — confirmed via a live read-only query (2 real profiles in production, this account matching neither), then fixed at the project owner's explicit instruction by inserting a real profiles row (employee_number 90001, job title chosen deliberately to have a real career_path entry) plus an employee-role user_roles row, not by changing any code. Built `UserMenu.tsx` (new client component): a circular avatar button — a generic Lucide icon placeholder, per the project owner's explicit "start with a generic icon now, add photo upload later" decision, since no photo column or Storage bucket exists yet — opening a dropdown (name header + 5 links to `/profile#<anchor>`, closes on outside click/Escape), replacing the old plain username-pill Link in `TopBar.tsx`.
