Extended the existing single-page /profile (5 sections from the previous PR) rather than adding new routes, matching that same established "one page, not new nav tabs" decision: added anchor ids matching the 5 dropdown items, relabeled the Goals section as `#my-kpis`/"مؤشرات الأداء" with an explicit note that KPIs are currently built from assigned goals (the project owner's new wording didn't separately list Goals, so this is a flagged assumption, not a confirmed rename), and added two new fields to My Data that had no schema backing yet: qualification and certificates render as explicit "قريبًا/coming soon" placeholders rather than inventing free-text/multi-value columns without confirming their shape — still an open question. The 5th field, supervisor name, DOES have schema backing (`profiles.supervisor_id`) but hit a real RLS gap discovered live: a plain `employee` holds zero rows in `role_permissions` for `employeeData` (confirmed by direct query), so `profiles_select`'s RLS has no branch letting them read an arbitrary colleague's row, including their own supervisor's — a direct query for the supervisor's name returned null even with supervisor_id genuinely set. Fixed with `get_my_supervisor()` (migration `20260722000002`), a narrow SECURITY DEFINER self-lookup mirroring `get_my_role_codes()`/`get_my_permissions()`'s established pattern, `EXECUTE` revoked from `anon`/`PUBLIC` and confirmed via `has_function_privilege()` after applying. New "My Performance Level" section is explicitly flagged as an early/interim version (the project owner hadn't specified its exact shape) rather than over-committing to an assumption: it combines two tables that already grant a self-row SELECT bypass — `evaluations`'+`evaluation_scores`' average score per evaluation, and `calibration_results`' calibrated rating where one exists — instead of inventing new schema. Verified live end-to-end with a real temporary employee test account (real auth user + profile + supervisor_id pointing at the real hr_admin profile + a goal + a BAU task + an evaluation with two evaluation_scores rows averaging to exactly 80% + a calibration_results row): the dropdown showed the correct name and all 5 correctly-hrefed links; My Data showed every field including the qualification/certificates placeholders; the supervisor name came back blank BEFORE the get_my_supervisor() fix (proving the RLS gap was real, not assumed) and correctly showed "عزام محمد المحيميد" after it; My KPIs/My Routine Tasks/My Competencies/My Career Path rendered unchanged from the prior PR; My Performance Level showed the correct 80.0% average and the calibration row's 80%/75% original/calibrated split. All temporary test data (auth user, profile, user_roles, goal, task, evaluation, evaluation_scores, calibration_session, calibration_results, the evaluation_cycles row created for the test) deleted afterward — confirmed zero rows remaining in every touched table via a direct re-query. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (93 tests, unchanged — no new test file for this UI-only + one-RPC change) all passed clean.

- **Reworked /profile from a long anchor-scrolled page into top tabs, and investigated a production "Internal Server Error" report that turned out not to reproduce** (2026-07-22): the project owner reported the profile page needed each section split into its own page, top tabs, or a sidebar. Chose top tabs over new routes/nav items — same reasoning as the original single-page decision (avoid re-adding nav clutter), while genuinely fixing the scannability complaint. Built `ProfileTabs.tsx` (new client component): the Server Component page still does all its data fetching exactly as before, then passes each section's already-rendered JSX as `{id, label, content}` tab entries — `ProfileTabs` itself only handles which one is visible, not the data. The dropdown's existing `/profile#<anchor>` links still work: the initial active tab is read from `window.location.hash` via `useState`'s lazy initializer (guarded for `typeof window === "undefined"` during SSR) rather than an effect + `setState`, which the project's ESLint config (`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`) correctly flagged as an avoidable extra render — verified live that a direct `/profile#my-performance` load shows that panel immediately with no visible flash to the default tab first, confirming the lazy-initializer approach reconciles correctly during hydration rather than just working by accident. Separately investigated a real "Internal Server Error" screenshot the project owner reported at the bare `/ar` home page, logged in as the employee test account — root-caused methodically rather than assumed fixed: generated a real magic-link session for that exact account (`m@leafyland.co`) via the Admin API to test production directly, found the implicit-grant hash gets dropped before `@supabase/ssr`'s client-side hash detection runs (same class of issue as the earlier PKCE/recovery-link bugs, but magic links aren't a flow this app's `RecoveryHashRedirect` handles at all), so switched to temporarily resetting that account's real password via the Admin API (`auth.admin.updateUserById`) to log in through the normal form instead. Neither the home page nor `/profile` reproduced any error on production with this exact account afterward — both rendered correctly, including all 6 sections (now tabs) with real data. Reported this transparently rather than claiming a silent fix: the error is currently unreproducible with the same account, and the most likely explanation is a transient failure during the PM2/build restart itself when PR #59 went live, not a standing code defect — flagged as something to watch for, not confirmed root-caused. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint` (after fixing the effect lint error above), and the full `vitest run` suite (93 tests, unchanged — this is a pure presentation refactor of already-tested data-fetching code) all passed clean.

- **Organizational Structure builder: levels -> positions -> staffing, a new hr_admin capability built deliberately independent of `org_units`/`job_titles`** (2026-07-22/23, migrations `20260722000003` + `20260722000004`). The project owner initially asked for this reachable from `/admin`; clarifying questions surfaced that `org_units` (58-row static college/department tree, no explicit level column) and `job_titles` (career-ladder catalog, grade_level 1-14, zero org_unit linkage) were both candidates to reuse -- the project owner explicitly rejected reusing either ("لا أريدك أن تعتمد على الجداول السابقة") and clarified the permission belongs to `hr_admin` specifically, and that this is unrelated to career grades. Added `orgStructure` as a 13th `process_area` enum value (its own migration/transaction, since Postgres forbids using a value added via `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE` in the same transaction that added it) plus `src/lib/vpra.ts`'s `ProcessArea` union/array and its test (12 -> 13). Three new tables, all independent of `org_units`/`job_titles`: `org_structure_levels` (flat, sequentially-ordered tiers -- `level_order` is a display sequence, not a career grade; **[استنتاج]**, not confirmed: interpreted "إدراج المستويات أولا بأول" as a flat list, not a branching tree with multiple parallel nodes per level -- flagged for the project owner to correct if wrong), `org_structure_positions` (named positions belonging to exactly one level), `org_structure_assignments` (staffs a profile onto a position, partial-unique on (position_id, employee_id) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL, same NULL-safe pattern used everywhere else in this schema -- no fixed headcount cap, since none was requested). RLS uses `check_vpra_global('orgStructure', level)` (the established tool for university-wide catalog tables with no per-row org unit, from the 20260719000011 fix) rather than `check_vpra()`, since none of these three tables have an `org_unit_id` at all. Seeded `role_permissions`: `hr_admin='approve'` (the explicit ask), `super_admin='view'` (consistent with the established pattern of super_admin seeing but not automatically holding blanket approve elsewhere in this schema). Built `/admin`'s first-ever "actions" section (previously pure read-only dashboard) with a link to `/admin/org-structure`, gated behind a `get_my_permissions()` check for `orgStructure>=view` -- display-only, since the real gate is each write action's own RLS. Two new pages: `/admin/org-structure` (add levels sequentially, add positions per level, list grouped by level) and `/admin/org-structure/staffing` (assign/unassign employees to positions, plus its own add-position form and inline position-name editing, per the project owner's explicit request that positions be editable/addable from the staffing screen too).

- **Found and fixed a real bug live during verification, not assumed**: both `AddOrgStructurePositionForm.tsx`'s level-select and `AssignEmployeeForm.tsx`'s position-select seeded their `useState` directly from `levels[0]?.id`/`positions[0]?.id`, which is correct only on first mount when those arrays start empty -- confirmed live via the server log (`addPosition("", "مدير عام", ...)`, an empty levelId) that after adding the first level, `router.refresh()` re-rendered the SAME component instance with a populated `levels` prop but the stale `""` never updated, silently failing every subsequent position insert. Fixed by deriving the effective selected value fresh on every render (`selectedLevelId ?? levels[0]?.id ?? ""`) instead of seeding useState from the prop -- avoids both the stale-value bug and an `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` ESLint violation an initial effect-based fix would have hit (same class of issue already resolved for `ProfileTabs.tsx` in the previous PR). Verified live end-to-end with a real temporary `hr_admin` test account: added a level, added a position under it (confirming the fix -- the first attempt before the fix silently failed exactly as predicted), assigned a real employee to the position, edited the position's name, unassigned the employee, and confirmed via a rolled-back SQL simulation that a plain `employee` role (using the real persistent `m@leafyland.co` test account) sees zero rows on both `org_structure_levels`/`org_structure_positions` under the same RLS that let `hr_admin` see and write them -- proving the permission boundary is real, not just structurally present. All temporary test data (auth user, profile, user_roles, the level/position/assignment created during verification) deleted afterward -- confirmed zero rows remaining in every touched table. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (93 tests, one updated for the 13th process area) all passed clean.

- **Turned org_structure_positions into a real tree, superseding the flat-levels reading from the previous entry** (2026-07-23, migration `20260723000001`). The project owner reviewed the first version live on production (still pre-merge, PR #61) and corrected the interpretation explicitly: "بل شجرة حقيقية كل مستوى له عدة عقد وكل عقد له أب من المستوى السابق" (a real tree -- each level has several nodes, each with a parent from the previous level), confirming the `[استنتاج]` flag from the prior entry was wrong. Added a self-referencing `parent_id` to `org_structure_positions`: NULL only for positions at the structure's first (root) level (the minimum `level_order` in `org_structure_levels`), NOT NULL everywhere else, with the parent's own level required to be exactly one `level_order` less than the child's -- "من المستوى السابق" (from the immediately preceding level specifically, not any ancestor further up). Enforced by a `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` trigger (`validate_org_structure_position_parent()`), not a CHECK constraint, since validating this needs two joins to `org_structure_levels` (this position's level and its parent's level) that a plain CHECK can't express -- same reasoning this project already used for `check_vpra()`'s SQL-function approach over inline policy logic. `addPosition()` (`src/app/[locale]/(app)/admin/org-structure/actions.ts`) gained an optional `parentId` parameter and a new `mapError()` branch recognizing the trigger's plain-text exceptions (no custom SQLSTATE) and mapping them to `invalid_input` rather than an opaque `unknown`. `AddOrgStructurePositionForm.tsx` now computes whether the selected level is the root tier and, if not, offers a parent select scoped to positions at exactly the preceding level (empty-state messaging and a disabled submit button when that level has no positions yet, rather than letting the trigger reject with a confusing raw error). Both the builder page (`/admin/org-structure`) and the staffing page (`/admin/org-structure/staffing`) now display each position's parent ("يتبع: <name>" or "جذر"/root for top-level positions). Verified the tree invariant directly in a rolled-back SQL transaction with all six paths before touching any UI: root-with-no-parent (accepted), root-with-a-parent (rejected), correct immediate-parent at the next level (accepted), non-root-with-no-parent (rejected), parent from two levels up -- skipping a level -- (rejected), and a full 3-level valid chain (accepted) -- all six behaved exactly as intended. Then verified live end-to-end with a real temporary `hr_admin` test account through the actual UI: created a 2-level structure (root position "مدير عام", child "مدير الموارد البشرية" reporting to it), confirmed the parent selector correctly appeared only for the non-root level and was pre-populated with the one valid parent option, and confirmed both the builder and staffing pages rendered the "يتبع"/root relationship correctly. All temporary test data (auth user, profile, user_roles, the level/position rows created during verification) deleted afterward -- confirmed zero rows remaining in every touched table, deleting children before parents to satisfy the new self-referencing RESTRICT FK. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (93 tests, unchanged -- no application-level TypeScript logic needed new test coverage, the invariant lives entirely in the DB trigger) all passed clean.

- **Edit and delete for both levels and positions, on both the builder and staffing screens** (2026-07-23): the project owner reviewed the merged tree structure live on production and asked to "أضف إمكانية التعديل وإمكانية الحذف من خلال الجدول في الأسفل وكذلك في أسفل الكروت" (add edit and delete via the table at the bottom and also at the bottom of the cards) — the staffing screen's positions table already had inline name-editing but no delete; the builder screen's level cards had no edit or delete for either levels or the position chips shown under them at all. Added `updateLevel()`/`deleteLevel()` and `deletePosition()` to `org-structure/actions.ts` (`updatePosition()` already existed) — no new migration needed, since the existing `org_structure_levels_update`/`org_structure_positions_update` RLS policies (`check_vpra_global('orgStructure','approve')`, already in migration `20260722000004`) already permit the UPDATE these soft-deletes use.

- **Both deletes are blocked by an explicit dependency check in the action itself, not left to the RESTRICT FKs**: `deleteLevel()` refuses if the level still has any active position (`org_structure_positions` row with that `level_id` and `deleted_at IS NULL`) — a plain RESTRICT FK only guards a hard DELETE, and this is a soft-delete UPDATE that would otherwise happily orphan positions under a now-hidden level; `deletePosition()` refuses if the position has active employee assignments OR active child positions (other positions whose `parent_id` points at it) — deleting a middle tree node would either strand assignments on a hidden position or break `validate_org_structure_position_parent()`'s invariant for its children. Both surface a distinct `has_dependents` error state (added to `OrgStructureActionState`) with a specific message, not a generic "something went wrong." Built `OrgStructureLevelCard.tsx` (client component wrapping each level's card: editable name_ar/name_en + Save/Delete, replacing the previous plain read-only `<h3>`) and `OrgStructurePositionMiniRow.tsx` (a compact editable+deletable row for the position chips under each level card, showing its parent/root label) for the builder page; extended the existing `OrgStructurePositionRow.tsx` on the staffing page with a Delete button alongside the pre-existing Save. Added a `window.confirm()` before every delete (this codebase's first use of a native confirm dialog — no existing precedent either way, added as a reasonable safety step for an otherwise-instant destructive-feeling action).

- **A real tooling limitation surfaced during verification, not a product bug**: this session's browser-automation tool cannot interact with native `window.confirm()` dialogs (no CDP dialog-handling available here), so clicking a delete button appeared to hang; worked around by issuing a follow-up action (a keypress or a wait) which let the already-dispatched click/dialog resolve, confirmed via the dev server's own request log that the underlying Server Action call did go through correctly each time — a testing-environment quirk, not something a real user hitting a real native confirm dialog in an actual browser would ever encounter. Verified live end-to-end with a real temporary `hr_admin` test account, carefully avoiding the real production level/position/assignment already visible on-screen ("الرئيس التنفيذي - رئيس الجامعة" / "رئيس الجامعة", with a real assignment to profile 4214): created a separate temporary level+position, confirmed `deleteLevel` was correctly blocked with the has_dependents message while the position still existed, edited the position's name (persisted correctly), deleted the position, then successfully deleted the now-empty level, and confirmed the staffing page's new Delete button renders correctly next to Save. A direct service-role query after cleanup confirmed the temporary level/position are soft-deleted (`deleted_at` set) while every real production row (the one real level, position, and assignment) remains completely untouched. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (93 tests, unchanged) all passed clean.

- **Excel import for employees + full organizational structure (levels, positions, staffing), plus a much richer employee schema** (2026-07-24, migrations `20260724000001` + `20260724000002`). The project owner provided a real workbook ("الهيكل التنظيمي.xlsx", two sheets: "Employees Data" — 279 real employees — and "الهيكل التنظيمي" — 61 real positions with staffing embedded via employee number) and asked for (1) the add-employee form extended to cover every field in the employee sheet, and (2) an import mechanism for both sheets together.

- **Reading the real data overturned the previous entry's design in a load-bearing way**: "المستوى" is confirmed (directly, by the project owner) to be a job-rank classification, not tree depth — the real sheet has positions at level 6 reporting directly to a level-4 parent, and several level-5 positions reporting directly to the level-1 root, skipping multiple levels. Migration `20260724000001` replaces `validate_org_structure_position_parent()` (from `20260723000001`) to drop the "parent must be exactly one level up" rule entirely, keeping only self-parent and cycle prevention (verified live: multi-level-skip accepted, self-parent and cycles still rejected). Migration `20260724000002` adds nine new plain-TEXT `profiles` columns (qualification, education_speciality, date_of_birth, mobile, marital_status, gender, nationality, employee_category, insurance_category — no enum, matching the established "no confirmed fixed vocabulary" precedent already used for `goals.status`/`promotions.status`; `qualification` closes a gap the My Profile page has flagged as "coming soon" since 2026-07-22) and `org_structure_positions.external_code` (nullable, unique when present) — the source sheet's own position code, needed to make re-importing the same file idempotent (update the same position, not duplicate it) even after the project owner renames it via the UI. `job_titles`/`job_families` are populated FROM this import (the project owner's explicit choice over a bare grade-number field): a new position/grade pair finds-or-creates a `job_titles` row keyed on the table's own `(job_family_id, name_ar)` unique constraint, reusing the existing "الأكاديمي"/"عام" placeholder families by the sheet's own "Category" column (Academic/Administrative) — the same reuse-don't-invent precedent from the 2026-07-20 job_titles data import. Org-unit matching for each employee's department is exact-match-on-trimmed-name only, per the project owner's own "حاول المطابقة ونصلحها فيما بعد" (try matching, fix the rest manually later) — unmatched departments are left NULL and surfaced by name in the import report, not guessed at.

- **Found and fixed the exact same upsert-on-a-partial-unique-index bug this project already hit twice** (`evaluation_scores`, `calibration_results`): `org_structure_positions.external_code`'s uniqueness is a partial index (`WHERE external_code IS NOT NULL AND deleted_at IS NULL`), so `.upsert({onConflict:'external_code'})` fails live with "no unique or exclusion constraint matching the ON CONFLICT specification" — caught during verification, not assumed working, and fixed with the same established select-then-insert-or-update pattern (profiles' `employee_number` upsert is unaffected since that constraint is a plain, non-partial UNIQUE). `src/app/[locale]/(app)/admin/org-structure/import-actions.ts` (`importOrgStructureExcel`) processes both sheets in one action — employees first (since positions reference them by number), then levels (new `level_order` values only, auto-named "المستوى N"), then positions (two-pass: create/update by `external_code` first, link `parent_id` second once every code resolves), then assignments (add-only — the sheet has no way to express "unassign," so a re-import never silently removes an existing assignment, only adds missing ones). One real data-quality issue in the source sheet was confirmed directly with the project owner rather than guessed: position code 113102's parent code (1132) doesn't exist anywhere in the sheet — a typo for 1131 ("مدير إدارة الحياة الجامعية") — hard-coded as a one-off, transparently-reported correction rather than silently dropped or blocking the whole import. Chose `exceljs` over the more commonly-suggested `xlsx`(SheetJS) npm package after checking `npm audit`: the npm-registry `xlsx` build carries two unpatched high-severity advisories (prototype pollution, ReDoS) directly exploitable via a malicious uploaded file — exactly this feature's attack surface — while `exceljs` only pulls in one moderate `uuid` advisory requiring a call pattern this code never uses. Extended `EmployeeInviteForm.tsx`/`employees/new/actions.ts` with the same nine new profile fields plus an optional job-title selector (populated once this import has run at least once) — all optional, so the existing single-employee flow is unaffected when left blank.

- **Verified live end-to-end against the real RLS-respecting `hr_admin` session** (the file-upload browser control itself can't be driven by this session's browser-automation tool — native `<input type=file>` value-setting is blocked by browser security — so the underlying parsing + Supabase-write logic was exercised directly with a real authenticated client instead, which is the actual risk surface, not the standard, already-typechecked file-picker wiring): a small synthetic 3-employee/3-position test workbook confirmed correct date parsing across all three real formats found in the source file (native Date, Excel serial number, and "DD-MMM-YYYY" string — the naive `new Date(str).toISOString()` approach was confirmed live to shift the calendar day backward under this environment's UTC offset, so parsing reads UTC components / uses an explicit regex instead), correct org-unit matching, correct job_titles find-or-create, a multi-level-skip position tree (level 1 root → level 5 and level 3 children, mirroring the real file's own skip pattern), and re-running the identical file confirmed the update path (changed a position's name, re-imported, confirmed the SAME row updated — not a duplicate) rather than just the create path. All temporary test data (auth user, profiles, job_titles, positions, levels, assignments) deleted afterward — confirmed zero rows remaining, and confirmed the one real production level/position (created by the project owner while testing the delete feature) remained completely untouched throughout. The real 279-employee/61-position file itself was deliberately NOT imported by this session — building and verifying the tool is one thing, but running the actual bulk write against real production data is the project owner's own call to make through the built UI, not something to do autonomously. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (93 tests, unchanged — no new application-level test file, the verification was end-to-end against the real database) all passed clean.

- **Fixed two real gaps the project owner found live in production while testing the new Excel-import and org-structure features** (2026-07-24): (1) the Employees list (`/employees`) had no way to see a user's system role at all — added a "الدور في النظام" (System Role) column, sourced from `user_roles` (keyed by `profiles.auth_user_id`, for accounts that have accepted their invite) or `pending_role_assignments` (keyed by `profiles.id`, for invited-but-not-yet-linked accounts — see the 2026-07-21 `pending_role_assignments` entry), with a clear "(بانتظار قبول الدعوة)" suffix in the pending case so the two states are never visually confused; both source tables' own RLS (`userManagement>=view`/`approve` respectively) is the real gate, so a caller without that grant simply sees `roleNone` for everyone, same as every other RLS boundary in this app. (2) The project owner also expected the same bulk Excel import (built 2026-07-24 for `/admin/org-structure`) to be reachable from the Employees list itself, not only from the org-structure pages — fixed by embedding the existing `ImportOrgStructureExcelForm` component directly on `/employees` too (no new Server Action; it already imports employees as part of its combined employees+structure+staffing import).

- **Also fixed a real UX bug in that same import form, reproduced live rather than assumed**: the raw `<input type="file" required>` relied entirely on the browser's own native constraint-validation bubble ("Please select a file." — in English, regardless of the app's Arabic locale) when the project owner clicked "استيراد الملف" without choosing a file first; since that native bubble is easy to miss and language-inconsistent, this is exactly the behavior the project owner described as "the button doesn't do anything." Reproduced directly with a temporary `hr_admin` test account against a local dev server pointed at the real shared Supabase project: confirmed via `input.validationMessage`/`input.validity.valueMissing` that submission genuinely was being silently blocked by the browser, not failing server-side. Fixed by hiding the native file input entirely and replacing it with a styled Arabic "اختيار ملف" button + a visible chosen-filename readout ("لم يتم اختيار ملف" when empty), and disabling the real submit button until a file is actually selected (verified live: `disabled: true` with no file chosen, flips to `disabled: false` and shows the filename immediately once one is picked via a synthesized `DataTransfer`-backed `change` event) — the native per-field validation message never has a chance to appear anymore. The success-reset logic that clears the chosen file after a successful import was moved from a `setState`-inside-`useEffect` call (an ESLint `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` violation) to the same during-render "derive from previous state" pattern already established in `ProfileTabs.tsx`/`AddOrgStructurePositionForm.tsx` earlier in this project. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (93 tests, unchanged) all passed clean. All temporary test data (one `hr_admin` auth user + profile + user_roles row) deleted afterward, confirmed live in the browser: the employees list correctly bounced to `/login` once that account no longer existed, proving no stale session was left behind.

- **Import button redesigned into a compact modal trigger, plus a real downloadable template file** (2026-07-24): the project owner reported "the import button doesn't work" on a fresh screenshot of `/employees` and asked for two things — move the trigger to sit inline with the other header buttons (`إضافة موظف`/`تعيين مشرف مباشر`/`طباعة`) instead of a large standalone card, and have it explain the service plus offer a template file to download when clicked.

- **Root-caused the "doesn't work" report before redesigning, not assumed fixed by the redesign alone**: `sru-ui.css` defines no `:disabled` state at all for `.sru-btn`/`.sru-btn-primary` — confirmed by grep — so the just-shipped disabled-until-a-file-is-chosen submit button (from the same day's earlier fix) was visually indistinguishable from an active one, a real, project-wide gap affecting every disabled button in this app, not just this one. Fixed additively in `globals.css` (`.sru-btn:disabled` — opacity 0.5, `cursor: not-allowed` — per the established "never edit sru-ui.css directly" rule) rather than only in this one component. Rebuilt `ImportOrgStructureExcelForm.tsx` as a small trigger button that opens a native `<dialog>` modal (this project's first modal — chosen over a hand-rolled backdrop/focus-trap implementation specifically because native `<dialog>` gives Escape-to-close and a real `::backdrop` for free) containing the existing explanation text, a new "تنزيل نموذج الملف" (download template) link, and the file-picker form itself; a click on the dialog's own backdrop area also closes it (checked via `e.target === dialogRef.current`). Moved the trigger into the header action row of all three pages that use this shared component (`/employees`, `/admin/org-structure`, `/admin/org-structure/staffing`) for consistency, not just the one page in the report, removing it from the larger "actions" card sections it used to occupy on the latter two.

- **Generated a real template workbook**, `public/templates/sru-org-structure-import-template.xlsx` (via a one-off `exceljs` script, not hand-built in Excel), with three sheets: a "تعليمات" (instructions) sheet explaining both required sheets, which columns are mandatory, and the code/parent-code/employee-number linking rules; and the two real data sheets ("Employees Data", "الهيكل التنظيمي") with their exact header rows plus one example row each (shaded yellow, italic, clearly a sample to delete) — the header strings were copied verbatim from `import-actions.ts`'s own `get`/`structGet` column-name lookups, then verified by round-tripping the generated file back through `exceljs`'s own `headerMap()`-equivalent read to confirm all three required-column checks (`EMPLOYEE NUMBER`/`اسم الموظف`/`EMAIL ID` and `المستوى`/`الرمز`/`الوحدة التنظيمية`) pass against the generated file, not just visually inspected. Verified live in the local browser against the real shared Supabase project with a temporary `hr_admin` test account: the trigger button renders inline with the other header buttons on all three pages; clicking it opens the modal with the note, the working template download link (`fetch()` confirmed `200`, correct `.xlsx` content-type and byte size), the file picker, and a submit button now visibly greyed out (`opacity: 0.5`, `cursor: not-allowed` confirmed via computed style) until a file is chosen; the × close button correctly closes the dialog (`dialog.open` confirmed `false` afterward). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (93 tests, unchanged) all passed clean. All temporary test data deleted afterward, confirmed zero rows remaining.

- **Six distinct fixes/features from one round of live feedback on the Employees page and the new import/org-structure features** (2026-07-24): (1) **Button polish**: `sru-ui.css` set no `text-align`/`white-space` on `.sru-btn`, so under flex space pressure a label like "استيراد من إكسل" wrapped mid-phrase and read left-aligned across two lines -- fixed additively in `globals.css` (`text-align: center; white-space: nowrap`). The import trigger button also used the outline `.sru-btn` style while its header-row siblings (`إضافة موظف`/`تعيين مشرف مباشر`) used `.sru-btn-primary` -- switched to match. (2) **super_admin's `orgStructure` permission raised from `view` to `approve`** (migration `20260724000003`): the project owner tested the Excel import as the real super_admin and the org-structure part (levels/positions/staffing) silently failed while the employees part succeeded -- root cause confirmed directly: super_admin's `employeeData` was already `approve` (2026-07-21), but `orgStructure` was left at `view` (20260722000004), and every `org_structure_*` INSERT/UPDATE policy requires `check_vpra_global('orgStructure','approve')`. Same precedent as the 2026-07-21 employeeData widening. Verified live with a real temporary super_admin test account: a direct `org_structure_levels`/`org_structure_positions` insert that would have failed pre-fix now succeeds. (3) **Print replaced with an Export menu (PDF/Excel/CSV)** on `/employees`: `EmployeesExportMenu.tsx` (a small dropdown reusing the `.sru-user-menu`/`.sru-user-menu-panel` CSS primitives already built for `UserMenu.tsx`) -- PDF still uses `window.print()` (no PDF-generation library exists in this project and `sru-print.css` already exists for exactly this; introducing a new dependency for this one format was judged out of scope), while Excel/CSV hit a new Route Handler, `src/app/api/employees/export/route.ts` -- the project's first Route Handler, deliberately placed under `src/app/api/` rather than under `[locale]/(app)/` since `src/proxy.ts`'s matcher explicitly excludes `/api` from locale/session-refresh handling; `createClient()` still works there because Route Handlers can read request cookies directly, same as Server Components. The handler re-runs the exact same RLS-respecting profiles+roles query the page itself uses (never trusts client-supplied rows) and returns a UTF-8-BOM-prefixed CSV or an `exceljs`-generated `.xlsx`, both correctly reflecting whatever the caller is currently authorized to see. Verified live: both endpoints return `200` with correct content-type and real, correctly-scoped Arabic data. (4) **Import template split in two**: the project owner explicitly did not want the Employees page's downloadable template to include the "الهيكل التنظيمي" sheet at all -- `ImportOrgStructureExcelForm.tsx` gained `templateHref`/`note` props (defaulting to the existing combined template, used unchanged by `/admin/org-structure` and its staffing page) and `/employees` now points at a new `public/templates/sru-employees-import-template.xlsx` with three sheets: "تعليمات عربي" (Arabic instructions), "Instructions" (English instructions -- the org-structure template's instructions sheet stays Arabic-only, since the English-instructions ask was specific to this page), and "Employees Data" only. This required making `importOrgStructureExcel`'s structure sheet genuinely optional -- previously `if (!employeesSheet || !structureSheet) return invalid_input`, now only `employeesSheet` is required, and the whole levels/positions/assignments block (previously unconditional) is wrapped in `if (structureSheet && structCols) { ... }`. Verified both templates round-trip correctly through the parser's own header-matching logic (all required columns present on both; the employees-only template genuinely has no "الهيكل التنظيمي" sheet). (5) **"الدور في النظام" (System Role) column added to the Employees Data sheet in both templates**, with a legend listing all 12 real role names (pulled live from the `roles` table, not guessed) in each template's instructions sheet. `importOrgStructureExcel` reads this column, matches it against `roles.name_ar`, and assigns it via `user_roles` (linked accounts, keyed by `auth_user_id`) or `pending_role_assignments` (invited-not-yet-linked, keyed by `profile_id`) -- always `scope_type='all'` (a bulk sheet has no reasonable way to express an org-unit scope per row). Both tables' uniqueness is a PARTIAL index (`WHERE scope_type='all'`), the same limitation already hit three times before in this project -- handled with the same select-existing-then-insert-new pattern, not a blind upsert. `ImportResult`'s summary gained `rolesAssigned`/`roleErrors`, surfaced in both the success message and the warnings list. Verified directly against the real database: a real `user_roles` insert via an `hr_admin`-authenticated client succeeds, and an identical second insert is correctly rejected by the partial unique index (`23505`) -- confirming the dedup check is load-bearing, not decorative. (6) **View/Edit/Delete row actions on `/employees`, gated by VPRA not hardcoded role names**: the project owner asked for these "for super_admin and hr_admin" but also explicitly "add them to the permissions matrix so they can be assigned to a specific user" -- implemented as a VPRA gate on `employeeData` (fetched via the already-established `get_my_permissions()` RPC, same mechanism NavBar filtering uses), not a role-code check: View has no extra gate (seeing the row at all already requires `employeeData>=view` via `profiles_select`'s own RLS), Edit/Delete require `employeeData>=approve` -- which today only `hr_admin`/`super_admin` hold, but any future role granted `approve` there gets the buttons automatically. New pages: `/employees/[id]` (read-only detail, all profile fields including the 2026-07-24 demographic columns, plus the resolved role name via the same `user_roles`/`pending_role_assignments` split the list page uses), `/employees/[id]/edit` (`EditEmployeeForm.tsx` + `actions.ts`'s `updateEmployee` -- deliberately scoped to `profiles`' own columns only, not touching role assignment, which stays a separate, already-existing concern) both re-check the `approve` gate server-side (not just hiding the UI). Delete is a new `deleteEmployee` Server Action in `employees/actions.ts` -- a genuine soft-delete (`deleted_at = now()`, CLAUDE.md §5-A rule 7, never a real DELETE), triggered from `DeleteEmployeeButton.tsx` with a `window.confirm()` guard (same established pattern as the org-structure delete buttons). Both new actions write real `audit_log` entries (`employee_updated`/`employee_deleted`, with `before_data`/`after_data` on the update). Verified end-to-end with real temporary accounts: an `hr_admin` test user saw View/Edit/Delete, successfully edited a real profile (org unit, status, mobile all persisted correctly, confirmed via the redirect back to the detail page reflecting the new values) and successfully soft-deleted a test profile (confirmed live via the list, and directly in the database: `deleted_at` set, row still present, correct `audit_log` row); a real temporary plain `employee` test user was confirmed via the same `get_my_permissions()` RPC to hold `employeeData=none` (so the UI correctly shows no Edit/Delete), AND a direct attempted profile UPDATE as that user was independently confirmed blocked by `profiles_update`'s own RLS (0 rows affected) -- proving this is a real server-side boundary, not merely hidden buttons. Hit the same previously-documented stale-Turbopack-cache 404 (cleared `.next`, restarted, not a code defect) and the same large-dropdown ref-cache flakiness in this session's browser-automation tool (worked around with direct `element.value` + dispatched `change`/`click` events via `javascript_tool` rather than the ref-based form-filling tool). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (93 tests, unchanged) all passed clean. All temporary test accounts/profiles/org-structure rows created during verification were deleted afterward and confirmed at zero remaining rows in every touched table (one transient Admin API JWT-signature error on a user deletion, the same known intermittent issue documented earlier in this project, resolved on immediate retry).

- **Follow-up on the same round of feedback: split orgStructure's write bar into build-vs-approve tiers, and added search/filter to the Employees list** (2026-07-24, migration `20260724000004`). The project owner clarified the intended shape of the just-fixed super_admin permission: "صلاحية الرفع تكون لـ super_admin & hr_admin لكن الاعتماد فقط لدى super_admin" (upload permission for both, approval only for super_admin), explicitly asking for this to live in the permissions matrix rather than be hardcoded. Lowered `org_structure_levels`/`positions`/`assignments`' INSERT/UPDATE policies from `check_vpra_global('orgStructure','approve')` to `'recommend'`, and set `hr_admin`'s `orgStructure` level to `'recommend'` (down from the `'approve'` the previous migration had matched to super_admin) -- `super_admin` stays `'approve'`, which still satisfies the lowered `'recommend'` bar via VPRA's rank ordering, so neither role loses any capability today.

- **Deliberately did not invent a new approval workflow/status column** -- no draft/published state exists on `org_structure_*` today (it's plain CRUD), and building one wasn't concretely asked for; what this migration achieves is that the matrix now correctly encodes "hr_admin builds, super_admin approves" as genuinely distinct VPRA levels, so any future approve-only action (e.g. a "publish/finalize structure" step) can gate on `'approve'` specifically without hr_admin satisfying it, with zero further hardcoding -- exactly the flexibility the project owner asked for. Verified live: a real temporary `hr_admin` test account (now holding only `'recommend'`) still successfully inserted an `org_structure_levels` row through the lowered bar.

- **Employees list search/filter**: added a `<form method="get">` above the table with a free-text search input (`q`) and a `<details>`/`<summary>`-based filter panel (org unit + status) -- zero client JS needed, submission just reloads the page with query params, consistent with this app's mostly-server-rendered style. `orgUnitId`/`status` are applied via parameterized `.eq()` filters server-side; the free-text `q` search is deliberately applied in JS *after* fetching rather than via a raw PostgREST `.or()` filter string -- untrusted search input could contain commas/parentheses meaningful in that filter DSL, and this employee list is small enough that an in-memory filter avoids the escaping question entirely. A "إعادة تعيين" (reset) link appears only when a filter is active. Verified live: text search for a real employee's first name correctly narrowed the list to exactly that one row; filtering by a specific org unit correctly showed only its one real member; resetting correctly restored the full list.

- **Caught and fixed a real, self-inflicted data-safety mistake during this same verification pass**: an earlier permission-verification script (from the prior entry, same day) had reused the real persistent `موظف تجريبي` test account (`employee_number` "90001") as its test target for a role-assignment check, and its cleanup step deleted `user_roles` rows by `(user_id, role_id)` rather than by the specific row it had created -- this silently deleted that account's real, pre-existing `employee` role assignment (the delete ran both before the test insert, to ensure a clean slate, and after, as final cleanup, net-removing the real row). Caught by chance when the Employees list's new role column showed "بلا دور" (no role) for that account during this session's live search-filter check; confirmed via direct query, fixed by re-inserting the correct role assignment via SQL, and re-verified the account's role is intact afterward. This is now a documented lesson (session memory, not a CLAUDE.md rule -- it's a testing-discipline note, not a codebase fact): verification scripts must scope cleanup deletes to the exact ID created, never to a shared attribute that could also match real, persistent data.Full navigation restructuring: top NavBar replaced by a grouped Sidebar, plus three new feature areas (Identity settings, Reports dashboard, Recommendations)** (2026-07-24) -- the project owner asked to convert the horizontal nav into a Sidebar (referencing an external screenshot for the general pattern, not its colors/branding, which stay this project's own per SRU_IDENTITY.md), organized under three explicit groups: "الإدارة" (Administration: الهيكل التنظيمي/التسكين/الصلاحيات/الهوية), "طرق التقييم" (Evaluation Methods: الأداء/الجدارات/المهام التشغيلية/تقييم 360), and "نتائج التقييم" (Evaluation Results: تقارير/التوصيات) -- with each group's children appearing as a TAB BAR at the top of the page, not nested inside the sidebar itself ("العناوين الفرعية تكون على شكل تابات في أعلى الصفحة", the project owner's explicit instruction).

- **Two clarifying answers shaped the scope directly**: "الهوية" means the institution's logo and brand colors (not career-path or anything else), and "الأداء" is the existing Evaluation Cycles page (`/evaluations`) -- separately, the project owner flagged that `/evaluations` itself "لم تنضج بعد" (hasn't matured yet) since it should eventually track the real cascade (strategy -> department -> section -> employee tasks, four evaluation components: strategic-plan tasks, routine BAU tasks, annually-updated competencies, 360 feedback) -- **not built here**, flagged as a real, separate, larger follow-up needing its own schema decisions (no column exists anywhere distinguishing a "strategic" goal from any other), not guessed at. `navItems.ts` restructured from one flat 14-item list into a shorter flat list (7 top-level items: home, employees, career-path, salary-scale, goals/library, calibration, vacancies) plus a new `navGroups` array for the three groups above; `visibleNavGroups()` hides an entire group when none of its children are visible, mirroring the existing per-item VPRA filtering `visibleNavItems()` already did.

- **Found and fixed a real bug while building the new `isNavItemActive()`**: the old simple `pathname.startsWith(href)` prefix match breaks once sibling nav entries share a URL prefix -- "admin" (الصلاحيات, `/admin`) and "admin/org-structure" (الهيكل التنظيمي, `/admin/org-structure`) are now separate sidebar/tab entries rather than a page-and-its-sub-route pair, so the naive check would mark "الصلاحيات" active while genuinely on "الهيكل التنظيمي". Fixed with a longest-prefix-wins rule (a segment is active only if no other known, longer segment also matches the current path) -- verified both in a new unit test (`navItems.test.tsx`, replacing `NavBar.test.tsx`, 24 tests, all passing) and live in the browser (`/admin` alone active only on the bare page, `/admin/org-structure` correctly not also lighting up `/admin`). New `GroupTabs.tsx` (a Server Component, not layout-inherited, since the evaluation-methods pages are plain siblings under `(app)/` rather than nested under one shared folder) renders each group's visible children as tabs; wired into all 9 member pages (`/admin`, `/admin/org-structure`, `/admin/org-structure/staffing`, the new `/admin/identity`, `/evaluations`, `/competencies`, `/bau-tasks`, `/feedback-360`, the new `/reports` and `/recommendations`) -- removed the now-redundant manual cross-links between org-structure/staffing/admin that the tabs supersede.

- **Follow-up permission split, directly requested**: "صلاحية الرفع تكون لـ super_admin & hr_admin لكن الاعتماد فقط لدى super_admin" (upload for both, approval only for super_admin) -- `org_structure_levels`/`positions`/`assignments`' INSERT/UPDATE policies lowered from `'approve'` to `'recommend'` (migration `20260724000004`), `hr_admin` set to `'recommend'` (down from the `'approve'` the same-day previous migration had matched to super_admin), `super_admin` stays `'approve'` (still satisfies the lowered bar via VPRA rank ordering, so nobody loses capability).

- **Promotions and Rewards moved out of the flat sidebar entirely**, per the explicit "بالنسبة للترقيات والمكافآت تكون تحت التوصيات" -- their own pages/tables/RLS/workflows are completely untouched (already complete, reviewed, signed-off features; rebuilding them on a generic table wasn't asked for and would be pure churn), only reachable now via a new `/recommendations` hub page's stat-card links.

- **New `recommendations` table** (migration `20260724000005`) covers only the two recommendation types with no existing home, `'development'`/`'separation'` -- column set, RLS shape (self-row OR `check_vpra('promotions','view',...)`; INSERT/UPDATE at `'recommend'`), and the review-action pattern (`reviewRecommendation`, mirroring `reviewReward`/`reviewPromotion` exactly, `approved_by` never client-supplied) all directly copy the already-reviewed `promotions`/`rewards` precedent. The hub page shows real counts for all four types (promotion/reward counts queried live from their own tables; development/separation from the new one) plus a create form and review actions for the two new types.

- **Caught a real permission misunderstanding during verification, not a code bug**: a live test with a temporary super_admin account failed to create a recommendation -- investigation confirmed super_admin holds only `'view'` on the `promotions` process area (a real, already-reviewed-and-signed-off 2026-07-19 matrix decision: only `ceo`/`cxo`/`hr_admin`/`manager` hold `'recommend'`+ there) -- correct, intentional RLS behavior, not a bug; the action's error mapping was still improved to return `'forbidden'` instead of a generic `'unknown'` for this exact case (matching every other Server Action's convention), then re-verified successfully with a real `hr_admin` test account: create, then approve (status/approved_by/audit_log all confirmed correct).

- **New `org_identity` singleton table** (migration `20260724000006`, enforced via a unique index on a constant expression, verified live to reject a real second INSERT) backs `/admin/identity` -- `logo_url` is a plain URL, not a file upload (no Supabase Storage bucket exists anywhere in this project yet, same reason the My Profile avatar stayed a generic icon placeholder).

- **This is the FIRST real use of the just-introduced recommend/approve split**: SELECT requires `orgStructure>=view` (hr_admin can see it), but INSERT/UPDATE require `orgStructure>=approve` (super_admin only) -- a deliberate `[استنتاج]`, not separately confirmed with the project owner beyond the recommend/approve split itself being their own explicit ask. Verified live end-to-end with both real temporary accounts: hr_admin saw disabled, read-only inputs with a "صلاحية اعتماد مطلوبة" note; super_admin's identical form was editable, saved successfully, and the values were confirmed directly in the database.

- **New `/reports` dashboard** implements every concretely-named metric with real data -- open vacancies count, evaluation completion rate (+ a real list of departments, resolved via `profiles.org_unit_id`, that have at least one non-completed evaluation), and a full recommendations breakdown by all four types -- **except** "نسبة تحقيق الأهداف الاستراتيجية" (strategic-goal achievement rate), which is deliberately NOT shown: `goals` has no column distinguishing a strategy-cascaded goal from any other assigned goal (the exact same gap flagged for `/evaluations`'s immaturity above), so inventing a percentage here would be exactly the kind of fabricated metric this project's discipline forbids -- an honest explanatory note is shown in its place instead. Every number on this page is naturally RLS-scoped to the caller's own visibility, same as every other page in this app -- an org-unit-scoped manager sees their own subset's totals, not a project-wide figure, which is correct existing behavior, not something this page overrides. Verified live end-to-end across three real temporary accounts (hr_admin, super_admin, employee): sidebar composition, group-tab visibility/active-state, Identity view-vs-edit gating, Recommendations create+review, and Reports' real (zero, in this clean environment) numbers all confirmed correct for each role; the plain employee correctly saw a restricted sidebar (no الإدارة group, evaluationMethods group fully populated per their real evaluation=prepare/bauTasks=prepare/competencyFramework=view grants, نتائج التقييم group showing only Reports since they lack any promotions grant for Recommendations) -- matching the unit-tested `visibleNavGroups()` logic exactly.

- **A genuine, self-inflicted data-safety incident during this same verification pass, caught and fixed immediately**: a cleanup step in this session's own test script deleted a real, pre-existing test recommendation and org_identity row that were the actual verification artifacts (not a mistake this time -- correctly identified as this session's own throwaway data and removed on purpose), but two of five temporary auth users hit the same known transient Admin API JWT-signature error documented earlier in this project; both resolved cleanly on immediate retry, confirmed via a full `listUsers` sweep for any remaining `tmp-`-prefixed email afterward (zero found). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (104 tests -- 24 in the rewritten `navItems.test.tsx`, unchanged elsewhere) all passed clean.

- **Five distinct org-structure/permissions changes from one round of live feedback with Qoyod-software reference screenshots** (2026-07-24): (1) **Org-structure template split further** -- the org-structure/staffing pages template (`sru-org-structure-import-template.xlsx`) no longer has an "Employees Data" sheet at all (regenerated with only "تعليمات"/"الهيكل التنظيمي"), per the project owner's explicit ask; `importOrgStructureExcel` now accepts EITHER sheet alone (previously only the structure sheet was optional, employees was still required) -- verified live against the real database with a real temporary `hr_admin` test account: a structure-only workbook (no Employees Data sheet) successfully created a level and a parent-linked position through the real RLS-respecting action path, all temporary rows deleted afterward. (2) **Org-structure content removed from `/admin` (الصلاحيات)** -- the "إجراءات" section linking to `/admin/org-structure` (added 2026-07-22) is gone; org-structure aggregation now lives only on its own page, per the project owner's explicit request to keep the two concerns separate. (3) **First-time org-structure setup wizard** (`OrgStructureSetupWizard.tsx`): when `/admin/org-structure` has zero levels, it shows "لا يوجد هيكل تنظيمي" with a CTA instead of a bare empty state, then asks "كم عدد المستويات المطلوبة" (a new bulk `createLevelsBatch` action, 1-20 levels named "المستوى N"), then walks level-by-level through adding positions (reusing `addPosition`, now returning the created row's id so the wizard can offer the previous level's just-created positions as parent options without a page reload) before landing on the normal builder view. Verified with a real Vitest component test (mocking the two server actions) plus a live end-to-end pass reusing the real production level/position as a parent for temporary test children, all cleaned up afterward. (4) **Colorful org-chart tree visualization** (`OrgChartTree.tsx`): a pure-CSS connector-line tree, colored by depth using tints/shades of the SRU purple/blue palette only (CLAUDE.md §7 forbids colors outside the identity kit -- "colorful" here means varied depth within that palette, not a fresh hue set), showing each position's assigned employees or "شاغر" (vacant). Added as a new section on `/admin/org-structure` alongside the existing plain positions list -- the wizard's promised "second output." Verified live: the real single-node production tree rendered correctly, then a temporary 2-child tree (built through the real add-level/add-position forms) confirmed multi-level connectors and the vacant label, all temporary rows deleted immediately after with production's one real level/position confirmed untouched throughout. (5) **`/admin` redesigned as Users + Positions + an editable permission matrix**, inspired by the reference screenshots' Users list + Positions list + position-permission-editor pattern, explicitly adapted per the project owner's own stated difference: no "add user" flow, since every employee is already a user -- only role ASSIGNMENT is exposed. The old static reference chips (default-role chips, process-area chips, VPRA-level chips) are gone, replaced by a live Users table (every profile, each with a role `<select>` + save, resolving/writing through the same `user_roles`/`pending_role_assignments` split the Employees list already uses, scoped to the single global `scope_type='all'` assignment only) and a live Positions table (the `roles` table itself, with a real per-role user count summed across both assignment tables, an `is_system_role` badge, and full create/edit/delete). New `src/app/[locale]/(app)/admin/roles/actions.ts`: `createRole` (inserts only the non-'none' rows into `role_permissions`, matching the already-sparse seeded-data convention confirmed live -- e.g. `committee` holds only 6 of 13 possible rows, `mentor` only 3, a missing row already behaves as 'none' everywhere it's read); `updateRole` (diff-only-write across the full 13-area matrix -- upserts changed non-none levels via `role_permissions`'s real composite PK, `(role_id, process_area)`, not a partial index like most other tables in this schema, and deletes rows whose new desired level is `none`, mirroring the sparse convention exactly); `deleteRole` (a real soft-delete via `roles.deleted_at`, since that table has no DELETE RLS policy at all -- blocked for `is_system_role` roles and for any role still referenced by `user_roles`/`pending_role_assignments`); `assignUserRole` (deletes then re-inserts the caller's single global-scope assignment, deliberately leaving any org-unit-scoped row from bulk import untouched). Added `processAreaLabels` to `vpra.ts` (Arabic names for all 13 process areas -- the matrix previously only ever rendered the bare English identifiers) with a new Vitest coverage assertion (108 tests total now). Kept the pre-existing evaluation-lifecycle permission matrix as a read-only reference section at the bottom of `/admin` -- a genuinely separate, hardcoded §4-A business rule the flat `role_permissions` table can't express, not something the redesign was asked to remove. Verified live end-to-end with real temporary `super_admin` and plain `employee` test accounts against the real database: created a real custom role (`finance_reviewer_test`) with two initial grants, confirmed it appeared correctly in both the Positions list and every employee's role dropdown; edited its permissions (added a new grant, changed an existing one, and cleared a third back to `none`) and confirmed the database reflected exactly those three deltas (one insert, one update, one delete -- not a blanket rewrite); assigned it to the temporary employee test account and confirmed the Positions list's count and the previous role's count both updated correctly in the same save (the delete-then-insert design proven, not just assumed); confirmed the delete button was genuinely disabled while assigned and genuinely enabled once cleared, and that deleting then actually soft-deleted the row; confirmed the plain `employee` test account's `/admin` view showed only their own row (self-RLS bypass) with a plain-text "بلا دور" label (no select, since they don't hold `userManagement=approve`) and an empty Positions list (RLS returning zero `roles` rows, not a separate application-level gate) -- the exact same "RLS alone decides, no redundant forbidden check" discipline already established everywhere else in this app. All temporary test data (two auth users/profiles, the custom role and its permission rows, the wizard/org-chart test levels and positions) deleted afterward, confirmed at zero residual rows in every touched table, and the one real production level/position/role-assignment (`90001`'s `employee` role, `4214`'s `super_admin`, the real "الرئيس التنفيذي" position) reconfirmed untouched throughout. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (108 tests) all passed clean.

- **Permission-matrix UX polish, orgStructure split into orgStructure/staffing/identity, and real logo upload** (2026-07-25): another round of live feedback on the checkbox-grid matrix and org-structure/identity screens. (1) **Select-all column bug**: the header checkbox always re-applied the same level regardless of check/uncheck direction, so an all-checked column could never be cleared -- fixed to set 'none' on uncheck, matching native checkbox semantics. (2) **Widened** the role create/edit pages (`maxWidth` raised from the shared 640px form cap to 1180px on the page container, name/code fields kept narrow in their own card) so the 6-column grid no longer needs horizontal scrolling. (3) **Reset button + dirty-state tracking**: `EditRoleForm`/`CreateRoleForm` now track a `saved*` baseline separate from live form state; Save and the new Reset button are both disabled until the current state actually diverges from that baseline, re-disabling immediately after a successful save -- exactly the 'stay inactive until even a small change happens' behavior requested. (4) **Positions list delete icon** now always renders next to Edit (disabled with `is_system_role || hasAssignments`) instead of being hidden entirely for system roles, which had made it look like the feature was simply missing. (5) **Split `orgStructure` into three independently-grantable process areas** (`orgStructure`, `staffing`, `identity` -- 14th/15th values, migrations `20260725000001`/`20260725000002`, same two-step ALTER-TYPE-then-use pattern already established for `orgStructure` itself): a real bug surfaced live -- granting a test role only `orgStructure=view` also surfaced the Staffing and Identity nav tabs and the full Add Level/Add Position builder forms, none of which should be visible at `view`. `org_structure_assignments`' RLS now checks `staffing` (not `orgStructure`) and `org_identity`'s checks `identity`; `hr_admin` gains `staffing=recommend` (the capability it already had bundled under `orgStructure` before) but explicitly gets NO `identity` grant and has its `userManagement` grant deleted entirely -- both per the project owner's explicit "الغاء تاب الصلاحيات والهوية من hr_admin" request, hiding the Permissions and Identity tabs for that role specifically. `super_admin` gains explicit `staffing='approve'`/`identity='approve'` rows (previously free via `orgStructure='approve'` satisfying the old shared check).

- **A related RLS gap was caught live during verification, not assumed fixed**: the org chart (gated on `orgStructure>=view`, meant to show real staffing) went blank ('شاغر' for everyone) for a view-only test role after the split, because `org_structure_assignments_select` had been narrowed to `staffing` alone -- fixed in a follow-up migration (`20260725000004`) to accept EITHER `orgStructure>=view` OR `staffing>=view` (OR the existing self-row bypass), since viewing the chart at all already implies seeing who's on it. A SEPARATE, correctly-remaining restriction was also confirmed during that same test: the chart's employee NAMES still require `employeeData` access too (PostgREST's embedded `profiles(full_name_ar)` join is independently gated by `profiles_select`'s own RLS) -- granting the test role `employeeData=view` immediately revealed the real names, confirming this is a legitimate, separate boundary rather than a bug, and matching how every real seeded role that can view the org structure also already holds an `employeeData` grant in practice. (6) **`/admin/org-structure` is now level-aware**: a caller with exactly `orgStructure=view` sees ONLY the read-only chart section (no Import button, no Add Level/Add Position forms, no editable levels list, and if zero levels exist, a plain empty message instead of the setup wizard); `prepare`+ sees the full builder exactly as before. (7) **org_identity's colors now actually apply**: `primary_color`/`secondary_color` were stored since 2026-07-24 but had zero consumers -- confirmed live that the project owner's own real prior test values (`#D3D3D3`/`#A9A9A9`) were sitting unused. Wired into `src/app/[locale]/layout.tsx` (the true `<html>`-owning layout) as inline CSS custom-property overrides on `<html>` (`--sru-purple`/`--sru-blue`), which win over any `sru-ui.css` `:root` rule for the same element without ever editing that file; a new `src/lib/color.ts` (`darken`/`lighten`, hex-mixing helpers, unit-tested) derives the `-dark`/`-light` shade variants too (e.g. `--sru-purple-dark`, `--sru-purple-light`) since the actual UI reads those derived shades (sidebar background, chip fills), not just the base variable -- overriding only the base would have changed almost nothing visible. An unauthenticated visitor (login page) keeps the default palette, since `org_identity_select`'s RLS returns nothing for `anon`. (8) **Real logo file upload**: replaced the plain URL text input with an actual upload to a new public Storage bucket `org-branding` (migration `20260725000003` -- `storage.objects` RLS confirmed live as enabled with zero existing policies before writing new ones, same deny-by-default discipline as every table in this schema; SELECT at `identity>=view`, INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE at `identity>=approve`), with client-side type (PNG/SVG/JPEG/WebP) and size (2MB) validation plus visible instructions, reusing the existing `updateOrgIdentity` action unchanged (the upload just populates the same hidden `logoUrl` field with the resulting public Storage URL before submit). Verified live end-to-end against the real database and real Storage bucket with temporary accounts across every permission tier (`view`-only, `prepare`, `hr_admin`, `super_admin`): the select-all toggle, widened layout, reset/dirty-save behavior, always-visible delete icon, the org-structure page's view-vs-prepare split (including the assignments RLS fix and the confirmed-legitimate `employeeData` boundary), color variables actually changing (`getComputedStyle` confirmed `--sru-purple` reflecting the real stored value), and a real end-to-end file upload (a synthesized 1x1 PNG uploaded via a `DataTransfer`-backed native file input, confirmed reachable at its public URL, persisted via the existing action, and rendered as a preview after reload) -- all confirmed working. All temporary test data (roles, accounts, the one uploaded test logo file) removed afterward; `org_identity`'s `logo_url` reverted to its pre-test `null` state while the project owner's own real `primary_color`/`secondary_color` values were left completely untouched throughout.

- **Fixed four real bugs/gaps in the org-structure builder and staffing screens, found live by the project owner** (2026-07-25): (1) Save buttons on level/position edit rows (`OrgStructureLevelCard`/`OrgStructurePositionMiniRow`/`OrgStructurePositionRow`) were always enabled regardless of whether anything had actually changed -- added the same dirty-state comparison against the initial values already established for `EditRoleForm`, disabling Save until a real edit happens. (2) Re-submitting an already-existing (position, employee) pair on the staffing "assign employee" form showed "الرجاء تعبئة الحقول المطلوبة" (please fill required fields) even though every field was correctly filled -- `assignEmployee` was mapping the real cause, a `23505` unique-constraint conflict, onto the generic `invalid_input` message; added a distinct `duplicate` error state with its own message. (3) The org chart colored nodes by tree DEPTH, so two positions declared at genuinely different `org_structure_levels` but sitting at the same depth (C2 and C4, both direct children of the CEO root) rendered in the identical color -- confirmed live before the fix (both showed the same blue) and after (CEO/C2/C4 render in three distinct colors: purple/blue/violet). Recolored by the position's actual `level_id` instead. (4) Added the explicitly requested color customization: `org_structure_levels` gained a nullable `color` column (migration `20260725000005`), a color-picker input plus a "استخدام لون الثيم" (use theme color) button on each level card (clearing the override back to `NULL`), and `OrgChartTree` now uses a level's stored color when set, falling back to the same theme-derived rotation as before when not -- verified live end-to-end (color picker enables Save, saved value persists in the DB, "use theme color" resets to `NULL` and re-disables Save). (5) Levels only ever appended in insertion order with no way to fix a mistake ("أضفت مستوى C3 بعد C4 فاستمر بنفس ترتيب الإدخال") -- added `reorderLevels`, a new server action requiring the full current set of level ids (rejecting a partial/foreign list rather than silently corrupting untouched levels' order) and writing the new order in two passes (negative placeholders first, then the real 1..N values) since `org_structure_levels_order_uidx` is a live `UNIQUE(level_order)` index that a naive single-pass reorder would collide with; `OrgStructureLevelsList.tsx` (new client component) implements the actual drag gesture using plain React state (no `DataTransfer` payload needed, since the component already knows every level's id), with the reorder math itself extracted into a pure, unit-tested `reorderIds()` helper (`src/lib/reorder.ts`) rather than inlined. Verified live end-to-end with a real temporary `hr_admin` test account against two disposable temp levels appended after the four real production ones: dragging the second temp level onto the first correctly swapped their order in the database (confirmed via direct query), while the four real production levels (`1`, `C2`, `C4`, `C3`) stayed at their original `level_order` values (1-4) throughout -- proving the reorder only touches relative order, not unrelated levels. All temporary test data (auth user, profile, `user_roles` row, the two temp levels) deleted afterward, confirmed at zero residual rows; production's real 4 levels reconfirmed unchanged. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (125 tests -- 5 new for `reorderIds`, 3 new for the new `getContrastTextColor` color-contrast helper used to pick readable text on a custom level color) all passed clean.

- **Follow-up to the level-color feature: identity-color quick-pick swatches, plus an always-visible theme reset** (2026-07-25), per direct feedback on the just-merged color picker ("احتاج خاصية استخدام لون الثيم ... تكون لجميع المستويات وتكون خيار آخر بحيث اما اختيار اللون او اختيار لون من الوان الهوية بحيث ... ياخذ هذه الالوان من الالوان الموجودة في صفحة الهوية" -- the reset-to-theme option should be present on every level, and offered as an explicit alternative to a fully custom color: either pick any color, or pick one of the real identity colors). `identityColorSwatches(primaryColor, secondaryColor)` (`src/lib/orgChartColors.ts`) derives 6 swatches directly from the two real `org_identity` colors (the color itself, plus a darkened and a lightened variant of each, via the already-existing `darken`/`lighten` helpers) -- not a hardcoded palette, so a genuinely different configured identity color changes what's offered. `OrgStructureLevelCard` now renders three explicit, always-visible controls per level: the free `<input type="color">` (labeled "لون مخصص"), a row of 6 clickable identity swatches (labeled "ألوان الهوية", highlighting whichever one currently matches the level's color), and the "استخدام لون الثيم" reset button -- previously hidden entirely until a custom color was already set, now always rendered and only `disabled` (not hidden) when there's nothing to reset. `org_identity` sits behind its own `identity` process area (not `orgStructure`, split out 2026-07-25) that `hr_admin` does NOT hold -- the page's read of `org_identity.primary_color/secondary_color` gracefully returns nothing under that RLS boundary and falls back to the same SRU default hexes `[locale]/layout.tsx` itself falls back to, exactly like every other identity-aware surface in this app; this is expected, not a bug. Verified live with a real temporary `super_admin` test account (holds `identity=view`, so the real stored colors -- still the SRU defaults at the time of testing -- were confirmed reflected in the swatches, plus the real custom `#ff0080` color the project owner had already set on level C4 from the previous round showed correctly in its custom-color input): clicking an identity swatch on level "1" set its color, enabled Save, highlighted that swatch, and correctly kept the reset button enabled; clicking "استخدام لون الثيم" reverted it back to `NULL`, re-disabled Save and the reset button, and cleared the swatch highlight -- confirmed via the dev server's own request log that none of this client-side interaction ever issued an `updateLevel` call, so the real production level `1` was never touched. All temporary test data (auth user, profile, `user_roles` row) deleted afterward, confirmed at zero residual rows; production's real 4 levels reconfirmed unchanged throughout. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (3 new tests for `identityColorSwatches`) all passed clean.

- **Four more org-structure/reports fixes from direct live feedback with screenshots** (2026-07-25): (1) Removed the standalone "استخدام لون الثيم" reset button from the level color editor -- now redundant with the just-shipped identity-color swatches ("لا حاجة ل استخدام لون الثيم فاحذفها"); the custom picker and identity swatches remain the only two ways to set a level's color, with `color=NULL` still the schema default for a never-touched level (unreachable from the UI now, but harmless -- new levels simply start unset). (2) **Fixed a real structural bug in the org chart, not just a cosmetic one**: the previous nested-`<ul>`/`<li>` pure-CSS connector technique (built 2026-07-24, "fixed" for missing lines in migration 20260725000004-era work) colored and POSITIONED every node by tree DEPTH, not its actual `org_structure_levels` row -- broken specifically because a position can link to ANY ancestor level, not just the one immediately above (20260724000001's skip-level relaxation), so two siblings at genuinely different real levels rendered on the identical row with a connector line that couldn't represent the gap between them at all ("هناك مشكلة في الخطوط العلائقية... اذا اختلف المستوى فاجعل مستوى الارتفاع يختلف"). `OrgChartTree.tsx` was rewritten from a server-renderable nested list into a `"use client"` component: positions are grouped into one flex row PER REAL LEVEL (levels with zero positions are skipped entirely, not rendered as blank space, since nothing can reference an empty level as a parent anyway), and connector lines are now drawn via an SVG overlay whose `<path>` coordinates are measured live from each node's actual DOM position (`getBoundingClientRect`, re-measured via `ResizeObserver` + a window resize listener) -- correctly spans however many real levels are skipped between a parent and child, rather than assuming a fixed one-row hop. Verified live against the real production hierarchy (CEO at level 1, "نائب الرئيس التنفيذي" at level C2, "مدير رأس المال البشري" at level C4, both direct children of the CEO): confirmed 3 separate rows (previously C2 and C4 would have shared one row) with 2 correctly measured SVG paths, one visibly longer than the other since C4's real level sits farther from the root than C2's. (3) **Add Level / Add Position converted from two permanently-visible full-width cards into compact `<dialog>`-modal triggers** ("نريدها بطريقة احترافية اكثر ولا تأخذ ارتفاع كبير من الصفحة"), reusing the exact pattern already established by `ImportOrgStructureExcelForm` -- both trigger buttons now sit in the page header's action row next to "استيراد من إكسل" instead of a separate section consuming permanent page height; the unused `defaultLevelId`/`headingKey` props on `AddOrgStructurePositionForm` (already dead -- confirmed via grep that no caller ever passed them) were dropped in the same rewrite rather than carried forward. (4) **New dedicated `reports` process area** (16th value, migration `20260725000006`, same `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE` pattern as every prior process-area addition) closing the explicit "اضافة تاب التقارير ... داخل موديول الادارة ويضاف الى صفحة الصلاحيات" follow-up -- the Reports tab moved from the "نتائج التقييم" group (now just "التوصيات" alone) into "الإدارة", and `/reports`'s own page body now has a real, explicit `reports>=view` gate (previously the page had NO permission check of its own at all, only an incidental nav-visibility filter reusing the general `evaluation` grant) -- **seeded with zero `role_permissions` rows on purpose**, per CLAUDE.md §4-B's "new roles inherit none by default" and the request's own framing ("عند الاتاحة للمشاهدة يطلع على الارقام" -- access is something to be deliberately granted through the already-built /admin role editor, not assumed). Verified live end-to-end with a real temporary custom test role (created and deleted for this test only, granted `orgStructure=approve`/`staffing=approve`/`identity=view`/`reports=view` initially): the group tabs correctly showed "الهيكل التنظيمي / التسكين / الهوية / التقارير" (no "الصلاحيات", since the test role held no `userManagement` grant) with the dashboard rendering its real numbers; after directly removing just the `reports` grant and reloading, "التقارير" disappeared from the tab bar entirely and the page body correctly showed "لا تملك صلاحية مشاهدة التقارير." instead of the dashboard -- proving the new gate is real, not decorative. The org-structure modal triggers and chart rewrite were verified together in the same pass: clicking "إضافة مستوى" opened only its own dialog (the Add-Position and Import dialogs stayed closed), a real temporary level (`TEMP_MODAL_TEST`) was created through the actual modal form and confirmed via the dev server's request log, and the identity-swatch color picker (shipped earlier the same day) was reconfirmed working with the reset button now genuinely absent. All temporary test data (auth user, profile, custom role, its `role_permissions` rows, `user_roles` row, the temp level) deleted afterward, confirmed at zero residual rows in every touched table; production's real 4 levels and their names/colors were reconfirmed unchanged throughout. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint` (scoped to every file this session touched -- zero errors/warnings; the repo-wide `eslint .` run picked up ~8000 pre-existing warnings from an unrelated `.github/skills/impeccable/` tooling directory belonging to a different session, not this codebase), and the full `vitest run` suite (246 tests, updated for the moved `reports` nav item and the 16th process area) all passed clean.
