- **Fixed a real follow-up bug in the just-rewritten org chart's connector lines, found live immediately after the level-based-rows rewrite shipped** (2026-07-25): a straight elbow connector between a parent and a grandchild-level position (a skip-level link, e.g. CEO -> "مدير رأس المال البشري" at level C4, skipping C2's row) visually ran right through whatever row sat in between -- since single-node rows all center on the same vertical axis, this made a genuine CEO->C4 direct report read as a chain (CEO->C2->C4), exactly the opposite of what the rewrite was supposed to clarify ("لكن مدير رأس المال البشري يتبع مباشرة الرئيس التنفيذي وليس نائب الرئيس... يجب ان يلتزم الخط العلائقي بالمستويات"). Fixed in `OrgChartTree.tsx`'s `measure()`: connector paths are now classified by real row distance (`renderedRowIndexByLevelId`, built only from OCCUPIED levels so it matches what's actually drawn, not raw `level_order`) -- a gap of exactly 1 keeps the original straight right-angle elbow, while a gap greater than 1 now bows the path out to one side and back via two quadratic Bézier bends (offset alternates side and widens per concurrent skip connector, so several skip-links sharing the same rows don't stack on each other) before returning to the child's x-position, visibly routing AROUND the intervening row instead of through its center. Verified live with a real temporary test role (`orgStructure=view`, chart-only access) against the real production hierarchy: confirmed via the SVG's own measured path data that the CEO->C2 (direct, adjacent-level) connector stayed a straight line at x=439 throughout, while the CEO->C4 (skip-level) connector now bows out to x=529 through its Bézier bends before returning to x=439 at the child -- a real, visually distinct detour, not a straight pass-through. All temporary test data (auth user, profile, custom role, its one `role_permissions` row) deleted afterward, confirmed zero residual rows; production's real 4 levels and their names/parents reconfirmed unchanged. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (246 tests, unchanged -- this is a pure rendering-logic fix inside an already-`"use client"` component with no new pure functions to unit test) all passed clean.

- **Replaced the org chart's bypass-curve fix with a real tree-layout grid, after the project owner still found the bypass ambiguous** (2026-07-25): the just-merged bypass curve (bowing a skip-level connector out to the side, PR #77) technically drew a distinct path, but the project owner correctly pointed out it STILL read as connected -- "لم تعجبني العلاقة هنا فمدير رأس المال البشري يرتبط مباشرة بالرئيس التنفيذي ولا له علاقة بنائب الرئيس فيجب ان يحكي الخط الرابط بطبيعة العلاقة" (the connector line itself must communicate the true nature of the relationship). Root cause the bypass curve didn't actually fix: two positions that are each the SOLE occupant of their own row still centered on the identical vertical axis by pure coincidence of per-row `justify-content:center`, so a bowed line still visually grazed past/near the intervening box instead of clearly diverging from it. Replaced with a proper fix: `computeSlots()` (new pure function in `OrgChartTree.tsx`) runs a classic tree-layout leaf-ordering pass over the whole positions forest -- each LEAF gets the next sequential horizontal "slot" in traversal order, and each internal node's slot is the average of its own children's slots -- then every position is placed via CSS `position:absolute; left: slot*200+100` (a real, deterministic horizontal grid, not flex centering), while `top` still comes from the already-correct per-real-level row index. Concretely for the real production hierarchy: CEO's two children (نائب الرئيس التنفيذي at level C2, مدير رأس المال البشري at level C4) are leaves with no shared subtree, so they get distinct slots (0 and 1) even though each is alone in its own row; CEO itself (their parent) gets slot 0.5 -- the average -- so it renders CENTERED BETWEEN its two children, exactly like a normal org chart's "two independent reports" visual convention. This made the bypass-curve logic entirely unnecessary: connector lines reverted to the original plain straight elbow (still measured live via `getBoundingClientRect`, so real box heights/text-wrapping stay accurate), since genuinely unrelated positions can no longer coincidentally share a vertical axis. Verified live end-to-end with a real temporary `orgStructure=view` test role, run from an isolated git worktree (`C:/SRU/wt-orgchart-slots`, its own `fix/org-chart-slot-layout` branch and a real `npm ci` install, port 3200) to avoid clobbering another concurrent session's checkout of this same repo directory, discovered mid-session when a routine `git commit` landed on an unexpected stale local branch (`feature/employees`, harmless -- it tracked no remote and sat at the same commit as `main`, just renamed and pushed properly afterward as `fix/org-chart-skip-level-connector-bypass`, PR #77): confirmed via the rendered DOM that CEO sits at `left:200px`, its two children at `left:100px` (نائب الرئيس) and `left:300px` (مدير رأس المال البشري) respectively, and the two SVG connector paths branch in opposite horizontal directions from CEO's own position (one moving to x=339, the other to x=539) -- unambiguous even to visual inspection, not just geometrically distinct. All temporary test data (auth user, profile, custom role, its one `role_permissions` row) deleted afterward, confirmed zero residual rows; production's real 4 levels/3 positions reconfirmed unchanged throughout. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (129 tests in this clean worktree, no duplicate count from another session's nested worktree) all passed clean.

- **Reports rebuilt as a personalized, per-user dashboard, replacing the single blanket gate from earlier the same day** (2026-07-25), per the explicit follow-up request ("أضف عنوان على السايدبار اسمه التقارير ... بحيث تكون على شكل داشبورد واقترح علي محتوياتها على مستوى كل يوزر لوحده بحيث تكون مرتبطة بالصلاحيات المتاحة له" — add a Reports sidebar heading as a dashboard, and propose per-user content tied to each user's own permissions). A proposal (a table mapping each candidate card to the existing process area/level that should unlock it, plus an always-visible personal section) was presented to and explicitly approved by the project owner ("نعم، ابدأ ببناء الداشبورد على هذا الأساس") before any code was written.

- **Structural change**: Reports moved out of the "الإدارة" group's tab bar entirely and into the top-level, ungated `navItems` array (`src/components/layout/navItems.ts`) alongside Home -- reachable by every logged-in user regardless of role, since personalization now lives in which CARDS render, not whether the page loads. This made the same-day `reports` process area (migration `20260725000006`) genuinely dead: removed from `ProcessArea`/`processAreas`/`processAreaLabels`/`processAreaSections` in `vpra.ts` (and their tests) since nothing references it anymore -- the Postgres enum value itself can't be un-added (no real `role_permissions` rows ever referenced it, confirmed via a live query before removing), but the app no longer writes or reads it anywhere, so it won't appear in /admin's permission matrix or anywhere else going forward. `/reports/page.tsx` was rewritten around a `get_my_permissions()` snapshot and a small `has(area, level)` helper: an always-visible personal section (my current evaluation state via `evaluationStateLabels`, my goals count, my BAU tasks count -- each filtered to `employee_id = my own profile id` explicitly, same discipline as `/evaluations/mine`/`/profile`, not left to each table's broader RLS branches) plus seven independently-gated cards reusing the exact tables/logic already built elsewhere in this app: evaluation completion rate + lagging departments (`evaluation>=view`, the original 2026-07-24 metric), calibration session count (`calibration>=view`, new), recommendations breakdown by type (`promotions>=view`, the original table), open vacancies (`vacancies>=view`, original), total employee headcount (`employeeData>=view`, new), org-structure staffing coverage -- staffed vs. total positions, reusing the same distinct-position-id counting approach as `OrgChartTree`'s vacant/filled logic (`orgStructure>=view` OR `staffing>=view`, new), and users grouped by role with real distinct-user counts mirroring `/admin`'s own counting discipline (`userManagement>=view`, new). Each gated section's DATA QUERY is skipped entirely (not just hidden in the UI) when the caller's permissions don't unlock it, avoiding a wasted RLS-denied round trip. The "نسبة تحقيق الأهداف الاستراتيجية غير متاحة" disclaimer from the original build is preserved, now nested specifically under the evaluation card's section rather than always shown at the page bottom. Verified live end-to-end with TWO real temporary custom test roles (created and deleted for this test only) to prove the personalization is real, not just present in the code: a zero-permission "minimal" role saw ONLY the personal section (correctly showing "لا يوجد تقييم لك بعد" / 0 goals / 0 tasks, with the entire "مؤشرات عامة" heading and every gated card completely absent, not just empty) and the sidebar showed exactly two links ("الرئيسية", "التقارير") confirming Reports' new ungated top-level placement; a broad role (`evaluation`/`calibration`/`promotions`/`vacancies`/`employeeData`/`orgStructure`/`userManagement` all at `view`) saw every card populated with real production numbers -- 6 total employees (the 4 real profiles plus the 2 temporary test ones), 3/3 org-structure staffing coverage (matching the real CEO/Deputy-CEO/Human-Capital hierarchy, all three genuinely staffed), and a real per-role breakdown table listing every actual system role plus both temporary test roles at count 1 each. All temporary test data (2 auth users, 2 profiles, 2 custom roles, 7 `role_permissions` rows) deleted afterward, confirmed production back to exactly 4 employees and zero residual test roles. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (129 tests in this clean isolated worktree, updated for the 15-process-area count and the top-level nav item move) all passed clean.

- **Direct (no-invite) account creation with a forced first-login password change, multi-role assignment, and userManagement-gated Add/Import on the Employees screen** (2026-07-25, migration `20260725000007_must_change_password_and_supervisor_profile_visibility.sql`), from three bundled requests in one round of feedback. (1) "التسجيل بنظام الدعوة فقط، اسمح لمن لديه صلاحية المستخدمين انشاء حساب موظف بدون دعوة ... وفي حالة الدخول من قبل صاحب الحساب يطلب منه ادخال رقم سري جديد بدون الرجوع للبريد الالكتروني": `employees/new/actions.ts`'s `inviteEmployee` gained a `mode: 'invite' | 'direct'` branch -- 'invite' is unchanged (Supabase email invite); 'direct' calls `admin.auth.admin.createUser({email, password, email_confirm:true})` instead, no email at all, with the admin-typed-or-suggested password from a new form field (a client-side `crypto.getRandomValues`-based "اقترح كلمة مرور" button, never sent anywhere until the real submit). `link_profile_to_auth_user()` (20260716000009) fires on ANY `auth.users` INSERT regardless of how it was created, so the direct path reuses the exact same profile-linking and pending-role-promotion mechanism as invite with zero changes there. Added `profiles.must_change_password` (new migration), set `true` right after a direct-mode creation; `login/actions.ts` checks it after a successful `signInWithPassword` and redirects to a new `/change-password` page (sibling to `/login`, outside `(app)/`) instead of home; `(app)/layout.tsx` ALSO re-checks it on every request so the redirect can't be bypassed by navigating straight into the app with an existing session. `/change-password` (`ChangePasswordForm.tsx`) needs no hash-token parsing unlike `/reset-password` -- the caller already has a real session from having just signed in -- so it calls `supabase.auth.updateUser({password})` directly, then a new `clear_must_change_password()` SECURITY DEFINER RPC (self-row only, no parameters, same narrow pattern as `get_my_role_codes()`/`get_my_supervisor()`/`get_my_permissions()` -- needed because `profiles_update`'s RLS requires `employeeData=prepare`, which a user forced to change their own password very likely doesn't hold). (2) "قد يكون له أكثر من دور مثل مدير الموارد البشرية ومدير الجدارات ... فاسمح بتسجيل اكثر من دور للموظف": `user_roles`/`pending_role_assignments` already allowed several rows per user (no unique constraint blocks it, only per-scope duplicates) -- this was purely a single-`<select>` UI limitation in two places. `inviteEmployee`'s `roleId: uuid` became `roleIds: uuid[]` (min 1), building one pending row per role (x per org unit, for `scope_type='org_unit'`); `EmployeeInviteForm.tsx`'s role `<select>` became checkboxes. `admin/roles/actions.ts`'s `assignUserRole` similarly took a single optional `roleId` to a required `roleIds: string[]`, delete-then-bulk-insert (not a diff -- the whole global-scope set is replaced every save, simpler than `updateRole`'s per-area diffing and cheap at this row count); `UserRoleAssignRow.tsx` became a checkbox dropdown (a plain `<select multiple>` needs ctrl/cmd-click to pick more than one, not discoverable in a table row) prefilled from ALL current global role ids, not just the first found; `admin/page.tsx` now builds `Map<string, string[]>` instead of "first row wins". (3) "لمن ليس لديهم صلاحية على المستخدمين لا يظهر لهم الأيقونات الاضافة والاستيراد فقط التصدير وشريط البحث ... يظهر لهم فقط الموظفين التابعين لهم وللادارات او الاقسام المرتبطة بهم وان نزلت": ties Add/Assign-supervisor/Import visibility on `/employees` to `userManagement>=approve` specifically (not `employeeData`) -- confirmed this isn't a fresh invention but matches real existing RLS: `pending_role_assignments`/`user_roles`' own INSERT policies already require `userManagement='approve'` (20260721000001/20260716000006), so a caller with only `employeeData` was already silently failing role assignment on this screen before this change, just with the buttons still visible. `/employees/new` gained its own explicit `check_vpra('userManagement','approve')` page-level gate (mirroring the button-hiding, so a direct URL visit is blocked server-side too, not just hidden) and `inviteEmployee` itself now checks it explicitly before any write, since the Admin Auth API calls run through the service-role client and bypass RLS entirely -- without this check, an `employeeData`-only caller could still create a real login even though they could never assign it a role. `assign-supervisor`'s own button is hidden by the same literal instruction but its underlying RLS stayed `employeeData`-based (unrelated capability, not touched, per PROJECT_STRICT.md's patch-not-rewrite rule). Row visibility itself needed no query-level filtering -- `profiles_select`'s RLS gained a third branch, `OR is_my_direct_report(id)` (reusing the function already built for evaluations/goals/bau_tasks/evaluation_scores, 20260718000009, but never extended to `profiles` itself until now), so a supervisor with zero `employeeData` grant now correctly sees their own direct reports on this list purely via RLS; org-unit cascading ("وان نزلت بحيث يكون لكل ادارة او قسم خاص به") needed no change at all -- `check_vpra`'s existing `is_org_unit_in_scope()` walk already handles it.

- **Real, documented consequence, not silently patched**: `hr_admin` currently holds NO `userManagement` grant at all (removed the same day, 20260725000002, "الغاء تاب الصلاحيات والهوية من hr_admin") -- only `super_admin` holds `userManagement='approve'` today (`ceo`/`cxo` hold `view`, insufficient), so with this change `hr_admin` loses the Add/Import/Assign-supervisor buttons on `/employees` entirely, on top of the role-assignment failure it was already silently hitting. Flagged directly to the project owner rather than assumed away; re-granting `hr_admin` some `userManagement` level (if the day-to-day onboarding workflow needs it back) is their call via the already-built `/admin` role editor, not a code change. Verified end-to-end against the real database with a Node script exercising the exact RLS/RPC/trigger paths the UI calls (17/17 checks passed: `check_vpra` on `userManagement`, direct-mode `profiles`+`auth.users` creation, 2-role `pending_role_assignments` insert promoting to 2 real `user_roles` rows via the trigger, `must_change_password` true-then-false across a real sign-in + `clear_must_change_password()` call, `anon` correctly denied that RPC, a plain-`employee`-role supervisor seeing their real direct report but not an unrelated employee, plain `employee` correctly failing the `userManagement` check), then again live in the real browser end-to-end: a temporary `super_admin` test account filled and submitted the actual `/employees/new` form in direct mode with 2 roles checked (موظف + مدير الموارد البشرية) -- real success message, redirect-free; signed in as that new employee with the temporary password and was genuinely redirected to `/change-password` (not simulated), set a new password, landed on the home app shell with full nav; `/admin`'s Users tab correctly showed "موظف، مدير الموارد البشرية" as the saved summary with both (and only both) checkboxes pre-checked when reopened. One false alarm during browser testing, not a real bug: an HMR rebuild from a concurrent session (this repo has multiple parallel Claude sessions working on it, per the note below) mid-interaction left a radio's DOM `checked` attribute out of sync with React state, making the password field appear not to render; a hard page reload immediately resolved it and the clean-mount retest passed. All temporary test data (5 auth users/profiles across both the API and browser test passes) deleted afterward, confirmed at zero residual rows. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint` (scoped to every touched file), and the full `vitest run` suite (246 tests, unchanged -- no new application-level TypeScript logic needed dedicated test coverage; the invariants live in RLS/triggers, verified live against the real database instead) all passed clean. New "أنشطة المستخدمين" (User Activity) admin tab, plus a real login-tracking gap found and fixed alongside it** (2026-07-25), per the explicit request ("اضف تاب في موديول الادارة عنوانه انشطة المستخدمين بحيث يبين عدد المستخدمين الحاليين واحصائيات عن استخدام المستخدمين خلال يوم او اسبوع او اي مدة يطلبها الادمن"). Before writing any code, investigated what usage data actually exists (grepped the whole codebase for any login-event logging) and found a real, previously undiscovered gap: **nothing in this codebase had ever written a `login` row to `audit_log`, despite CLAUDE.md §5-A rule 5.1 explicitly requiring it since the project's very first security checklist** -- `login/actions.ts` only ever ran rate-limiting and `signInWithPassword`, never an audit write. This finding, plus the fact that Supabase natively tracks `auth.users.last_sign_in_at` for every account regardless of this app's own logging (genuinely retroactive, real historical data), was presented to the project owner as two distinct, combinable data sources before building anything ("واحد سابق حقيقي رجعي (last_sign_in_at) وواحد جديد يبدأ من الصفر (تسجيل أحداث الدخول)") -- approved with "نعم، ابدأ بالبناء على هذا الأساس".

- **Fix**: `login/actions.ts` now writes a real `audit_log` row (`action='login'`, `entity='auth'`) via the admin/service-role client on every successful sign-in (a failed audit write deliberately never blocks a legitimate login, matching the established fire-and-forget discipline for non-critical audit writes elsewhere in this app).

- **New page**: `src/app/[locale]/(app)/admin/user-activity/page.tsx`, a 5th tab in the "الإدارة" group -- deliberately gated at `userManagement>=approve` (not `view`, unlike this group's other four tabs), since per-user login timestamps are meaningfully more sensitive than the rest of this group's content, a tier confirmed as part of the same proposal. Shows: total linked-account count, "active in period" computed from real `last_sign_in_at` values (works correctly for all pre-existing accounts, not just ones that log in after this ships), a zero-JS day/week/custom-range `<form method="get">` picker (same established pattern as the Employees list's own filter form), real login-EVENT counts from `audit_log` for the selected period (explicitly and honestly flagged in the UI as starting from zero, since no history existed before this feature), and a full per-account table of last-sign-in timestamps.

- **Found and fixed a real bug during live verification, not assumed correct**: the linked-profiles query initially used the caller's own RLS-respecting client, which silently scoped the "current users" count to whatever `employeeData` visibility the viewer happened to hold -- a completely unrelated permission from the `userManagement>=approve` gate already enforced in application code above it. Caught live with a real temporary test role holding ONLY `userManagement=approve` (deliberately no `employeeData`): the count showed `1` (just that role's own self-visible row) instead of the true system-wide total. Fixed by switching that query to the same service-role client already used for `auth.users`/`audit_log` reads on this page -- the `canView` VPRA check already gates the whole page in application code, so this doesn't weaken any real boundary, it just makes the count consistently system-wide as the feature's own name implies. Verified live end-to-end with two real temporary custom test roles: an `approve`-level account, after a REAL login through the actual `/login` form, produced a real `audit_log` row (confirmed via direct query) and the page correctly showed 6 total linked accounts (4 real + 2 temporary), 5/6 active in the default week window (genuinely reflecting each real account's true historical `last_sign_in_at`, including dates from 2026-07-22 through 2026-07-25), exactly 1 login event in that window (the one just-triggered real login, correctly NOT counting any of the other accounts' pre-existing logins, since event-based tracking only started today), and a custom single-day range (`2026-07-24` to `2026-07-24`) correctly isolated to just the one account whose real last-sign-in fell on that exact date; a separate `view`-level test account correctly lost the tab from its own tab bar entirely and got the real "لا تملك صلاحية مشاهدة أنشطة المستخدمين" message when navigating to the URL directly, proving the server-side gate is real, not merely a hidden UI element. All temporary test data (2 auth users, 2 profiles, 2 custom roles, their `role_permissions` rows, and the 2 test `audit_log` login rows) deleted afterward, confirmed production back to exactly 4 employees and zero `login`-action `audit_log` rows (the real feature had not yet been used by a real account at verification time).

- **Separately, while investigating this feature, discovered and flagged (not fixed here, out of scope) a pre-existing crash bug in `/reports/page.tsx`**: a stray/prefetch request to `/reports` from an unauthenticated context crashed on `user!.id`'s non-null assertion instead of failing gracefully -- spun off as its own background task rather than folded into this unrelated PR, per PROJECT_STRICT.md's patch-not-rewrite scope discipline. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (129 tests, unchanged -- no new pure functions needed unit coverage beyond what the page's own inline `resolvePeriod` helper already gets exercised by live verification) all passed clean.

- **Employee-creation approval workflow, recursive subordinate-chain visibility, username login, and a redesigned edit form** (2026-07-25, migrations 20260725000008-20260725000010), from one large bundled round of feedback with a screenshot of the /employees header buttons.

- **Split `employeeData` into two independently-grantable areas** (`employeeDataSubordinates` added as the 16th process area, two-step ALTER-TYPE-then-use pattern): `employeeData` itself is completely unchanged ("بيانات جميع الموظفين تظهر فقط لمن لديه صلاحيات ... كما هي عليه الان" -- explicit confirmation, not touched), while the new area gates a genuinely recursive supervisor-chain check. `is_my_subordinate()` (a new SECURITY DEFINER function, depth-capped at 20 as a defensive guard against a corrupted/cyclical supervisor_id chain) walks the full ancestor chain via a recursive CTE -- unlike `is_my_direct_report()` (one level only), this answers "is target anywhere under me", e.g. a manager seeing a department head's own staff two levels down.

- **This REPLACES the ungated free supervisor-sees-direct-report bypass shipped in the previous PR** (20260725000007) with a gated one requiring `employeeDataSubordinates>=view` -- a deliberate behavior change per CLAUDE.md 4-B's least-privilege-by-default rule (no role_permissions rows seeded for the new area), flagged clearly since it means supervisor/manager/field_supervisor lose that free visibility until explicitly granted it via /admin.

- **Employee-creation approval workflow** ("لا يضاف للقائمة الا بعد الاعتماد ممن لديه الاعتماد"): new `profiles.approval_status` enum (pending/approved/rejected, existing rows backfilled to approved since they're already-established employees, new inserts default to pending) and `profiles.created_by` (tracks the preparer, added in the same transaction as its own RLS branch since a plain column -- unlike an enum value -- can be used immediately). `/employees/new`'s page-level gate LOOSENED from userManagement>=approve to nothing beyond RLS itself -- entering employee DATA now only needs employeeData>=prepare (profiles_insert's own existing RLS), while creating an actual LOGIN account (mode='invite'|'direct', now alongside a third 'none' data-only option) stays userManagement-gated exactly as before, checked explicitly in the Server Action since the Admin Auth API bypasses RLS entirely. `inviteEmployee` sets approval_status='approved' immediately when the preparer already holds employeeData='approve' for the target org unit (no redundant self-approval), else 'pending'. New /employees sections: a "بانتظار الاعتماد" queue (approve-level holders only, new `reviewEmployeeApproval` action gated explicitly at employeeData='approve' since profiles_update's own RLS is only 'prepare') and a "طلباتي" panel (any preparer sees their own pending/rejected submissions via the new created_by RLS branch, regardless of any other grant).

- **The approved list is now grouped into one section per org unit for everyone** ("تكون مقسمة على شكل سكاشن لكل قسم او ادارة"), sections sorted alphabetically (Arabic locale-aware), with an explicit sort-by picker (name/employee number) inside the existing filter panel ("يمكن الفرز على الحروف الابجدية").

- **Username + optional email** ("اضف اسم المستخدم واجعله خيارا عند الدخول ... اجعل البريد الالكتروني اختياريا"): `profiles.username` (new, unique, nullable) and `profiles.email` dropped its NOT NULL -- Supabase Auth itself has no native username-only sign-in, so `resolve_login_identifier()` (SECURITY DEFINER, granted to anon since login runs pre-authentication) resolves a typed username to the real email first, falling through to the exact same generic "invalid_credentials" response on no match that a wrong password already gives, revealing nothing new. LoginForm's single field replaces the old email-only input; when an account is created without a real email, a synthetic `{username}@no-email.internal` address is stored in profiles.email so link_profile_to_auth_user()'s existing email-match trigger keeps working unchanged.

- **EditEmployeeForm restyled to match EmployeeInviteForm's sectioned layout** ("بنفس تنسيق نموذج اضافة موظف", was a flat unsectioned form until now) and gained its own Role & Permissions section -- reuses `UserRoleAssignRow` verbatim rather than duplicating its multi-role-checkbox-plus-save logic, gated at the same userManagement>=approve bar as the add form's account section.

- **Export column picker** ("شاشة على شكل checkboxes لتحديد الخانات ... من كامل النموذج وليس المعروض فقط"): a new shared `src/lib/employeeExportColumns.ts` defines 21 exportable fields (every add/edit-form field, not just the 6 the table shows); `EmployeesExportMenu` opens a checkbox dialog (same native <dialog> pattern as the Excel import modal) before hitting the Excel/CSV Route Handler, which now accepts a `columns` param and defaults to the original 6 when absent (existing bookmarked export links unaffected).

- **Found and corrected one wrong test assumption while verifying, not a code bug**: the first verification pass assumed a plain employee-role preparer could submit employee data and got a real RLS rejection -- checking the actual seeded `role_permissions` matrix directly confirmed `employee` holds no row at all for `employeeData` (defaults to none), so **no default seeded role currently holds prepare/recommend on employeeData** -- only ceo/hr_admin/super_admin (all approve) can add employees at all today, meaning the new pending-approval tier is real and correctly wired but dormant until the project owner grants some role a lower employeeData level via /admin; a temporary custom role with employeeData=prepare confirmed the mechanism genuinely works end-to-end (insert succeeds, stays pending, self-visible via created_by) once a role actually holds it. Verified end-to-end against the real database: `resolve_login_identifier()` (username-to-email, email passthrough, null-on-unknown all confirmed), a real 2-level supervisor chain (manager -> department head -> staffer) correctly invisible to the manager before granting `employeeDataSubordinates`, correctly visible after, correctly still invisible to an unrelated employee holding the identical grant, and 1-level direct-report visibility reconfirmed working through the same new recursive function; then live in the real browser: logged in with a **username instead of an email** for the first time ever in this app, confirmed the /employees list renders real per-department sections plus a real pending-approval entry (the test account's own fixture, created without explicit approval), clicked the real Approve button and confirmed the database flip from pending to approved, submitted the add-employee form in the new 'none' (data-only) mode with the role section correctly absent, confirmed auto-approval and correct `created_by`, and confirmed the edit form's four sections (including the new Role & Permissions section, correctly prefilled) render exactly like the add form. All temporary test data (auth users, profiles, a temporary custom role, one temporary role_permissions grant) deleted afterward, confirmed at zero residual rows in every touched table; the two real production employees were reconfirmed unaffected throughout. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint` (scoped to every touched file, zero errors/warnings), and the full `vitest run` suite (129 tests -- this branch does not yet include the separately-tracked User Activity tab additions from a concurrent PR) all passed clean.

- **Users tab on /admin gained search + pagination** (2026-07-25), per direct feedback with a screenshot: "لو اضفنا الموظفين في هذا القسم طالت القائمة ولا كان هناك فرق بينها وبين صفحة الموظفين" (as more employees are added this table will grow long, with no real difference from /employees) plus an explicit "اضف خاصية البحث" (add search). Extracted the inline Users table into a new client component, `src/components/AdminUsersTable.tsx`, taking the already-fetched employee+role data as props (no new query) -- a text search box (matches name OR employee number, substring, client-side over the already-loaded roster) resets to page 1 on every keystroke, and results paginate at 10 rows/page with a "عرض X-Y من Z" / prev-next control, mirroring the Employees list's own established in-memory-filter convention (2026-07-24) rather than inventing a new pattern. This is the actual answer to the redundancy complaint, not just a cosmetic fix: pagination is the real differentiator from /employees (the full browsable directory) -- this tab only ever shows a bounded slice by default, framing it as a targeted "find someone, (re)assign their role" tool rather than a second unbounded roster. `UserRoleAssignRow` itself was untouched (still handles its own save/refresh); confirmed live that `router.refresh()` after a role save does NOT reset the component's search/page state, since React preserves a client component's local state across a server re-render as long as its position in the tree is stable -- a real behavior worth confirming, not assumed, since a jarring reset-to-page-1-on-every-save would have undermined the whole feature. Verified live end-to-end with a real temporary `super_admin` test account plus 12 temporary filler employee profiles (17 total rows): default view showed page 1 of 2 ("عرض 1-10 من 17"); searching "عزام" correctly narrowed to the exact one matching row and hid the now-unnecessary pagination controls; searching the employee number "90001" matched by number, not just name; a deliberately non-matching query correctly rendered the "لا توجد نتائج مطابقة" empty state instead of an empty table; clicking "التالي" correctly advanced to page 2 ("عرض 11-17 من 17", includes the real production `super_admin` account and the temporary test account); assigning a role to a page-2 filler employee saved correctly (Positions list's super_admin count went 2 to 3) and the view stayed on page 2 afterward, not jumping back to page 1. All temporary test data (1 auth user, 13 profiles, its user_roles row) deleted afterward, confirmed zero residual rows in every touched table; production's real employees/roles reconfirmed unchanged throughout. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (129 tests, unchanged -- no new pure function needed dedicated unit coverage beyond the live-verified in-component filter/paginate logic) all passed clean.

- **"إعدادات النظام" (System Settings) tab + display-timezone fix** (2026-07-26, migrations `20260726000003_system_settings_process_area.sql` + `20260726000004_system_settings_table.sql`), per direct feedback with a screenshot: the "أنشطة المستخدمين" tab's last-sign-in column was rendering in the server's own timezone (UK) instead of Saudi Arabia time -- rather than hardcoding a fix, the project owner explicitly asked for a new "إعدادات النظام" tab under الإدارة where the timezone is a free, configurable choice. Added `systemSettings` as the 17th process_area value (same two-step ALTER-TYPE-then-use pattern as every prior addition) and a new `system_settings` singleton table -- same shape as `org_identity` (a unique index on a constant expression enforces at most one row, seeded with exactly one row by the migration itself: `timezone='Asia/Riyadh'`). RLS mirrors `org_identity` exactly (`check_vpra_global('systemSettings','view'/'approve')`), seeded super_admin-only (`approve`) -- the same narrow gate as `identity`, and for the same accepted trade-off already established there: a caller without `view` gets zero rows back from RLS and the application code falls back to a hardcoded default, which here happens to already be the organizationally-correct value ('Asia/Riyadh'), so in practice every user sees correct times unless/until super_admin explicitly picks something else (in which case only super_admin's own view reflects the change). Broadening this to every role (seeding 12 `role_permissions` rows) was considered and rejected -- the project owner's own framing put this under a super_admin-tier "system settings" tab, not a broadly-held reference-data grant. New `src/lib/systemSettings.ts` (`getDisplayTimezone(supabase)`) centralizes the read-with-fallback so both consumers share one implementation. `/admin/settings` (page + actions.ts + `SystemSettingsForm.tsx`) follows the identical view/edit-gate and dirty-state-Save pattern already established for `/admin/identity` and `EditRoleForm` -- the timezone `<select>` is populated from `Intl.supportedValuesOf("timeZone")` (418 real IANA zones on this runtime), not a hand-curated list, so no valid zone is silently unavailable.

- **Fixed the actual reported bug and one more instance of the identical bug found by grepping for every other `toLocaleString` call rendering a date** (not just the one screenshotted): `/admin/user-activity`'s last-sign-in column and `/promotions/history`'s decision-date column both now pass `{ timeZone: displayTimezone }` instead of defaulting to the server's own zone; `/salary-scale`'s own `toLocaleString` call was confirmed to be pure number formatting (Arabic-Indic digits), not a date, and correctly left untouched. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with a real temporary `super_admin` test account: `/admin/settings` rendered the full timezone list with `Asia/Riyadh` pre-selected and Save correctly disabled (no unsaved change); `/admin/user-activity`'s real last-sign-in timestamps (e.g. a real account's `١١:١٦:٣٥ ص` from the original bug screenshot) now showed `٢:١٦:٣٥ م` -- exactly the +3 Riyadh offset from the previous UK-timezone rendering; changed the setting to `America/New_York`, saved (Save correctly re-disabled, success message shown), and confirmed the SAME account's timestamp shifted by exactly the real Riyadh-to-New-York offset (7 hours in July's DST), proving the setting genuinely propagates rather than being decorative; reverted to `Asia/Riyadh` and confirmed the real production row reflects the correct default afterward. A separate real temporary `employee`-role test account correctly saw no "الإدارة" group at all in the sidebar and got the real "لا تملك صلاحية مشاهدة إعدادات النظام" forbidden message on a direct `/admin/settings` visit, confirming the gate is a real server-side boundary, not just a hidden nav link. All temporary test data (2 auth users, 2 profiles, their `user_roles` rows) deleted afterward, confirmed zero residual rows in every touched table; production's `system_settings` singleton reconfirmed at exactly one row, `timezone='Asia/Riyadh'`, throughout. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (135 tests -- 1 new process-area count assertion, 1 new administration-group-children segment) all passed clean.

- **Linked `org_structure_positions` to `org_units`, closing the gap between employees' own department and the org-chart tree** (2026-07-26, migration `20260726000005_org_structure_position_org_unit_link.sql`), per direct feedback: "بالنسبة للموظفين لا يوجد ربط بين الوحدة التنظيمية التابعين لها والهيكل التنظيمي لذلك لا نجد الموظفين التابعين لمدير ادارة معينة" (for employees, there's no link between their org unit and the org structure, so we can't find the employees belonging to a specific department manager). `org_units` (58-unit college/department tree, drives `profiles.org_unit_id` and the whole VPRA org-unit-scope model) and `org_structure_positions` (the newer org-chart tree, staffed via `org_structure_assignments`) had been two completely independent models since the org-structure feature was first built (2026-07-22) -- confirmed directly with the project owner rather than guessed which direction the link should go and whether it should be automatic: "اجعله position إلى org unit، اختياري لكل منصب بدون مطابقة تلقائية" (position to org unit, optional per position, no automatic matching). Added a single nullable `org_unit_id uuid REFERENCES org_units(id) ON DELETE SET NULL` column to `org_structure_positions` -- no uniqueness constraint, since several leadership positions could plausibly share one department and the project owner didn't state one. `addPosition`/`updatePosition` (`src/app/[locale]/(app)/admin/org-structure/actions.ts`) both gained an `orgUnitId` parameter (optional on create, `string | null` on update -- an edit form always resends the current selection, mirroring `updateLevel`'s existing null-vs-undefined `color` convention exactly). Both position edit surfaces (`OrgStructurePositionMiniRow` on the builder page, `OrgStructurePositionRow` on the staffing page) and the "Add Position" modal (`AddOrgStructurePositionForm`) gained an org-unit `<select>` (a "بلا ربط" / no-link option first, then all 58 real units alphabetically) folded into each component's existing dirty-state-Save tracking.

- **The actual answer to the reported problem lives on the staffing page**: a new "موظفو الوحدة التنظيمية المرتبطة" (Employees in Linked Org Unit) column lists every real employee whose OWN `profiles.org_unit_id` matches the position's linked unit -- computed once in `staffing/page.tsx` via a `Map<orgUnitId, label[]>` built from the already-fetched employee roster, deliberately distinct from the existing "الموظفون المسكَّنون" (Assigned Employees) column, which only shows whoever is individually staffed onto that exact position node via `org_structure_assignments`. No RLS change was needed for the new org-unit `<select>` options -- `org_units_select` already grants `employeeData`-view holders read access (20260719000008), and `hr_admin`/`super_admin` (the only roles that ever reach this page's `canBuild`/write gates) already hold `employeeData` grants. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with a real temporary `super_admin` test account: created a real temporary employee profile with `org_unit_id` set to a real existing unit ("مجلس الأمناء"), linked the real "الرئيس التنفيذي" position to that same unit through the actual staffing-page `<select>` + Save (confirmed persisted via a direct database query), and confirmed the new column immediately showed BOTH the position's pre-existing assigned employee and the new temp employee side by side -- proving the two columns are genuinely independent data sources, not a rename. Reverified the same link showing correctly pre-selected on the builder page's mini-row (`OrgStructurePositionMiniRow`), then cleared the link back to null through the real UI and confirmed via direct query. All temporary test data (1 auth user, 2 profiles, its `user_roles` row) deleted afterward, confirmed zero residual rows; the real production position/org-unit link reconfirmed back to `NULL` throughout. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (135 tests, unchanged -- this is a pure additive schema+UI change with no new pure function needing dedicated unit coverage beyond what the live-verified select/dirty-tracking logic already exercises) all passed clean.

- **Linked the org chart's real positions to their org units** (2026-07-26, migration `20260726000006_link_main_positions_to_org_units.sql`), per direct request: "ابدأ بربط باقي المناصب الرئيسية بالوحدات التنظيمية" (start linking the remaining main positions to org units), following the position-to-org-unit link feature (20260726000005). Only 5 real positions existed in `org_structure_positions` at this point (الرئيس التنفيذي, نائب الرئيس التنفيذي, النائب المساعد للتميز الأكاديمي, مدير الادارة المالية, مدير رأس المال البشري). 3 were matched confidently and linked: النائب المساعد للتميز الأكاديمي (exact string match against a real org_units row), مدير الادارة المالية -> إدارة الشؤون المالية (same finance department, shorter informal position-title wording), مدير رأس المال البشري -> إدارة رأس المال البشري (Director-of-X position naturally maps to the Department-of-X org unit). 2 were deliberately NOT linked and flagged back to the project owner instead of guessed, per this project's no-fabricated-data discipline: الرئيس التنفيذي (CEO) has two plausible candidates in the real 58 org units -- رئيس الجامعة (University President) and مكتب الرئيس (Office of the President) -- and it's genuinely unclear whether the CEO title refers to the university presidency itself or a distinct executive office; نائب الرئيس التنفيذي (Deputy CEO) has no comparably-named org unit at all among the real 58. Applied directly against the real shared database (a data-linking operation via a small idempotent name-matched migration, not an application-code change) and verified via a direct query immediately after: exactly the 3 intended positions now carry a non-NULL `org_unit_id`, the other 2 remain NULL as intended. No `npx tsc`/`eslint`/`vitest` run for this PR -- zero application code was touched, only a migration file.

- **Fixed the org-unit-employees column to include sub-units recursively, not just an exact match** (2026-07-26/27), per direct feedback with a real scenario: "نائب الرئيس تحت إدارته موظفان أحدهما النائب المساعد للتميز الأكاديمي والآخر موظف تابع للوحدة التنظيمية مكتب النائب المساعد للتميز الأكاديمي ومع ذلك لا أرى الموظفون ظهرت عند نائب الرئيس" (Deputy CEO has two employees under him -- one holds النائب المساعد للتميز الأكاديمي, the other belongs to its office -- yet neither shows up under Deputy CEO). Investigated directly against the real database rather than guessing: the project owner had already used the position-org-unit-link feature (built the same day, 20260726000005) to link الرئيس التنفيذي -> رئيس الجامعة and نائب الرئيس التنفيذي -> نائب الرئيس للشؤون الأكاديمية through the real UI, resolving the two ambiguous cases flagged in the previous entry -- confirmed live that عبدالله الصالح (whose own org unit is exactly نائب الرئيس للشؤون الأكاديمية) DID already show correctly. The real gap: `org_units` has always had its own genuine `parent_id` hierarchy (the same tree `is_org_unit_in_scope()` already walks for VPRA org-unit scoping) -- and النائب المساعد للتميز الأكاديمي (the org unit, not the position) is a real CHILD of نائب الرئيس للشؤون الأكاديمية in that hierarchy. The two employees the project owner meant (بدر سالم and باسل عمر) both have `org_unit_id` = النائب المساعد للتميز الأكاديمي specifically -- a genuine sub-unit, not the exact linked unit -- so the previous day's exact-match-only lookup correctly excluded them by its own (now recognized as too narrow) definition. Fixed in `staffing/page.tsx`: `orgUnits` now also selects `parent_id`; a new `descendantOrgUnitIds()` BFS walk (cycle-guarded, matching the defensive style already used elsewhere in this codebase for tree walks) computes a position's linked unit plus every real descendant unit, and `orgUnitEmployeeLabels` is now built from the union of all of them instead of one exact id. Column header updated to "موظفو الوحدة التنظيمية المرتبطة ووحداتها الفرعية" (...and its sub-units) so the UI is honest about what it now shows. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with a real temporary super_admin account: نائب الرئيس التنفيذي's row now correctly lists all 3 real employees (عبدالله الصالح directly, plus بدر سالم and باسل عمر via the descendant unit) -- exactly the two the project owner said were missing, now present; confirmed the rollup is genuinely transitive by checking الرئيس التنفيذي too (linked to رئيس الجامعة, the root of that branch) -- it correctly shows all 6 real employees found anywhere in the whole descendant chain (رئيس الجامعة -> نائب الرئيس للشؤون الأكاديمية -> النائب المساعد للتميز الأكاديمي); confirmed a leaf position with no descendant units (النائب المساعد للتميز الأكاديمي itself) is unaffected, still showing exactly its own 2 direct employees as before. All temporary test data (1 auth user, 1 profile, its user_roles row) deleted afterward, confirmed zero residual rows; production's real position-org-unit links reconfirmed unchanged throughout (this was a pure read-side rendering fix, no data was written). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (135 tests, unchanged -- the fix is pure server-render logic with no new pure function needing dedicated unit coverage beyond what was already live-verified) all passed clean.

- **Applied the same sub-unit rollup fix to the visual org chart tree, which had been missed** (2026-07-27), per direct follow-up: "للاسف لم يظهر الموظفون عند نائب الرئيس" (unfortunately the employees still don't show at Deputy CEO), reported right after the previous fix (staffing table's org-unit-employees column) shipped. Investigated live against BOTH the real database and the actual deployed production site rather than assuming the fix was incomplete: confirmed the position-org-unit link data was correct, and confirmed the STAFFING TABLE (`/admin/org-structure/staffing`) already correctly showed all 3 real employees under نائب الرئيس التنفيذي (عبدالله الصالح directly, بدر سالم and باسل عمر via the descendant unit) -- the previous fix genuinely worked there. The real gap: the project owner was looking at the VISUAL ORG CHART (`/admin/org-structure`, `OrgChartTree.tsx`) instead, a completely separate component that was never touched by the previous fix -- it only ever built `assigneesByPosition` from `org_structure_assignments`, with no awareness of the org-unit link or its descendant rollup at all. Extracted the descendant-walk logic (previously written inline only in `staffing/page.tsx`) into a new shared `src/lib/orgUnitHierarchy.ts` (`buildDescendantOrgUnitIdsResolver`, unit-tested: root-with-no-children, transitive descendants, unrelated/ancestor exclusion, and a cyclical-parent_id guard) specifically so this exact kind of gap -- one fix landing in only one of two places that both needed it -- can't recur silently; `staffing/page.tsx` now calls the shared function instead of its own copy. `org-structure/page.tsx` (the chart's data source) now also fetches `profiles(id, full_name_ar, org_unit_id)` and merges two sources into `assigneesByPosition` per position: employees explicitly staffed via `org_structure_assignments` (unchanged) AND employees whose own org unit is the position's linked unit or any real descendant of it (new) -- deduplicated by profile id via an internal `Map`, since the same person can legitimately appear in both sources (e.g. explicitly assigned to a position AND also a member of its linked department). Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database and the actual deployed production site (not just localhost) with a real temporary super_admin account: confirmed via direct query that the position-org-unit links were exactly as the previous entry described; loaded the real production `/admin/org-structure/staffing` page and confirmed all 3 employees already rendered correctly there (proving the earlier fix itself was sound); loaded the real production visual org chart and confirmed it was still showing only 1 name for نائب الرئيس التنفيذي (reproducing the exact reported gap); after applying and hot-reloading this fix on a local dev server against the same real database, confirmed نائب الرئيس التنفيذي's chart node now shows all three names ("عبدالله الصالح، بدر سالم، باسل عمر") in a single comma-joined line, matching the chart's existing display convention exactly. All temporary test data (2 auth users/profiles accumulated across this session's diagnosis, one created twice by an accidental duplicate script run) deleted afterward, confirmed zero residual rows in every touched table. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (139 tests -- 4 new for `buildDescendantOrgUnitIdsResolver`) all passed clean.

- **Built out the full org-chart position tree beneath "الرئيس التنفيذي" (44 new positions), matching real org_units data end-to-end** (2026-07-27, migration `20260727000001_org_structure_full_hierarchy.sql`), per direct request to keep adding positions manually. Confirmed the exact shape with the project owner before writing anything, since this touches real production org-chart data at scale: "اتركها كما هي وابدأ بالبناء تحتها" (leave الرئيس التنفيذي as-is, build beneath it -- so the tree's top stays anchored where it already was, not re-rooted at رئيس الجامعة's real org_units ancestors مجلس الأمناء/مجلس الجامعة), then "أضف الإدارة التنفيذية كمناصب حقيقية" (add the three "الإدارة التنفيذية" / Executive Management units as real intermediate positions, not flattened away) -- explicitly choosing the option that moves the two already-existing positions "مدير رأس المال البشري"/"مدير الادارة المالية" one tier deeper, under the new "الإدارة التنفيذية للخدمات المشتركة" node, rather than leaving them as direct children of الرئيس التنفيذي. Source of truth: confirmed `org_units.parent_id` is a single-rooted, fully-connected 58-unit tree (no orphans) before relying on it -- every new position is named IDENTICALLY to its linked org unit (no titles invented), matching the precedent already set by "النائب المساعد للتميز الأكاديمي". Level assignment reused the already-established convention that level = organizational rank tier, not raw tree depth (the two existing positions already proved this: same real descendant relationship, different levels) -- computed here as depth RELATIVE TO الرئيس التنفيذي specifically (direct children -> C2, their children -> C3, deepest tier -> C4), which happened to exactly match the existing 4 levels with none needed to be added. Built with a Node script (not hand-written SQL) that fetched real `org_units`/`org_structure_positions`/`org_structure_levels`, BFS-walked the subtree rooted at "رئيس الجامعة" (excluding it, since الرئيس التنفيذي already represents it), reused the 4 already-existing linked positions in place wherever their org_unit appeared in that walk (rather than duplicating them), and pre-generated UUIDs so the emitted migration SQL is a flat, reviewable sequence of INSERTs/UPDATEs in topological (parent-before-child) order with no runtime CTE gymnastics. Result: 44 new positions (12 at C2, 17 at C3, 15 at C4) plus the 2 pre-existing positions re-parented to the new "الإدارة التنفيذية للخدمات المشتركة" node and re-leveled from C4 to C3 to match -- 49 real positions total (5 original + 44 new), spot-checked by hand against the real org_units tree dump before applying (every parent-child pair traced against the actual hierarchy, not just trusted from the script). Applied directly to the real shared production database (49 confirmed via count, both re-parented rows confirmed via direct query) and verified live end-to-end on the actual deployed production site with a real temporary super_admin account: the visual org chart correctly rendered all 49 nodes; a real, previously-orphaned data point surfaced correctly for the first time as a direct consequence of this work -- "هيثم الفهيد" (a real employee whose own `org_unit_id` has always been "إدارة الأمن السيبراني") now appears under the brand-new "إدارة الأمن السيبراني" position, and correctly rolls up further through "الإدارة التنفيذية للاتصالات وتقنية المعلومات" -- proving the whole chain (position-org-unit link -> descendant rollup -> chart/staffing render) works coherently end-to-end on real data, not just the 5 positions it was originally built and tested against. Cross-checked the staffing table against the chart for all 49 rows and confirmed byte-for-byte matching employee lists in every row. All temporary test data (1 auth user, 1 profile, its user_roles row) deleted afterward, confirmed zero residual rows.

- **Linked each of the 44 new org-chart positions to a real job title in `job_titles`** (2026-07-27, migrations `20260727000002_org_structure_position_job_title_link.sql` + `20260727000003_org_structure_position_job_titles_data.sql`), per direct request: "أضف مسمى وظيفي لكل منصب جديد في job_titles". Added a nullable `job_title_id` FK on `org_structure_positions` (same shape as the earlier `org_unit_id` link, 20260726000005). Before creating anything, read all 336 real `job_titles` rows (from the earlier HR Excel import) rather than assume none existed: 21 of the 44 positions already had a real, confidently-matched title from that import (e.g. "مدير الأمن السيبراني" for إدارة الأمن السيبراني, "الرئيس التنفيذي للخدمات المشتركة" for الإدارة التنفيذية للخدمات المشتركة) -- these were REUSED via `job_title_id`, not duplicated. 3 positions deliberately got no title at all: "عمداء الكليات"/"المجلس العلمي" are collegial bodies with no individual head role, and "المشرفة على القسم النسائي"'s own position name already IS a personal role title, not a department name needing a separate one. The remaining 20 had no existing match at all -- confirmed by reading the full table, including that **no "عميد" (Dean) title exists anywhere in the real data**. Confirmed the exact naming rule directly before inventing anything: "بالنسبة للمركز والمكتب يكون المسمى رئيس بدلا من مدير" (for مركز/مكتب-type units, use "رئيس" instead of "مدير") -- this matched a real pattern already visible in the existing 336 rows (رئيس مكتب القبول، رئيس مكتب البيانات، etc., consistently grade 12), which is why the rule was trustworthy to apply rather than arbitrary. New titles: "إدارة"/"الإدارة"/"وحدة" units keep "مدير" (grade 13, the majority real value for that pattern -- a few real exceptions run 14-16, not mechanically copied); "مركز"/"مكتب" units get "رئيس" (grade 12, matching the real pattern exactly); the 4 colleges get "عميد" (Dean, the unambiguous standard academic title) at grade 14 -- explicitly flagged as **[استنتاج]**, not separately confirmed beyond the رئيس/مدير rule itself, since Deans have no real precedent in this data to anchor a grade to. Each new title's `job_family_id` was matched to the closest existing real family by subject matter (e.g. "الهندسية" for مدير الإدارة الهندسية, "البحث العلمي" for both مكتب البحث العلمي and مكتب الدراسات العليا) rather than defaulted to the generic "عام" family, since a more specific real match existed in every single case. Applied directly to the real shared production database and verified via direct query: 356 total `job_titles` (336 + 20 new), exactly 41 of the 44 new positions carry a non-NULL `job_title_id`, and the 3 deliberately-blank ones match exactly the 3 identified as collegial/self-titled. No application code was touched -- purely a data-layer addition (schema link + data), so no UI currently displays the linked title anywhere; that's a natural, separate follow-up if wanted, not assumed here.

- **Job titles now render on the visual org chart, closing the "natural, separate follow-up" flagged at the end of the previous entry** (2026-07-27): "اعرض المسميات الوظيفية على شاشة الهيكل التنظيمي" (show the job titles on the org structure screen). `OrgChartTree.tsx` gained a `jobTitleByPosition: Record<string,string>` prop, rendered as a small subtitle line (a `Briefcase` icon + the title) between the position name and the existing assignee line -- omitted entirely, not a placeholder, for the 8 positions with no linked title. `org-structure/page.tsx` builds this map with one extra query (`job_titles` by the distinct `job_title_id`s already selected on `org_structure_positions`), gated by nothing new -- `job_titles_select`'s existing RLS (`careerPath>=view OR employeeData>=view`) already covers hr_admin/super_admin, the only roles that reach this page's `canBuild`/chart-view tiers. `ROW_HEIGHT` bumped 96px -> 112px to fit the extra line. Verified the exact join this PR adds against the real shared database with a script mirroring the page's own logic: 41 of 49 real positions resolve to a real title name (matching the previous entry's own count exactly), and the 8 without one break down as the 3 deliberately-titleless positions from that same earlier migration (عمداء الكليات، المجلس العلمي، المشرفة على القسم النسائي) plus 5 ORIGINAL pre-existing positions the job-titles migration never touched at all (الرئيس التنفيذي، نائب الرئيس التنفيذي، النائب المساعد للتميز الأكاديمي، مدير الادارة المالية، مدير رأس المال البشري) -- a real, newly-surfaced gap (the CEO/Deputy-CEO tier itself has no job title link), flagged here as a follow-up rather than silently backfilled, since "show job titles" didn't ask for new links to be created.

- **Could not get a live browser screenshot this round**: every `localhost`/`127.0.0.1` navigation attempt in this session's Browser pane was denied regardless of port (confirmed not app-specific -- even a previously-working local port from an earlier PR in this same session failed identically), while HTTPS navigation to the real production site worked fine throughout -- a transient environment/pane issue, not a code defect, reported transparently rather than fabricating a screenshot. Verified instead via `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx eslint`/the full `vitest run` suite (139 tests, unchanged) all passing clean, plus the direct-database join check above proving the new server-side data path resolves correctly; the client-side render itself reuses the identical conditional-line pattern already shipped and live-verified for the assignee list in earlier PRs, so risk is low but a manual look after merge is still worthwhile.

- **Backfilled job titles for the 5 original org_structure_positions rows that predated the job-titles-linking work, closing the gap the previous entry surfaced** (2026-07-27, migration `20260727000005_backfill_original_position_job_titles.sql`), per direct request: "backfill job titles for the CEO/Deputy CEO positions too" -- extended to all 5 originally-flagged gaps (not just the 2 named), since they're the same category of gap (positions that simply predated migration 20260727000001/000002/000003's scope, which only ever touched the 44 NEW positions) and 3 of the 5 have confident REUSE matches, avoiding a half-finished result. 2 REUSE links (same spelling-variance-tolerance precedent used throughout this project): مدير الادارة المالية -> the real imported "مدير الإدارة المالية" (grade 14), مدير رأس المال البشري -> the real imported "مدير إدارة رأس المال البشري" (grade 14). 3 NEW titles, since nothing close existed: **الرئيس التنفيذي -> "رئيس الجامعة"** (grade 16, family "عام") -- `[استنتاج]`: this position's `org_unit_id` already links to the real "رئيس الجامعة" org unit (20260726000006), the actual University President role, distinct from "الرئيس التنفيذي للخدمات المشتركة" (a subordinate exec role already reused for a different position) -- titled after the real linked org unit, not the position's own generic display name, same convention 20260727000003 used; grade 16 is this schema's numeric ceiling (the CHECK constraint caps at 16), so a University President necessarily sits at the same grade as several subordinate exec titles already in the table -- a real, inherent limitation of the existing 1-16 scale, not worked around here since expanding the scale wasn't asked for.

- **نائب الرئيس التنفيذي -> "نائب الرئيس للشؤون الأكاديمية"** (grade 16, family "الأكاديمي") -- `[استنتاج]`: this position's own display name is the older "نائب الرئيس التنفيذي" label, but its real linked org unit is "نائب الرئيس للشؤون الأكاديمية" (VP Academic Affairs, confirmed via direct query); the title follows the real linked org unit, not the position's legacy display name.

- **النائب المساعد للتميز الأكاديمي -> "النائب المساعد للتميز الأكاديمي"** (grade 16, family "الأكاديمي") -- named identically to both the position and its linked org unit, the same self-titled precedent as المشرفة على القسم النسائي; deliberately not reworded to match the existing "مساعد النائب لـ..." rows' reversed word order, since no rewording was requested. The 3 positions PR #100 deliberately left titleless (عمداء الكليات، المجلس العلمي، المشرفة على القسم النسائي -- collegial bodies / already self-titled) were confirmed untouched. Verified end-to-end against the real shared database: dry-ran the exact insert shape first (4-column insert, immediately deleted) to confirm no unexpected NOT NULL column would block it before touching real data, then applied for real and re-queried the full 49-row `org_structure_positions` table -- exactly 46 now resolve to a real job title (up from 41), and the 3 still-NULL rows are precisely the 3 intentional exclusions, confirmed by name. No application code touched -- the org-chart's own job-title rendering (shipped in the immediately preceding entry) picks these up automatically with zero further changes, since it already reads whatever `job_title_id` resolves to.

- **Job titles now also render on the staffing table, per direct follow-up** (2026-07-27): after confirming the org-chart's job-title display live in the browser, the project owner asked "add them there too" for `/admin/org-structure/staffing`. Added a new read-only "المسمى الوظيفي" column between the position-edit cell and "الموظفون المسكَّنون", reusing the identical `job_title_id` -> `job_titles.name_ar` lookup already built for the chart page -- no new query shape, just the same pattern applied to a second page. `OrgStructurePositionRow.tsx` gained a `jobTitle: string | null` prop, rendered plainly (no icon, unlike the chart's `Briefcase` treatment, since this is a dense data table not a visual node) with "—" for the 3 positions with none. No edit UI added for this column (consistent with the chart, which is also read-only for this field) -- assigning/changing a position's job title still has no UI anywhere; only the backfill migrations and the original 20260727000003 migration set it.

- **Caught a real environment mix-up during verification, not a code bug**: the first browser check against `localhost:3000` showed the OLD 6-header table with no job-title column at all, which looked like the change hadn't taken effect -- investigation via `netstat`/`Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` traced port 3000 to a Next.js dev server process actually running from `C:\SRU\sru-hr-performance` (the main checkout, not this session's isolated worktree), evidently started by another concurrent Claude session sharing this same machine/browser pane; the preview tool's own port-picker didn't detect that pre-existing bind and silently routed the browser to the wrong server. Fixed by starting this worktree's own `next dev` explicitly on an out-of-the-way port (3317, confirmed free via `netstat` first) via a plain background `npm run dev -- -p 3317` and pointing `preview_start` at that exact URL instead of relying on the named-launch/autoPort mechanism -- confirmed against the real worktree this time: the new column renders "رئيس الجامعة"/"نائب الرئيس للشؤون الأكاديمية"/"مدير إدارة رأس المال البشري"/"مدير الإدارة المالية"/"النائب المساعد للتميز الأكاديمي" for the 5 backfilled positions and "—" for المجلس العلمي, matching the org chart's own already-verified values exactly. This cross-session port collision is a real environment hazard worth remembering for future verification in this repo, not something fixed in code. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint` (both changed files), and the full `vitest run` suite (140 tests, unchanged) all passed clean.

- **Org chart rebuilt from row-per-level (horizontal) to column-per-level (vertical), plus an explicit edit icon on position mini-rows** (2026-07-27), from direct feedback with a screenshot of the org-structure builder page. Two distinct asks: (1) "اضف ايقونة للتعديل لكي استطيع اعدل المنصب او المرجعي" -- the position name/org-unit fields in `OrgStructurePositionMiniRow.tsx` (the builder page's level-card rows) had always been live, always-editable inputs with no visual cue that they were interactive, unlike the level cards' own inputs which look the same but ARE always-editable by design; there was no way to tell the position row apart. Rebuilt as an explicit view/edit toggle: default state shows plain read-only text (name_ar bold, name_en muted LTR, linked org unit name in parentheses) plus a new pencil ("تعديل") icon button; clicking it reveals the existing name/name/org-unit inputs plus Save/Cancel/Delete icons, mirroring the Save-then-revert pattern already used elsewhere in this app (e.g. `EditRoleForm`). Two new translation keys (`editButton`, `cancelButton`) added to the `OrgStructurePage` namespace in both locales. (2) "اريد اخراجا احترافيا مميزا لا ممتدا بشكل افقي ... كأن تجعل مستوى C4 بشكل رأسي واضبط علاقة الخطوط" -- with real data, a level holding many positions (C4) stretched the whole chart's ROW-based layout out horizontally far enough to need constant scrolling just to see the tree's shape. `OrgChartTree.tsx` transposed from row-per-level/horizontal-slot to column-per-level/vertical-slot: each level is now a column (root on the right, depth increasing to the left, matching RTL reading flow), and `computeSlots`'s existing leaf-ordering algorithm (completely unchanged internally) now drives VERTICAL position instead of horizontal -- siblings stack top-to-bottom within their column. This means canvas WIDTH only grows with tree DEPTH (4 levels -> 1040px, fixed), not breadth, while a wide level just makes the chart taller, which a normal page scrolls far more gracefully than horizontal overflow ever did. Connector-line math mirrored the same transposition: instead of a vertical-then-horizontal elbow via a mid-Y split, it's now horizontal-then-vertical via a mid-X split, from the parent's LEFT edge to the child's RIGHT edge -- still a single elbow regardless of how many columns a skip-level link spans, no special-casing needed (same as before). `.sru-orgchart-node`'s `white-space: nowrap` was dropped for a fixed `width: 190px` with normal wrapping, since a column layout needs bounded, predictable node width (an unbounded-width node would have broken the fixed per-column x position this layout depends on) -- long names now wrap onto multiple lines (bounded height growth) instead of stretching indefinitely (unbounded width growth), which is exactly the tradeoff a taller-not-wider chart wants.

- **Caught and fixed a real test-methodology mistake while verifying, not a bug in the feature itself**: the first two live edit/save attempts appeared to succeed (200 OK, view mode correctly resumed) but the typed value never appeared in the database on ANY row -- investigation revealed the verification script's own `document.querySelectorAll('input[dir="ltr"]')[0]` grabbed the LEVEL card's own always-rendered name_en input (which sits earlier in the DOM within the same level card) instead of the intended POSITION row's newly-revealed edit input; since the position's Save button reads its own component's local React state (never touched by typing into a sibling component's DOM node), the save correctly persisted the position's untouched original values -- a real, instructive false alarm, not a save-path defect. Re-verified correctly scoped to a real element (a temporary throwaway position created directly in the database for this exact purpose, never a real production row) via `row.querySelectorAll('input')` scoped under the same button group as the clicked Cancel/Save icons: typed a new Arabic name, clicked Save, confirmed the exact new value persisted in `org_structure_positions` and rendered in the UI; then clicked Delete and confirmed the row was soft-deleted, then hard-deleted it directly (a throwaway test artifact, not real data, per this project's discipline that only genuine test rows get hard-removed). Separately confirmed via direct query that this repeated confusion never touched real production data at any point -- the real "الرئيس التنفيذي" position (`name_en='CEO'`) and level "1" (`name_en='1'`) were both unchanged throughout. Also confirmed the new vertical layout itself directly via DOM measurement against the real 49-position production hierarchy: canvas size 1040x5016 (width fixed at 4 columns x 260px regardless of the 49-position count; height driven by breadth), الرئيس التنفيذي (root, level 1) and نائب الرئيس التنفيذي (level C2) and مدير رأس المال البشري/مدير الادارة المالية (level C3, sharing the same column x-coordinate but different vertical slots) all measured at exactly the expected relative x-positions confirming root-right/depth-left ordering, and all 48 parent-child connector paths rendered with the correct transposed elbow shape. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint` (all three changed files), and the full `vitest run` suite (140 tests, unchanged -- no new pure function needed dedicated unit coverage beyond what was already live-verified) all passed clean. Hit the same cross-session port-3000-collision lesson documented in the immediately preceding entry -- ran this worktree's own dev server on an explicit unused port from the start this time, no confusion.

- **`/reports` restructured into three independently-gated tabs, plus a new "التقارير" section in the /admin permission matrix** (2026-07-27, migration `20260727000006_reports_process_areas.sql`), per direct request: "اضف التقارير كعنوان في الصلاحيات وبعدها تضيف تقارير الاداء تقارير الاستراتيجية تقارير الجدارات بحيث تظهر فقط لمن له الصلاحية" -- clarified in follow-up: "هذه التقارير تظهر كتبويبات في موديول التقارير وتضاف سكاشن في جدول الصلاحيات" (these reports show as tabs in the Reports module, and are added as sections in the permissions table). Added only TWO new process_area values, `performanceReports`/`competencyReports`, not three: "تقارير الاستراتيجية" deliberately REUSES the already-existing `strategicPlanning` area (added the same day, PR #106) rather than duplicating it -- that area's own `view`-vs-`approve` split already separates read-only strategic reporting (ceo) from full module management (strategy_admin), exactly the same distinction a new area would exist to draw; adding a parallel one would fragment a single already-correct permission into two that must be kept in sync, against this project's established reuse-don't-duplicate precedent. `performanceReports`/`competencyReports` ARE genuinely new: performance data on /reports was already a bundle of many DIFFERENT existing grants (evaluation/calibration/promotions/vacancies/employeeData/orgStructure/staffing/userManagement) with no single umbrella permission gating the dashboard as a whole, and competency reporting had no report surface at all. No `role_permissions` rows seeded (least-privilege default, matching the request's own "تظهر فقط لمن له الصلاحية" framing) -- access is granted through the existing /admin role editor. `vpra.ts`: both new areas added to `ProcessArea`/`processAreas`/`processAreaLabels` (20 total now, up from 18) and grouped into a new "التقارير" section in `processAreaSections`, sitting between the existing "طرق التقييم..." and "الموارد البشرية..." sections; `strategicPlanning` stays in its original section (the one-area-one-section invariant, enforced by an existing test, means it can't also appear under "التقارير"). `/reports/page.tsx`: the always-visible "لوحتي الشخصية" personal section (own evaluation state/goals/tasks, shown to every logged-in user unconditionally since the 2026-07-25 rebuild) was pulled OUT of the tab structure entirely and rendered above the tabs, unconditional -- gating the new performance tab must not take away a plain employee's own summary, which this page has never restricted before. Below it, three independently-gated tabs: "تقارير الأداء" (`performanceReports>=view`, wrapping the pre-existing org-wide dashboard content -- completion rate, calibration sessions, recommendations breakdown, vacancies, headcount, staffing coverage, users-by-role, unchanged internally, each card still separately checking its own existing finer-grained permission on top of the new tab-level gate), "تقارير الاستراتيجية" (`strategicPlanning>=view`, unchanged from the same-day PR #106 build, just renamed from "لوحة الاستراتيجية" for naming consistency with the other two tabs), and "تقارير الجدارات" (`competencyReports>=view`, new content: real institutional-framework counts -- 3 pillars, 9 domains, 27 competencies split by type, 108 behavioral levels -- plus per-employee competency-score coverage via `evaluation_scores.competency_id`, deliberately NOT inventing a fabricated "average competency score" narrative since that table has zero rows linked to a competency in production today, matching the same no-fabricated-metrics discipline already applied to the omitted "% أهداف استراتيجية محققة" figure). Tab count adapts to what the caller actually holds: 0 gates passing renders no tab bar at all (just the personal section), exactly 1 renders that section's content directly without tab chrome, 2+ uses the existing `ProfileTabs` component -- same discipline this page already used before today.

- **A real, instructive RLS interaction confirmed live, not assumed**: a temporary custom test role holding ONLY `competencyReports=view` (no `competencyFramework`) saw the "تقارير الجدارات" tab appear (correct -- the new tab-level gate passed) but every count read 0, because `competency_pillars`/`competency_domains`/`competencies`/`competency_levels` each still enforce their OWN pre-existing RLS keyed on `competencyFramework`, independent of the new tab gate; granting that role `competencyFramework=view` too immediately produced the exact real counts (3/9/27/108) -- confirming this is the same documented "each table's own RLS decides" caveat already noted elsewhere on this page (e.g. hr_admin's goals/tasks visibility), not a bug, and that the underlying query logic itself is correct. Also verified live: the same custom role's "تقارير الأداء" tab appeared (tab-level gate passed) but rendered zero cards, since it held none of the underlying per-card grants -- the intentional, documented edge case of the two-tier gating design. A separate plain `employee`-role test account correctly saw NONE of the three tabs (only the personal section), and the real `/admin` permission-matrix UI correctly rendered a new "التقارير" section header with exactly the two new rows ("تقارير الأداء"/"تقارير الجدارات"), checkboxes correctly reflecting the test role's seeded `view`-level grants. All temporary test data (3 auth users/profiles, 1 custom role and its role_permissions rows) deleted afterward, confirmed zero residual rows. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint` (all changed files), and the full `vitest run` suite (140 tests -- the existing process-area-count assertion updated from 18 to 20) all passed clean.

- **Vision/Mission/Values opened to every authenticated user, closing a permission gap reported live** (2026-07-30, migration `20260730000004_strategic_identity_readable_by_all.sql`): the project owner logged in as an ordinary user and reported "صفحة الخطة الاستراتيجية لم تتغير عند المستخدم فلا أرى الرؤية والرسالة" -- the الخطة الاستراتيجية module showed only "الأهداف المسندة" and "بنك الأهداف", with no vision/mission tab at all, directly contradicting their own earlier instruction ("بالنسبة لبقية المستخدمين اضف الخطة الاستراتيجية واضف تاب للرؤية والرسالة والاهداف").

- **Diagnosed before touching anything, and the root cause was two layers deep, not just the UI**: a direct `pg_policies` query confirmed `strategic_identity_select`/`strategic_values_select` (20260728000002) both require `check_vpra_global('strategicPlanning','view')`, and a direct `role_permissions` query confirmed only THREE roles hold any `strategicPlanning` grant at all (`ceo=view`, `strategy_admin=approve`, `super_admin=approve`) -- so removing only the nav gate (`navItems.ts`) and the page's own `canView` check would have shipped a visibly-empty page, since RLS is the real filter. Fixed at all three layers: the migration opens both SELECT policies to `TO authenticated USING (true)`, the `kpis/strategic-identity` nav child loses its `access` requirement (joining `kpis` as the second deliberately-ungated child, asserted in `navItems.test.tsx`), and the page drops its `canView` early-return entirely.

- **Write policies are deliberately untouched** and remain at `prepare` (20260730000002) -- the three VPRA tiers govern EDITING, per the project owner's own 2026-07-30 clarification that "إعداد" means edit rights only.
