Seeded exactly as requested: `hr_admin='approve'`, `super_admin='view'`, no other role.

- **Approval is enforced in the Server Action, not RLS** -- Postgres policies gate rows, not columns, so `recruitment_plans_update` sits at `prepare` and `approveRecruitmentPlan` explicitly requires `approve` via the existing `get_my_permissions()` RPC before writing (same shape as `reviewEmployeeApproval`). `publishPlanItemAsVacancy` deliberately inserts through the caller's own client so `vacancies_insert`'s own `check_vpra('vacancies','approve', org_unit_id)` still decides -- no service-role bypass -- and seeds `requirements_ar` from the job title's `qualification_required`, the same source the manual create-vacancy form already prefills from. `importVacantPositionsIntoPlan` is idempotent in code (skips positions already in the plan) with the `(plan_id, position_id)` partial unique index as the real backstop. New `src/lib/recruitmentPlan.ts` holds the totals arithmetic + Arabic label maps, unit-tested (6 new tests, 163 total) rather than only exercised through the screen -- including the case that matters: items with no cost count toward headcount but are reported separately so a money total is never silently understated. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with two real temporary accounts (dev server on an explicit free port, per the documented cross-session port-3000 hazard). **hr_admin**: created a real 2027 plan; a second plan for the same year was correctly rejected with the real "توجد خطة لهذه السنة بالفعل" message (the partial unique index, surfaced as a clear message not a generic error); "استيراد المناصب الشاغرة" pulled exactly **44** items in one click with totals rendering ٤٤ / ٤١٠٬٢٤٠ monthly / ٤٬٩٢٢٬٨٨٠ annual; added a manual item (محلل بيانات, headcount 3, Q2, high) leaving cost blank and confirmed via direct query that it was auto-seeded to **4802**, byte-matching that title's real `salary_scale.step_a`; published that item and confirmed a REAL `vacancies` row was created (correct job title/org unit, requirements seeded from the job title) with the item flipped to `posted` and linked by `vacancy_id`; edited another item (headcount/quarter/priority/cost) and confirmed the values persisted; deleted one item and confirmed via direct query it was **soft-deleted** (`deleted_at` set, row still present -- 44 active, 1 soft-deleted), never a hard DELETE; approved the plan and confirmed `status='approved'` with `approved_by` set to the approver's own profile id, plus a complete 7-row `audit_log` trail (created/imported/added/published/updated/deleted/approved). **super_admin (view-only)**: at the RLS layer, SELECT returned the plan and all items while INSERT was rejected by Postgres (`42501`) and UPDATE affected 0 rows; in the browser, the same account saw the plan, its 44 items and the totals, but with NO create form, NO add-item form, NO import/approve buttons, NO row-action buttons and no "إجراءات" column at all.

- **A real data gap was surfaced, not papered over**: 23 of the 44 imported items have no cost -- 3 because the position genuinely has no job title, and **20 because their job title has no `salary_scale` row at all** (359 job titles vs 336 salary rows -- mostly titles added during the org-chart backfill, e.g. every "عميد كلية ..."), so the totals card shows an explicit "N بند بلا تكلفة مقدَّرة — الإجمالي أعلاه لا يشملها" note rather than presenting an understated budget as complete; filling those 20 salary rows is a data task for the project owner, not a code fix. All test data (2 auth users/profiles/user_roles rows, 1 plan + its 45 items via CASCADE, the 1 published vacancy, 9 audit rows) deleted afterward scoped to exact ids -- confirmed `recruitment_plans`/`recruitment_plan_items` back to 0 and `vacancies` back to exactly the 1 real production row (محلل أعمال), untouched throughout. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (163 tests) all passed clean.

- **"الشواغر" tab redesigned -- the التوظيف module's second tab** (2026-08-04), continuing the module's per-tab design pass.

- **No migration: this is entirely an application-layer change** on the existing `vacancies` table.

- **The real gap it closes, found by reading the code rather than reported**: `vacancies_update`'s RLS (`check_vpra('vacancies','recommend', org_unit_id)` -- hr_admin + manager) has existed since the table was created (20260719000007), but NOTHING in the app ever issued an UPDATE -- a posting could be created and then never closed, marked filled, or removed. New `src/app/[locale]/(app)/vacancies/actions.ts`: `updateVacancyStatus` (inline status change) and `deleteVacancy` (a genuine soft-delete setting `deleted_at`, since `vacancies` has no DELETE policy at all -- CLAUDE.md §5-A rule 7); both go through the caller's own client so that existing policy stays the only gate (an org-unit-scoped manager can close a posting in their own unit and nowhere else, decided by Postgres), both treat an RLS-denied UPDATE's zero matched rows as "forbidden" rather than a silent success, and both write real `audit_log` rows (`vacancy_status_changed` carries before/after status). `status` was rendering as the raw English string ("open") on an otherwise fully Arabic screen -- new `src/lib/vacancyStatus.ts` adds the open/closed/filled vocabulary with Arabic labels and, deliberately, a fallback that renders any OTHER stored value as-is (the column is free TEXT with no CHECK enum, and the Excel import can write anything), plus `countVacancyStatuses` where an unrecognized value lands in `other` so the summary parts always add up to the total -- 5 new unit tests (168 total). New `VacanciesTable` client component: summary counts (total/open/closed/filled), live hamza-insensitive search across job title + org unit + requirements (reusing `includesIgnoringHamza`, same in-memory-filter convention as JobTitlesTable/SalaryScaleTable), a status filter, a reset button, and -- only for callers clearing `recommend` -- an inline status `<select>` and a soft-delete button per row. The page also now shows each posting's **provenance**: postings created from a recruitment plan item (the "نشر كشاغر" flow shipped in the previous PR) display "من خطة التوظيف {year}", read through the caller's own client so a user without `recruitmentPlan>=view` simply sees no provenance line rather than an error. The create/import buttons are now gated at `vacancies>=approve` (the level `vacancies_insert` actually requires) instead of being shown to everyone and then rejected by Postgres. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with two real temporary accounts and real fixtures (2 temporary vacancies, one of them linked to a temporary 2031 plan item so the provenance line had real data to render). **hr_admin**: summary read 3 total / 2 open / 1 closed / 0 filled; changing a posting's status through the inline select flipped it open -> filled with the summary re-rendering to 1/0/1 and the database confirming both the new status and a `vacancy_status_changed` audit row carrying the correct before/after; searching "محلل اعمال" WITHOUT the hamza correctly matched the real "محلل أعمال" posting; the status filter isolated the closed one; a deliberately non-matching query rendered the "لا توجد شواغر مطابقة" empty state (distinct from the "no vacancies at all" message); deleting a test posting removed it from the list and the database confirmed a real soft-delete (`deleted_at` set, row still present), with a matching audit row. **employee (view-only, holds the real `vacancies=view` grant)**: saw the list, the summary and the search/filter controls, but with NO status selects, NO delete buttons, no "إجراءات" column at all, and no create/import buttons -- and, at the RLS layer, both an UPDATE and a soft-delete attempt against a real posting affected 0 rows, while `recruitment_plan_items` returned 0 rows (no `recruitmentPlan` grant), which is exactly why that account also saw no provenance line -- graceful degradation, not an error. All test data (2 auth users/profiles/user_roles rows, 2 vacancies, 1 plan + item, 4 audit rows) deleted afterward scoped to exact ids -- confirmed `vacancies` back to exactly the 1 real production row (محلل أعمال, `open`, `deleted_at` null), untouched throughout. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (168 tests) all passed clean.

- **"الترقيات" tab redesigned -- the التوظيف module's third and last tab** (2026-08-04), completing the module's per-tab design pass.

- **No migration: entirely an application-layer change** on the existing `promotions` table. Two substantive additions beyond the same polish the other two tabs got (Arabic status labels via a new `src/lib/promotionStatus.ts` -- قيد المراجعة/معتمدة/مرفوضة with a raw-value fallback since the column is free TEXT; summary counts; live hamza-insensitive search over employee name/number and both job titles; a status filter; and review buttons now gated at `promotions>=recommend` in the UI, mirroring the RLS bar rather than offering an action Postgres would refuse): **(1) the list now checks every proposal against the university's own career ladder.** `career_path` holds 157 real edges (built from the real Career Path workbook), so each row shows "ضمن المسار الوظيفي المعتمد" or "خارج المسار الوظيفي المعتمد" instead of the reviewer needing to know the ladder by heart. Deliberately INFORMATION, not a gate -- an off-ladder promotion is a real managerial decision, so nothing blocks it; and a proposal with no from-title (`from_job_title_id` is nullable) is classified `unknown` and shows no badge at all rather than being wrongly labeled off-path. The edges are read through the caller's own client, so a caller without `careerPath>=view` simply gets no badge. **(2) the propose form now reads what the database already knows.** Picking an employee prefills "from" with the job title recorded on their own profile (previously retyped by hand even though `profiles.job_title_id` was right there), and the "to" list is narrowed to the moves the ladder actually defines out of that title, behind a default-on, clearly-labeled opt-out checkbox ("عرض الوظائف ضمن المسار الوظيفي المعتمد فقط (N)") -- with an explicit note instead of silent narrowing when the ladder defines no next step for that title. 9 new unit tests (177 total) cover the status labels/counts and the two new pure helpers (`classifyPromotionAgainstCareerPath`, `nextCareerSteps`), including the "no from-title is unknown, not off-path" case and a real fan-out. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with two real temporary accounts and fixtures anchored on a REAL career_path edge (قائد فريق خدمة العملاء -> مدير مركز الاتصال, a title with 3 real defined next steps) plus a deliberately off-ladder target. **hr_admin**: the two seeded proposals rendered with the correct badges (on-path vs off-path) and Arabic statuses, summary 2/1/1/0; search and status filter narrowed correctly with a distinct "لا توجد ترقيات مطابقة" empty state; on the propose form, selecting the test employee auto-filled "from" = قائد فريق خدمة العملاء (درجة 10) from their profile and collapsed the "to" list **from 360 options to exactly the 3 real ladder steps** (مدير مركز الاتصال / مدير خدمة العملاء / مدير تجربة العملاء -- the real fan-out documented in the career-path data), unchecking the box restored all 360 and re-checking narrowed it again; a real proposal submitted through that flow was confirmed in the database with the prefilled from-title, and approving it through the list flipped it to معتمدة with the summary re-rendering to 3/1/2/0. **employee (no `promotions` grant at all)**: saw only their own promotions via the self-row RLS bypass, with no "إجراءات" column, no review buttons and no "اقتراح ترقية" button -- and at the RLS layer, approving their own pending promotion affected 0 rows while self-proposing was rejected (`42501`). One expected-behavior check worth recording: that account's sidebar shows "الشواغر" rather than "التوظيف", because `sidebarGroupLabelKey` deliberately uses the single visible child's own label when only one child is visible -- not a regression.

- **A real blocker on this tab is unchanged and NOT worked around**: `promotions.cycle_id` is NOT NULL and production still has ZERO `evaluation_cycles` rows, so the propose form correctly shows its honest "no evaluation cycles exist yet" message and cannot be used at all until one exists -- this session created a temporary cycle purely to test, then deleted it. There is no UI anywhere in the app to create an evaluation cycle (the /evaluations screen is a read-only list), which blocks promotions, rewards, calibration and targets alike; flagged to the project owner as the natural next piece of work rather than silently making `cycle_id` nullable. All test data (2 auth users/profiles/user_roles rows, 1 evaluation cycle, 3 promotions, 4 audit rows) deleted afterward scoped to exact ids -- confirmed `promotions`/`evaluation_cycles` back to 0 rows and `career_path` untouched at its real 157 edges. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (177 tests) all passed clean.

- **Added the ability to change an existing position's reporting line ("التبعية") on the org-structure builder and staffing screens** (2026-08-05), per direct request with a screenshot of the builder page's level-3 position rows: "اضف خاصية تغيير التبعية للمنصب" -- `org_structure_positions.parent_id` had been settable only at `addPosition` time since the tree feature was first built (20260723000001), with no way afterward to fix a mistake or reflect a real reorg; both edit rows (`OrgStructurePositionMiniRow.tsx` on `/admin/org-structure`, `OrgStructurePositionRow.tsx` on the staffing screen) only ever rendered the parent as read-only text. Added a parent `<select>` to both, mirroring `AddOrgStructurePositionForm`'s own already-established rule exactly: any position at a level ABOVE this one (lower `level_order`), not just the immediately preceding level -- `validate_org_structure_position_parent()` has only blocked a self-parent and cycles, not a fixed one-level-up hop, since migration 20260724000001 -- and a root-level position (the structure's single entry point) shows no selector at all, its parent forced to stay `NULL`, exactly like the create form's own behavior. `updatePosition` (`admin/org-structure/actions.ts`) gained a required `parentId: string | null` parameter (`null` explicitly for root positions, a real position id otherwise) on top of its existing `nameAr`/`nameEn`/`orgUnitId` params -- the action does not re-derive the root/non-root distinction itself, deliberately trusting `validate_org_structure_position_parent()` as the single source of truth for that invariant, the same trust model `addPosition` already uses.

- **Extracted the shared eligible-parent-options logic into a new `src/lib/orgStructurePositions.ts`** (`computeEligibleParentPositions`, `computeDescendantPositionIds`, `isRootLevelOrder`, unit-tested) rather than duplicating it inline in both row components -- this project has already been burned once by a fix landing in only one of two places that both needed it (the org-unit-employees rollup, 2026-07-26/27), so the same logic needing to live correctly in both `OrgStructurePositionMiniRow` and `OrgStructurePositionRow` was reason enough to share it from the start rather than risk drifting apart later. The extraction also excludes a position's own descendants from its eligible-parent list client-side -- structurally redundant for any tree built entirely through this UI (a child's `level_order` is always required to be numerically greater than its parent's, so the level-order filter alone already makes a cycle unreachable), but a defensive backstop for rows created by an earlier bulk import that might not follow that convention, so an obviously cyclical choice never appears as pickable instead of only surfacing as a raw server-side rejection.

- **A real bug in this exact new descendant-exclusion logic was caught by its own unit test before ever reaching the browser, not found live**: the initial BFS implementation guarded against infinite loops by checking `descendantIds.has(childId)`, but on a corrupted cyclical `parent_id` chain that loops back to the STARTING position itself, that guard never fires for the starting id (since it was never added to `descendantIds` in the first place) -- so the starting position got silently re-added to its own "descendants" set, contradicting the function's own documented contract ("excluding itself"). Fixed by seeding a separate `visited` set with the starting position id purely for cycle-guarding, while the returned `descendantIds` set never includes it; a dedicated test (`does not loop forever on a corrupted cyclical parent_id chain`) now locks this in. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared production database with a real temporary `super_admin` test account and one real temporary test position (seeded directly at level C3 with parent = the real "الرئيس التنفيذي" root): opened the position's edit row on `/admin/org-structure`, confirmed the parent select rendered with exactly 14 real eligible options (every real level-1/level-2 position) with the seeded parent pre-selected, changed it to "نائب الرئيس التنفيذي" and saved -- confirmed via direct query the real `parent_id` column updated accordingly; separately opened the real root "الرئيس التنفيذي" position's own edit row and confirmed it shows NO parent selector at all (cancelled without saving, so the real production root was never touched); reloaded and confirmed the staffing screen's equivalent row for the same test position showed the identical parent select, correctly pre-selected with the value just saved from the other screen, confirmed the Save button's existing dirty-tracking correctly extends to the new parent field (disabled until changed, enabled once a different parent is picked), changed it back to "الرئيس التنفيذي" from THIS screen and saved -- confirmed via direct query the change round-tripped correctly through both screens. All temporary test data (1 auth user, 1 profile, its `user_roles` row, 1 test position) deleted afterward, confirmed zero residual rows; the real production 49 positions / 4 levels were reconfirmed completely unchanged throughout (only the disposable test position was ever written to). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (173 tests -- 10 new for `orgStructurePositions.ts`) all passed clean.

- **"إضافة دورة تقييم" form restyled to the add-employee pattern, plus an explicit period-duration control** (2026-08-04), from a screenshot showing only the type/name fields. (1) `NewEvaluationCycleForm` used its own one-off Tailwind-utility inputs -- restyled to the same `sru-formsection`/`sru-formgrid`/`sru-field`/`sru-form-submitrow` pattern as `EmployeeInviteForm`, split into "بيانات الدورة" and "الفترة الزمنية" sections with icon badges. (2) The start/end date inputs already existed but rendered ONLY after a period type was chosen (a deliberate earlier "pick the type first" request) -- which is exactly why the screenshot showed no dates at all. Rather than silently reverting that earlier decision, the period fields now always RENDER, disabled with an explicit "اختر نوع الفترة أولًا" note until a type is picked, so the cycle's period is visibly part of the form. (3) Added the requested "مدة الفترة": a 3/6/9/12-month preset (plus "مدة مخصصة") that computes the end date from the start date, with a live summary of whatever span the two dates actually produce ("المدة: 12 شهرًا (365 يومًا)").

- **No schema change and no `duration` column** -- `evaluation_cycles` has only `start_date`/`end_date` and duration is simply their span, so inventing a stored column would have been redundant data to keep in sync. New `src/lib/cyclePeriod.ts` holds the date math (`addMonths` with month-end clamping, `computeEndDate` = the day BEFORE the same date N months later so 2026-01-01 + 12 months ends 2026-12-31, `describeCycleDuration` reporting inclusive days plus whole months when the range matches a preset exactly), all string-part arithmetic rather than `new Date(isoString)` -- the UTC-midnight parse that shifts a date back one calendar day in a negative-offset timezone is a bug this project already hit once in the org-structure Excel import. 15 new unit tests (`cyclePeriod.test.ts`), including leap years and the 31 Jan + 1 month clamp; **one of them caught my own wrong assumption before any browser check** (the first draft asserted 2026-02-29 parses, but 2026 is not a leap year -- the code was right and the test was wrong). Editing the end date by hand flips the preset to "مدة مخصصة" instead of leaving a stale preset label, and an end-before-start range shows an inline message mirroring the DB's own `evaluation_cycles_dates_valid` CHECK. Arabic month/day counts use ICU plural categories (`few`/`many`) so it reads "3 أشهر" but "12 شهرًا" and "365 يومًا", not a single hardcoded plural form. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with a real temporary `hr_admin` test account (`evaluation_cycles_insert` requires `check_vpra_global('evaluation','approve')`, hr_admin-only): both sections rendered, the period fields were correctly disabled with the note before a type was chosen and enabled after, 2026-01-01 auto-filled 2026-12-31 at 12 months / 2026-06-30 at 6 / 2026-03-31 at 3 with correct Arabic-plural summaries each time, a hand-picked end date switched the preset to "مدة مخصصة", an end-before-start range showed the inline error, and a real submission created the exact intended `evaluation_cycles` row (`calendar`, 2026-01-01 -> 2026-12-31). Could not capture a screenshot (the Browser pane wasn't displayed so it wasn't compositing frames -- an environment limitation, reported rather than fabricated); verified via real DOM/state inspection and direct database queries. All test data (1 auth user, 1 profile, its `user_roles` row, 1 evaluation cycle) deleted afterward scoped to the exact created ids -- `evaluation_cycles` confirmed back to 0 rows, the same zero-cycles state production was in beforehand. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (202 tests) all passed clean.

- **Vacancy requirements now prefill from the career path's own "متطلبات الانتقال", superseding the same-day job-title source** (2026-08-04): the previous entry flagged its source choice as `[استنتاج]` (`job_titles.qualification_required` over `career_path.requirements_ar`) and asked directly rather than assuming -- the project owner answered "نعم من متطلبات الانتقال في المسار الوظيفي", so the source was switched. `vacancies/new/page.tsx` now reads every `career_path` row whose `to_job_title_id` is the selected title (i.e. the requirements to move INTO that job) and passes them to the form keyed by target title; the from-side title's NAME is resolved in JS from the already-fetched `job_titles` list rather than a PostgREST embed, deliberately sidestepping `career_path`'s dual-FK-to-`job_titles` disambiguation problem this project has hit before. Checked the real data before designing the multi-edge case rather than assuming: of 150 job titles with any incoming edge, 148 carry requirement text and ZERO have conflicting texts across multiple predecessors (3 multi-predecessor titles all agree) -- so distinct texts are still all kept and joined, never silently reduced to whichever edge sorted first, but in practice today the hint names one predecessor.

- **A real, reported coverage trade-off, not hidden**: only 150 of 359 job titles have any incoming career-path edge, versus 265 carrying their own `qualification_required` -- so 211 titles now show "لا توجد متطلبات انتقال مسجّلة" and an empty box where the previous source would have prefilled something; deliberately NOT silently falling back to `qualification_required`, since mixing two sources under one label would make the hint text a lie about where the numbers came from. Flagged to the project owner as a one-line follow-up if they want the fallback. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with a real temporary `hr_admin` test account: selecting "مدير مركز الاتصال" prefilled the exact real edge text ("ماجستير

## الجزء الثاني (2026-08-04 → 2026-08-29)

- بكالوريوس خبرة 5 سنوات") with the hint naming its real predecessor "قائد فريق خدمة العملاء", a title with no incoming edge ("مدير تطوير الأعمال") correctly showed the empty box + explanatory note instead, manually typed text survived switching titles in both directions (with the restore button appearing), restore pulled the real edge text back, and a real submission created the exact intended `vacancies` row. - **Fixed a real bug on `/calibration`: the "جلسة جديدة" (New Session) button was shown unconditionally, even to callers who can't create one** (2026-08-05): direct request to re-check `/calibration` after the earlier `/calibration/new` misleading-message fix (PR #133). Read all three pages (`page.tsx`/`new/page.tsx`/`[id]/page.tsx`) again in full -- `/calibration/new` and `/calibration/[id]` both checked out correct, but the LIST page's "جلسة جديدة" link had no permission gate at all, unlike `/calibration/new` itself which already requires `calibration>=approve` (its own already-documented fix for "reachable and fully renderable by any authenticated user, same bug class as kpis/strategic-goals"). This is the identical show-an-action-the-caller-cant-actually-do pattern already fixed for `/vacancies`' and `/employees`' create/import buttons -- clicking the button would land on a page that immediately shows the real forbidden message, not a silent Postgres rejection, but still an unnecessary extra step for the majority of `calibration`-holding roles that don't reach `approve`. Added the same `get_my_permissions()` check already used by `/calibration/new` and hid the button entirely for accounts below `approve`.

- **A real, load-bearing discovery while verifying, not assumed from memory**: the very first live test used a temporary `super_admin` account expecting to reproduce the bug (per the 2026-07-19 sign-off in this file, super_admin was listed as `calibration=view`) -- but the button still showed. Investigated directly against the real `role_permissions` table rather than trusting the old log entry, and found the matrix has genuinely changed since that sign-off: `super_admin` and `ceo` now hold `calibration=approve`, `hr_admin`/`committee` hold `recommend`, and `cxo`/`strategy_admin`/`competencies_admin`/`manager` hold `view` -- a real, undocumented-until-now drift, and a second live illustration (after the earlier `role_permissions` snapshot-goes-stale lesson from PR #133 itself) that this matrix must be re-queried directly, never assumed from a prior session's notes. Verified the fix correctly with a real temporary `cxo` account (`view`, the current holder of that tier): button correctly hidden. Verified a real temporary `hr_admin` account (`recommend`, still below `approve`): button still correctly hidden. Verified a real temporary `ceo` account (`approve`): button correctly shown, and the full create flow still works end-to-end -- created one real temporary test `evaluation_cycles` row (production still has zero real cycles) since the create form itself requires at least one to render, submitted the form, and confirmed the real `calibration_sessions` row appeared correctly on the list with `status='draft'`. All temporary test data (3 auth users/profiles/user_roles rows, 1 evaluation cycle, 1 calibration session) deleted afterward, confirmed zero residual rows in every touched table. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (202 tests, unchanged -- a page-level conditional-render fix mirroring the already-tested `/calibration/new` pattern, no new pure function needing dedicated coverageEvaluation-cycles screen turned from a read-only list into a management screen** (2026-08-04).

- **A correction first, recorded because it was stated wrongly to the project owner**: the promotions PR reported that "no UI exists anywhere to create an evaluation cycle" — that was WRONG, based on this file's own older text rather than the actual code. `/evaluations/cycles/new` has existed since PR #108 (with the period-type selector added in #110); the real situation is that production simply has zero cycles because nobody has used it yet. What genuinely was missing is everything AFTER creation: `evaluation_cycles_update`'s RLS (`check_vpra_global('evaluation','approve')`, ceo/hr_admin/super_admin) had existed since 20260719000011 **with no consumer at all**, so a cycle could be created but never corrected — a typo in a name or a wrong end date was permanent — and there was no soft-delete either.

- **No migration: application layer only.** New `src/app/[locale]/(app)/evaluations/cycles/actions.ts` (`updateEvaluationCycle`, `countEvaluationCycleUsage`, `deleteEvaluationCycle`) plus `EvaluationCycleRow` (view/edit toggle with dirty-state Save, same shape as the org-structure and recruitment-plan rows) and `src/lib/evaluationCycle.ts` (9 new unit tests, 186 total). The list gained two derived columns: **status** (لم تبدأ/جارية/منتهية, computed from the cycle's own dates — deliberately not stored, since a status column would be a second source of truth next to `start_date`/`end_date`; compared as YYYY-MM-DD strings against "today" in the configured display timezone via the existing `getDisplayTimezone`, avoiding the UTC off-by-one-day class of bug this project already hit once) and **linked-record count** across all 10 tables with a `cycle_id` FK. Delete is a soft-delete (the table has no DELETE policy) and is **blocked while anything references the cycle** — checked explicitly, not left to the FKs, because every `cycle_id` FK is ON DELETE RESTRICT which guards a hard DELETE only; an UPDATE setting `deleted_at` would sail past them and strand real evaluations/goals/promotions pointing at a cycle that vanished from every list (same reasoning as `deleteLevel` on the org-structure screen). The empty state now explains that zero cycles is not cosmetic — it blocks promotions/rewards/calibration/goals/BAU tasks/targets, all of which have a NOT NULL `cycle_id` — with a different sentence for callers who can actually create one.

- **Found and fixed a real bug in this feature's own guard while verifying it, not assumed**: `countEvaluationCycleUsage` first used `select("*", {head:true, count:"exact"})`, which FAILS on `feedback_360` — that table deliberately has no table-level SELECT grant (20260718000005 revoked it and re-granted an explicit column list omitting `evaluator_id`), and since the code ignored the error it produced a silent zero, meaning a cycle whose only dependents were 360-feedback rows could have been soft-deleted. Caught by running the guard's exact queries through a real authenticated `hr_admin` client and seeing the `feedback_360` error; fixed to count `cycle_id` instead of `*`, and to return an error rather than a zero if any table errors — a permission gap must never become a deletion. Re-verified: all 10 tables now count cleanly. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with two real temporary accounts. **hr_admin**: the empty state rendered its manager-specific "create the first one to unblock those modules" sentence; the EXISTING create screen was exercised for real and worked (a real cycle created, appearing with status "جارية" and 0 linked records — confirming the promotions blocker is purely "no data yet", not broken code); editing the name and start date through the new inline editor persisted correctly with a before/after `audit_log` row, and Save was correctly disabled until something actually changed; after inserting a real dependent `evaluations` row the linked-record column showed 1 and the delete button was disabled with an explanatory tooltip; after removing the dependent, deleting through the UI produced a genuine soft-delete (`deleted_at` set, row still present) with a matching audit row. **employee**: saw the list and the new status/linked-record columns but no edit/delete icons and no "إضافة دورة تقييم" button — and at the RLS layer, INSERT was rejected (`42501`) and UPDATE affected 0 rows. One honest limitation of this round's verification: the "delete is blocked server-side" branch could not be exercised through the browser, because force-enabling the disabled button via the DOM does not make React dispatch the click (confirmed by the dev-server log showing no action call); the guard's decision input was instead verified directly through a real authenticated client (total = 1 -> `has_dependents`), and the allow-branch was verified end-to-end through the real UI. All test data (2 auth users/profiles/user_roles rows, 1 cycle, 1 evaluation, 4 audit rows) deleted afterward scoped to exact ids — production confirmed back to 0 cycles and 0 evaluations. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (186 tests) all passed clean.

- **"الوظائف المعلن عنها" (Advertised Jobs) tab + the advertise action on الشواغر** (2026-08-04, migration `20260804000003_vacancy_announcement.sql`), per direct request: an icon on each vacancy row that makes it appear in a new التوظيف-module tab. Two nullable columns on the existing `vacancies` table (`announced_at`, `announced_by`) rather than a new `job_postings` table — "advertised" is one fact about a vacancy whose job title, org unit, requirements and status already live here, so a separate table would duplicate all of them for nothing; `announced_at IS NOT NULL` IS the flag, and it records WHEN, which a boolean would not. No new RLS and no new process area: `vacancies_update` already gates writes at `check_vpra('vacancies','recommend', org_unit_id)` (hr_admin + manager), exactly the tier that should decide what gets advertised, and `vacancies_select` already lets every `vacancies>=view` holder (including plain `employee`, by design) read the row. `setVacancyAnnouncement` toggles both columns through the caller's own client (`announced_by` from the caller's own profile, never the client), writes an `audit_log` row, and treats a zero-row UPDATE as "forbidden" rather than silent success. **[استنتاج]**: advertising deliberately does NOT touch `status` — a closed posting keeps its ad rather than being silently withdrawn, so the new tab shows each posting's real status and adds an explicit "الشاغر لم يعد مفتوحًا رغم بقاء الإعلان" note instead of presenting a closed job as open. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database: an `hr_admin` test account advertised a vacancy (badge + toggled icon appeared, database confirmed `announced_at`/`announced_by` and the audit row), the new tab listed both advertised jobs newest-first with their announcement dates and the closed-status note, and withdrawing an ad reverted the row correctly; an `employee` test account saw the tab and its rows (correct — internal postings are for all staff) but no advertise icons at all, and at the RLS layer its own UPDATE affected 0 rows. All test data deleted afterward, **including reverting the one REAL production vacancy that was advertised during testing back to `announced_at = NULL`** — production confirmed back to exactly 1 vacancy, unadvertised.

- **Cross-session collision worth recording** (the repo has several parallel Claude sessions sharing one working tree): while this feature was mid-verification, another session committed the shared tree with `git add`-style breadth on its own branch, sweeping this feature's application files and message keys into ITS commit, which then merged to main as PR #143 — so the app code landed on main under an unrelated PR title, while this migration file (untracked at that moment) was left behind, i.e. main briefly had code depending on `vacancies.announced_at` with no migration in the repo. This PR carries only that migration, prepared in a SEPARATE git worktree so the other session's in-progress merge in the shared tree was never disturbed. Lesson, already applied here: when this working tree may be shared, do the commit/PR step from an isolated worktree rather than the shared checkout.

- **Vacancy requirements fall back to the job title's own text when the career path records no transition** (2026-08-04): closes the coverage trade-off flagged (not hidden) when the career-path source shipped hours earlier -- only 150 of 359 job titles have any incoming `career_path` edge, so 211 titles were left with an empty box; the project owner answered "نعم، ارجع لمتطلبات المسمى نفسه عند غياب رابط الانتقال". Resolution order is now: transition requirements from `career_path` first, then the title's own `job_titles.qualification_required`, then nothing.

- **The two sources are never blended or mislabeled** -- the hint under the box always states which one produced the text ("مأخوذة من متطلبات الانتقال في المسار الوظيفي (الانتقال من: ...)" vs. "لا يوجد رابط انتقال لهذا المسمى — مأخوذة من متطلبات المسمى الوظيفي «X» نفسه"), and the restore button after a manual edit names the same source it will pull from, so "where did this text come from" is answerable from the screen alone. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with a real temporary `hr_admin` test account, using one real title per branch (classified by direct query first: 148 titles have transition text, 148 have only their own text, 63 have neither): "قائد فريق خدمة العملاء" prefilled from the career path naming its 4 real predecessors, "مدير تطوير الأعمال" (no incoming edge) prefilled from its own real qualification text with the fallback hint, "عامل نظافة - عامل ضيافة" (neither) correctly showed the empty box + "لا توجد متطلبات مسجّلة" note, a manual edit on the fallback case showed the job-title-specific restore label and restored the right text, and a real submission created the exact intended `vacancies` row. All test data (1 auth user, 1 profile, its `user_roles` row, 1 test vacancy) deleted afterward scoped to the exact created ids, with the one REAL production vacancy (محلل أعمال) confirmed still present and untouched -- deliberately not swept up by an attribute-scoped delete. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (202 tests) all passed clean.

- **Redesigned the position edit UI on both org-structure screens after direct feedback with a screenshot** (2026-08-05): "تم اضافة تحرير المرجعية لكن للأسف الامور متداخلة فلا تعرف المنصب من المرجعية من غيرها" (the parent/reporting-line edit was added, but things are now tangled together -- you can't tell the position from the parent selector from the rest), asking for a professional, clear redesign. `OrgStructurePositionMiniRow.tsx`'s edit mode (`/admin/org-structure`) had grown into one flat, unlabeled flex row once the 2026-08-05 parent selector joined the pre-existing name_ar/name_en/org-unit fields -- every field used the same bare styling with no label at all, so nothing distinguished the position's own identity from its two organizational links. Rebuilt as a bordered "now editing" card (new `.sru-position-edit-*` rules in globals.css, tinted with the existing `--sru-purple-light`/`--sru-purple` tokens -- no new palette introduced) split into two explicitly named, individually-labeled groups: "بيانات المنصب" (name_ar, name_en) and "التبعية والربط التنظيمي" (the parent select, the org-unit select) -- reusing the exact `positionNameArLabel`/`positionNameEnLabel`/`positionParentLabel`/`positionOrgUnitLabel` translation keys `AddOrgStructurePositionForm` already established, just applied here for the first time to an inline row-level editor instead of a page form. The actions row (save/cancel/delete) also gained a visual divider before the destructive delete button, separating it from save/cancel. Applied the identical labeled-field treatment to `OrgStructurePositionRow.tsx`'s equivalent cells on the staffing screen's table (`/admin/org-structure/staffing`) -- the exact same three fields were previously stacked with zero labels in that table's "المنصب" column too -- closing the same class of gap on the second screen rather than fixing only the one in the screenshot, per this project's own repeated lesson about a fix landing in only one of two places that both needed it.

- **A real process mistake happened and was caught mid-task, not silently absorbed**: while researching the fix, a CSS edit was accidentally applied directly to the shared main checkout (`C:\SRU\sru-hr-performance`) instead of an isolated worktree -- caught immediately via `git status` showing the edit alongside a concurrent session's own unrelated uncommitted files, reverted cleanly with a scoped `git checkout -- src/app/globals.css` (verified via `git diff --stat` showing empty) without touching any of that other session's in-progress work, and the CSS was then correctly reapplied inside a fresh worktree via a saved patch.

- **Downstream consequence discovered later, disclosed rather than hidden**: PR #146 ("Fall back to the job title's own requirements when no transition exists", a same-day unrelated vacancies fix from a concurrent session sharing the same main checkout) ended up carrying that exact same 86-line CSS block in its own diff -- the concurrent session's own commit evidently staged the whole working tree, including this session's briefly-live accidental edit, before it was reverted. The content itself is a harmless, correct, purely-additive CSS block (confirmed identical to what this PR also needed), so no functional harm resulted and nothing needed fixing in that other PR, but it is a real illustration of exactly why this project's worktree-isolation discipline exists -- a same-directory edit, even one immediately caught and reverted, can still leak into a concurrent session's commit in the narrow window before the revert lands. Verified live end-to-end with a real temporary `super_admin` test account against the real production hierarchy: opened a real position's ("النائب المساعد للتميز الأكاديمي") edit card on `/admin/org-structure`, confirmed via computed styles the card renders with the correct purple-tinted background/border and exactly 2 group labels + 4 field labels, confirmed the staffing table's equivalent row renders the same labeled fields (195 total field instances across the real 49 positions) with the root position ("الرئيس التنفيذي") correctly still showing plain "جذر" text with no field wrapper (parent forced null, unchanged behavior). Cancelled without saving to avoid touching the real position, then directly re-queried the database to confirm its `parent_id`/`org_unit_id` and the real 49-position total were completely unaffected. All temporary test data deleted afterward. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (211 tests, unchanged -- a pure JSX/CSS restructuring of already-tested edit flows, no new pure function needing dedicated coverage) all passed clean.

- **Filled the missing `salary_scale` rows for 19 leadership job titles** (2026-08-05, migration `20260805000001_salary_scale_missing_leadership_titles.sql`), closing most of the gap the recruitment-plan work surfaced (23 of 359 job titles had no salary row, so plan items built from them showed no estimated cost).

- **Nothing invented**: this schema's scale is per `(grade_level, category)`, not per title — an established fact from the original 2026-07-20 import and already relied on by 20260720000004 — so each new row COPIES the single real figure set that already exists for that title's own grade+category (grade 12 -> 10,500..19,500; 13 -> 14,700..27,300; 14 -> 20,580..38,220; 16 -> 40,337..74,911 with `annual_increase_cap` 5). The generator asserts each `(grade, category)` has EXACTLY ONE distinct figure set and aborts rather than guessing if it doesn't; `effective_date` matches the table's single existing value (2026-07-20) so the new rows belong to the same scale version, and every INSERT is `NOT EXISTS`-guarded so a re-run is a no-op.

- **The 4 deans (عميد كلية ...) are deliberately still unfilled, and this is the substantive finding**: they are `grade_level=14` with `category='academic'`, but the academic track in this database has real figures only for grades 1-9 (أستاذ = grade 9, 23,849..55,648 across 9 steps A-I), while grade 14 exists only on the leadership track (20,580..38,220, 7 steps). So grade number alone does NOT determine salary here — the two tracks share the 1-16 number space with different meanings — and copying grade-14 leadership figures would cap a dean BELOW a full professor's mid-step (38,220 vs 39,749), almost certainly wrong. Their real figures were asked of the project owner rather than derived; also flagged: the deans' grade 14 is itself an `[استنتاج]` from the 2026-07-20 backfill (deans had no source-data grade), so if a dean belongs to the academic track their grade arguably belongs in 1-9, which is a data correction wider than adding salary rows. Verified by dry-running the whole migration inside a rolled-back transaction first (19 x `INSERT 0 1`, 336 -> 355 rows, only the 4 deans left unfilled, and every `(grade, category)` still showing exactly ONE distinct figure set — i.e. the copies match their peers), then applying for real and re-querying: 355 rows, the same 4 deans outstanding, and spot-checks of رئيس الجامعة / مدير الإدارة الهندسية / رئيس مكتب البحث العلمي matching their grade peers exactly. Prepared from an isolated git worktree, since the shared checkout is in use by parallel sessions.

- **"إدخال في الخطة" no longer turns into "إخراج من الخطة" — the status itself now says "بانتظار الاعتماد", with the undo as an icon beside it** (2026-08-08), from a screenshot of طلبات الاحتياج: pressing "إدخال في الخطة" swapped that same button to "إخراج من الخطة", which reads as though the row had moved backwards rather than forwards. Two changes, both made in the workflow table rather than in a component: `requestStatusLabels.included_in_plan` changed from "مُدرج في الخطة" to "بانتظار الاعتماد" (the row IS in the plan, but the plan is not approved yet — that is the fact the reader needs), and `TransitionRule` gained an optional `statusAdjacent` flag, set today only on `included_in_plan -> under_hr_review`. A new `src/components/RequestStatusCell.tsx` renders the status pill plus an `Undo2` icon for every transition the table marks `statusAdjacent`, and `RecruitmentRequestActions` filters those same rules OUT of the actions column — so the rule is declared once, neither component holds workflow knowledge of its own, and a future status-adjacent transition appears in both places with no component change. Which icons render still comes from `availableRequestTransitions(status, permissions)`, so a caller without `recruitmentPlan>=recommend` sees the status with no icon at all, and the Server Action re-reads the true status and re-runs the same guard, so an icon rendered from stale props cannot force anything.

- **Also fixed the bare "تعذر إتمام العملية." visible in the same screenshot**: every non-workflow failure (a row deleted or advanced by someone else, an expired session, a malformed payload, a duplicate) collapsed into that one generic string with nothing to act on — added five distinct message keys in both locales behind a new shared `src/lib/recruitmentRequestErrors.ts` (`recruitmentRequestErrorText`), used by both components rather than duplicated in each; the workflow's own precise refusals ("لا تملك صلاحية تنفيذ هذا الإجراء", "يجب كتابة سبب واضح") still take precedence and are unchanged. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with a real temporary `hr_admin` test account (confirmed by direct query to hold the real `recruitmentPlan=recommend` grant — re-queried rather than assumed, per this file's own repeated lesson that the matrix drifts) and a real temporary request seeded at `included_in_plan`: the status cell rendered "بانتظار الاعتماد" with exactly one icon titled "إخراج من الخطة" and an EMPTY actions column (no duplicate button anywhere); clicking the icon flipped the row to `under_hr_review` in the database with a matching `audit_log` row, and the screen re-rendered showing "قيد مراجعة الموارد البشرية" with the three forward actions back in the actions column and no adjacent icon; clicking "إدراج في الخطة" returned it to "بانتظار الاعتماد" with the icon back — the exact loop from the report, now reading as a status plus a correction rather than a button that changes its mind. Production `recruitment_requests` was confirmed EMPTY both before and after (the two rows seen earlier in this session had been removed outside it), so only the one clearly-temporary seeded row was ever written. All test data (1 auth user, 1 profile, its `user_roles` row, 1 request, its audit rows) deleted afterward scoped to the exact created ids — confirmed zero residual rows. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (349 tests — 3 new covering the status-adjacent split) all passed clean.

- **Every competency on a demand request now carries its required level** (2026-08-08), from the observation that the one real production request had all 6 of its competencies stored with `required_level = NULL`. `recruitment_request_competencies.required_level` has existed since the table was created (20260807000002, a nullable `behavioral_level` mirroring `job_title_competencies`) but **nothing ever wrote it** — the create form collected `competencyIds: string[]` and the action inserted `{request_id, competency_id}` with no level at all, so a request could name a competency without saying how well it must be held, which is most of the point of naming it.

- **No migration**: the column, its enum, and the Arabic labels (`behavioralLevelLabels` in `src/lib/data/competencies.ts` — أساسي/ممارس/متقدم/محترف) all already existed and are reused rather than re-declared. The action's schema now takes `competencies: {competencyId, requiredLevel}[]` with the level as a required `z.enum` of the four real `behavioral_level` values, so an unlevelled pair is rejected at the boundary rather than silently written as NULL.

- **The catalogue does the work wherever it can**: `job_title_competencies.required_level` is NOT NULL, so `new/page.tsx` now selects it alongside the id and picking a job title seeds each competency AND the level that title actually requires — verified live that selecting "رئيس تنفيذي للقطاع" ticks its 6 real competencies at their real `professional` level, with the prefill note updated to say so. In the form, the level `<select>` appears only once a competency is ticked (a level on an unticked row is a setting with no subject), and ticking a competency the catalogue does not cover leaves the level genuinely unchosen — **deliberately NOT defaulted to `basic`**, since guessing a level nobody decided is precisely the failure this change exists to end; submission is blocked instead, with a counted message ("حدّد المستوى المطلوب لكل جدارة مختارة (N بلا مستوى)."). Internally the form holds a `CompetencyDraft` whose level may be `""` while it is still being filled in, narrowed to the action's fully-levelled shape on submit via `flatMap` rather than a cast — an unsound `undefined as unknown as BehavioralLevel` was written first and replaced once it was clear it modelled a lie. The "restore from job title" comparison now keys on the id AND the level, so changing only a level counts as an edit and offers the restore button. Styling follows the project's existing convention (`className="text-sm text-red-600"`) after a first attempt invented a `--sru-danger` token that does not exist anywhere in the kit — checked before shipping, per §7's "never use a color outside the SRU palette". Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with a real temporary test account on the `hr_admin` role (re-queried `role_permissions` first rather than assuming: `recruitmentRequests` is held by hr_admin=recommend, super_admin=approve, employees_coordinator=prepare, cxo=view): the catalogue prefill seeded all 6 competencies at `professional` with submit enabled; ticking an uncovered competency ("إدارة المخاطر") rendered a level select whose placeholder is `disabled`, **disabled the submit button**, and showed the counted warning; choosing "متقدم" for it and changing a seeded level ("روح الفريق" → "ممارس") re-enabled submit; the saved request then held all 7 links in the database with the exact levels chosen — `professional` ×5, `practitioner`, `advanced` — and **zero NULLs**.

- **Two things deliberately NOT done, flagged rather than bundled**: the 6 NULL levels on the one real existing request are left untouched (guessing levels for someone else's real request is exactly the fabrication this project forbids — and `updateRecruitmentRequest` does not touch competencies at all, so there is no UI to fix them either); and nothing anywhere DISPLAYS a request's competencies yet (no request detail page exists — `recruitment_request_competencies` is written by the create action and read by nothing), so these levels are currently stored correctly but invisible, which is the natural next slice. Test data deleted afterward scoped to the exact created ids, with an explicit guard refusing to touch the real request id — confirmed production back to exactly 1 request (the real draft, unchanged), 10 auth users, 10 profiles. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (380 tests) all passed clean.

- **The competencies on a draft request can now be edited, closing the gap flagged when levels were made mandatory** (2026-08-09). The inline row editor on طلبات الاحتياج (added by a concurrent session) already covered headcount/reason/contract/quarter/cost/qualifications, but `updateRecruitmentRequest` never touched `recruitment_request_competencies` at all — so a competency set, once created, was permanently frozen, and the one real production request left with six NULL levels had no route to correction.

- **No migration**: the existing `recruitment_request_competencies_{select,insert,update}` policies (20260807000010, gated at `check_vpra('recruitmentRequests','prepare', r.org_unit_id)` through the parent request) already permit everything this needs, and editability still comes from the action's own unchanged rule (`draft` or `returned_for_revision` — the latter exists precisely to be fixed, so it is included rather than narrowed to `draft` alone).

- **Extracted `src/components/CompetencyLevelPicker.tsx`** rather than writing a second picker: the create form's inline markup and helpers moved there wholesale and both surfaces now render the same component, because this project has already been bitten twice by a fix landing in only one of two places that both needed it (the org-unit-employees rollup, 2026-07-26/27). The module also owns `CompetencyDraft` (level may be `""` while choosing), `countMissingLevels`, `toSavedCompetencies` (the narrowing that removes any need for a cast) and `sameSelection` (keys on id AND level, so a changed level counts as an edit).

- **The reconciliation in `syncRequestCompetencies` is shaped by three real constraints, each checked in the schema first, not assumed**: there is NO DELETE policy on the link table (and §5-A rule 7 wants soft deletes anyway), so a removed competency is stamped `deleted_at`; uniqueness is a PARTIAL index (`(request_id, competency_id) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`) which PostgREST's `on_conflict` cannot target, so this is the established select-then-insert-or-update shape rather than an `.upsert()`; and re-adding a previously removed competency must REVIVE exactly one of its soft-deleted rows, since inserting a second live one would violate that same index after a remove/add cycle. Every statement goes through the caller's own client, so RLS stays the real gate and this function adds no authority of its own. `competencies` is optional on the update schema — omitted means "leave them alone" (what an older caller still means), while an empty ARRAY is a real instruction to clear them; the row editor always sends the field, so removing the last competency genuinely clears it.

- **Old NULL-level links are carried through honestly**: the page reads `required_level` as possibly NULL into the draft type's `""`, so an untouched pre-existing request opens the editor already blocked with its own count — which is the point, it says out loud what was missing all along — instead of a level being invented to satisfy a stricter type. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with a real temporary `hr_admin` test account.

- **On the real production request** (the one with 6 NULL levels): the editor loaded all 6 correctly ticked out of the 27-competency catalogue, every level select empty, save **disabled**, and the counted warning reading "(6 بلا مستوى)" — then it was cancelled and **nothing was written**, proven afterwards by comparing every link row (id, competency, level, deleted_at) against a snapshot taken before the session started and by `updated_at` still equalling `created_at` on the request itself.

- **On a temporary request** seeded with three levelled competencies: all three operations were exercised in one save — removed one, changed another's level (advanced→professional), added a new one — and the database confirmed exactly the intended result, with the removed row **soft-deleted rather than deleted** (total row count unchanged at 4) and the untouched competency left alone; then the removed competency was re-added at a DIFFERENT level and the database confirmed **one row, revived at the new level, not a duplicate** — the partial-index case this was written for. Test data deleted afterward scoped to the exact created ids with a guard refusing the real request id; production confirmed back to exactly 1 request with its 6 NULL levels intact and zero temporary accounts. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (373 tests) all passed clean.

- **Still open, deliberately**: the six real NULL levels are for the project owner to fill in through this new editor — guessing them would fabricate data — and nothing yet DISPLAYS a request's competencies outside the editor (no request detail page exists), so they remain visible only while editing.

- **Evaluation-cycle period fields are now enabled from the start** (2026-08-05): the previous entry kept the "pick the period type first" gate (rendering the duration/date fields disabled with an explanatory note rather than not at all) specifically because that ordering had been an earlier explicit request, and flagged the choice back to the project owner rather than reverting it unilaterally -- they answered "نعم فعّلها من البداية". Removed the `periodDisabled` derivation, the `disabled` binding on all three controls, and the now-unused `periodNeedsCycleType` message key in both locales. Nothing else changed: `cycleType` is still `required`, so the browser itself still blocks submission until it is filled in, whichever field the admin starts from -- verified live rather than assumed (`form.checkValidity()` returned false with a fully-filled period and no type, with `validity.valueMissing` true on the type select specifically, then true once a type was chosen). Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with a real temporary `hr_admin` test account, deliberately exercising the REVERSE order the old gate forbade: all six fields rendered enabled on load with no note, filling the start date first (2027-03-01, before touching the type) auto-computed 2028-02-29 at the default 12 months -- correctly landing on a real leap day -- then 2027-08-31 after switching to 6 months, and choosing the type LAST still submitted correctly, creating the exact intended `evaluation_cycles` row (`fiscal`, 2027-03-01 -> 2027-08-31). All test data (1 auth user, 1 profile, its `user_roles` row, 1 cycle) deleted afterward scoped to the exact created ids -- `evaluation_cycles` confirmed back to 0 rows. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (211 tests) all passed clean.

- **Announcement details form + "بوابة التوظيف" (Recruitment Portal) tab** (2026-08-05, migration `20260805000002_vacancy_announcement_details.sql`), per direct request: clicking a job in "الوظائف المعلن عنها" opens a form holding عدد الوظائف بهذا المسمى / بداية الإعلان / آخر موعد للتقديم, and a new tab lists the ads whose time has come. Three more nullable columns on `vacancies` (`openings_count` NOT NULL DEFAULT 1 with a `> 0` CHECK, `announcement_start_date`, `application_deadline`, plus a CHECK that the deadline is not before the start) — continuing 20260804000003's decision that these are facts ABOUT a vacancy that already carries its job title, org unit, requirements and status, so a separate announcements table would duplicate all of them. **[استنتاج] on the two dates being nullable**: the one real vacancy advertised before this migration has neither, and inventing dates for it would be fabricating data — so the portal falls back to "live from `announced_at`" when there is no start date and "open-ended" when there is no deadline. Both fallbacks are deliberately conservative: they never hide an ad the HR team explicitly advertised, they only skip scheduling that needs dates to exist. New `src/lib/vacancyPortal.ts` holds the rule as one unit-tested pure function (`vacancyPortalState` -> live/scheduled/expired/not_open, 10 new tests, 221 total) rather than duplicating it between the two screens; dates are compared as YYYY-MM-DD strings against "today" in the configured display timezone, never via `new Date(iso)`, the off-by-one-day class of bug this project already hit once. **[استنتاج] on status**: an ad whose vacancy is no longer `open` is NOT shown on the portal even inside its window (the portal is the outward-facing list; advertising a filled post to applicants would mislead) — but the management tab keeps showing it with an explicit "الشاغر غير مفتوح" state, so an ad never silently vanishes without a visible reason. `updateVacancyAnnouncementDetails` validates the window in the action as well as the DB CHECK, writes a before/after `audit_log` row, and goes through the caller's own client so `vacancies_update`'s existing RLS (`vacancies>=recommend`, hr_admin + manager) stays the only gate; the detail page renders the same form read-only for `view`-level callers rather than hiding it. The advertised-jobs list gained the job-title link plus openings / deadline / portal-state columns. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with three purpose-built temporary vacancies (one inside its window, one starting in the future, one past its deadline) and two real temporary accounts: as `hr_admin` the three ads rendered as منشور / لم يبدأ النشر بعد / انتهى موعد التقديم respectively, the portal showed exactly the live one plus the real production vacancy (which has no dates — proving the `announced_at` fallback), the form rejected a deadline-before-start window client-side with Save disabled, and a real save (openings 2 -> 5, deadline changed) was confirmed in the database with a matching before/after audit row; closing the in-window vacancy then removed it from the portal while the management tab showed "الشاغر غير مفتوح". As a plain `employee` (the real `vacancies=view` grant): the portal tab and its live ad were visible, the detail form rendered with all three inputs disabled, no Save button and the read-only note — and at the RLS layer the same update affected 0 rows.

- **Caught a real duplication while verifying, not assumed**: a scripted patch and a follow-up manual edit both inserted the openings/deadline cells, so the table briefly had 10 cells against 8 headers — spotted by comparing header and cell counts in the live DOM, then de-duplicated and re-checked (8/8). All test data (2 auth users/profiles/user_roles rows, 3 vacancies, 4 audit rows) deleted afterward scoped to exact ids — production confirmed back to exactly the 1 real vacancy, still advertised, `openings_count` 1 and no dates. Prepared from an isolated git worktree, since the shared checkout is in use by parallel sessions.

- **Date fields now read as day, month-name, year (`5 أغسطس 2026`)** (2026-08-06), per direct request with a screenshot of the announcement form showing `08 / 09 / 2026` — genuinely ambiguous between day-month and month-day order.

- **A native `<input type="date">` cannot be reformatted**: the browser draws it in its own locale format, and no CSS or attribute changes that — so this replaces the input rather than restyling it. New `src/components/DateFieldDmy.tsx` renders three selects (day / month NAME / year) and keeps the value in and out as `YYYY-MM-DD`, so callers, the Server Action and the database all keep the exact shape they already used. Month names follow the page locale (يناير…ديسمبر in Arabic, January…December in English — the request's own `October` example rendered as أكتوبر in the Arabic UI, matching Saudi Gregorian usage; the exact separator was corrected mid-review from a first `03/أكتوبر/2026` draft to the requested `5 أغسطس 2026` — spaces, no leading zero, no slashes). Impossible dates are unreachable by construction: the day list re-lengths with the chosen month and the value CLAMPS (31 January -> September becomes the 30th, February 2026 the 28th), with the real leap-year rule. New `src/lib/dateParts.ts` holds the arithmetic as pure, unit-tested functions (11 new tests, 232 total) — all string-based on `YYYY-MM-DD`, never `new Date(iso)`, the UTC-parse that shifts the calendar day backwards and which this project already hit for real in the org-structure Excel import. The same `formatDateDmy` now renders the read-side too: the advertised-jobs list's announcement/deadline columns and the portal card's deadline, so the whole flow reads one way.

- **Found and fixed a real bug in this control by live-testing it, not by review**: the first version derived its three parts purely from the `value` prop, which made a date impossible to ENTER — choosing the day emits `""` (still incomplete), the parent stores `""`, and the next render forgets the day just chosen, so the parts never accumulate; the Save button stayed disabled forever. Caught by trying to type a real date through the actual form and seeing nothing register. Fixed by holding the in-progress parts in local state (adopted from `value` whenever the parent changes it from outside, ignoring the `""` the control itself emits mid-entry), derived during render rather than in an effect, per this project's `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rule. Scope is deliberately the announcement form only (the screen in the request); five other screens still use native date inputs (`NewEvaluationCycleForm`, `EvaluationCycleRow`, `EmployeeInviteForm`, `EditEmployeeForm`, the user-activity range filter) — rolling the control out there is a natural follow-up, not silently bundled here. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with a real temporary `hr_admin` account: on the real advertised vacancy the control loaded its stored date correctly (09 / أغسطس / 2026) and the clamping behaved (31 Jan -> September showed 30 options and clamped to 30, February to 28) **without saving**, confirmed by re-querying that the real row was untouched; then on a temporary vacancy a full entry of `03 / أكتوبر / 2026` and `15 / نوفمبر / 2026` saved and came back as `2026-10-03` / `2026-11-15` in the database, and clearing the deadline's month back to "—" persisted it as NULL.

- **Also worth recording**: the real vacancy already had its dates set by the project owner through the UI (audit row 2026-08-05) — start 2026-08-09, deadline 2026-08-30 — so the earlier offer to set dates for it was already answered by them; checking before writing is why nothing was overwritten. All test data (1 auth user/profile/user_roles row, 1 temporary vacancy, 3 audit rows) deleted afterward scoped to exact ids — production confirmed back to exactly the 1 real vacancy with the project owner's own dates intact. Prepared from an isolated git worktree, since the shared checkout is in use by parallel sessions.

- **Rolled the day/month-name/year control out to the remaining five screens** (2026-08-06), per direct request after it shipped on the announcement form. There is now **no native `<input type="date">` left anywhere in `src/`** (grep-confirmed; the only remaining match is the explanatory comment inside the control itself). Converted: `NewEvaluationCycleForm`, `EvaluationCycleRow`, `EmployeeInviteForm`, `EditEmployeeForm`, and the user-activity range filter — ten inputs in total.

- **The rollout needed two capabilities the first version didn't have**, both added rather than worked around: (1) an **uncontrolled mode** (`defaultValue` instead of `value`/`onChange`) and (2) an optional **`name`** that renders a hidden input carrying the same `YYYY-MM-DD` the native input used to submit — so FormData-based forms (employee invite/edit) and the plain `method="get"` filter form keep sending exactly the same fields to the server, with zero changes on the receiving side. The user-activity page is a Server Component, so its two fields moved into a small `UserActivityRangeFields` client wrapper rather than making the page a client component.

- **A real regression was prevented, not discovered later**: the evaluation-cycle form's dates carried `required`, and browsers do not validate hidden inputs — so that guarantee would have quietly disappeared. The submit button is now disabled while either date is empty, keeping the same promise without native validation. The read side was aligned too where the same dates are displayed: the cycle row's start/end cells and the employee detail page's hire-date/date-of-birth now render through `formatDateDmy` instead of raw ISO. Verified live end-to-end against the real shared database with a real temporary `super_admin` account whose profile carried real dates: the cycle form (the hardest integration — controlled AND updated from outside) took `1 يناير 2027`, wrote `startDate=2027-01-01` to its hidden input, and the 12-month preset filled the end date, which the control then DISPLAYED as `31 ديسمبر 2027` with the duration note reading "12 شهرًا (365 يومًا)" — proving external updates flow back into the control; the employee-invite form's hire date produced `hireDate=2026-08-05` in its hidden input; the user-activity filter submitted and came back at `?period=custom&from=2026-07-30&to=2026-08-06` with 8 rows rendered, i.e. the GET contract is unchanged; and the employee detail page showed `15 مارس 2024` / `2 نوفمبر 1990`. All test data (1 auth user/profile/user_roles row, 1 audit row) deleted afterward scoped to exact ids. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (233 tests) all passed clean. Prepared from an isolated git worktree, since the shared checkout is in use by parallel sessions.

- **Made the two announcement dates tell themselves apart** (2026-08-07), after the project owner reported an advertised job showing `تاريخ الإعلان 5 أغسطس` while the form held `9 أغسطس`, concluding that was why it never reached بوابة التوظيف.

- **The logic was correct and nothing was broken** — checked against the real row before changing anything: `announced_at` = 2026-08-05 (when the advertise action was taken) and `announcement_start_date` = 2026-08-09 (when the portal should start showing it) are two different facts, and with today = 2026-08-07 the ad is genuinely still scheduled. The defect was in the UI: two labels that read almost identically ("تاريخ الإعلان" vs "بداية الإعلان"), and the advertised-jobs list showed the one that does NOT govern visibility while hiding the one that does — so a manager could see a date in the past and reasonably conclude the feature was failing. Fixed by naming and completeness, not by changing behaviour: the list column is now "أُعلن في", a new "بداية النشر" column shows `announcement_start_date` (with "من لحظة الإعلان" when it is empty, which is the real fallback rule), the deadline column says "بلا موعد نهائي" instead of a bare dash when open-ended, and the portal-state cell now names the date that decides it — "لم يبدأ النشر بعد / يبدأ النشر في 9 أغسطس 2026" and "انتهى في …" — since a bare "not published yet" is exactly what reads as a malfunction. The form label also became "بداية النشر في البوابة" to match. Verified live against the real shared database with a real temporary `hr_admin` account: the row rendered `أُعلن في 5 أغسطس 2026` / `بداية النشر 9 أغسطس 2026` / `آخر موعد 30 أغسطس 2026` with the state cell reading "لم يبدأ النشر بعد — يبدأ النشر في 9 أغسطس 2026", and the detail form showed the renamed label; the real vacancy was re-queried afterward and confirmed unchanged (no data was written by this fix). All test data deleted afterward. `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npx eslint`, and the full `vitest run` suite (233 tests) all passed clean.

- **موديول «خطة التوظيف» بدورة اعتماد كاملة (Recruitment Plan workflow)** (2026-08-07، الهجرات 20260807000001–20260807000006، فرع `Recruitment_plan`): بُني على 6 مراحل بطلب مباشر من مالك المشروع، **فوق** موديول التوظيف القائم لا بديلًا عنه («ابني فوقه» — قراره الصريح بعد عرض تقرير الاستكشاف عليه، والذي كشف أن المواصفة المقدَّمة تفترض Prisma وطبقة services وأدوارًا بأسماء لا وجود لأي منها هنا).

- **أهم قرار تصميمي**: الفاعلون الأربعة (رافع الطلب / الموارد البشرية / المالية / صاحب الاعتماد) معرَّفون بمجال العملية ومستوى VPRA **لا بـ role_code إطلاقًا**، لأن مالك المشروع اختار إنشاء الأدوار الجديدة من محرّر `/admin` وقت التشغيل فلا وجود لأكوادها في أي هجرة؛ والتمييز بين رئيس القسم والموارد البشرية يقع على المستوى (`prepare` مقابل `recommend`) بدلالات VPRA الموثّقة في §4 نفسها، فلا يعود المحرّك بحاجة لمعرفة أي دور استوفى الشرط — وهذا يتفادى بنيويًا التباس «دور فردي ودور إشرافي يتقاسمان مستوى واحدًا» الذي أوقع هذا المشروع سابقًا في `evaluations`/`bau_tasks` واضطره لرفع الحاجز إلى `approve`. `recruitmentBudget` مجال جديد (الرابع والعشرون) للمراجعة المالية، **بلا أي منح مزروع عمدًا** (أقل امتياز، §4-B). جدول `recruitment_requests` يحمل `org_unit_id` فتستخدم سياسته `check_vpra()` المقيَّد بالوحدة لا `check_vpra_global()` — وهذه هي آلية «رئيس القسم يرى طلبات قسمه» كاملةً، مفروضة من Postgres عبر `is_org_unit_in_scope()` بلا أي ترشيح في التطبيق. `src/lib/recruitmentWorkflow.ts` جدول تحويلات صريح (10 للطلب، 10 للخطة) هو المرجع الوحيد لأي تغيير حالة، وأي زوج غير معرَّف مرفوض ولو بصلاحيات كاملة (اختبار يغطي الـ39 والـ54 زوجًا غير المعرَّف محسوبةً تلقائيًا من عدد الحالات، فأي تحويل يُضاف لاحقًا تشمله التغطية من تلقائه). الحالة الحالية لا تُقبل من العميل أبدًا (§4-A) بل تُقرأ من الصف، مع تحكم تزامن متفائل يمنع دهس قرار مستخدم آخر.

- **حُذف الاعتماد المسطّح القائم** (`approveRecruitmentPlan`) لأنه كان يضبط الحالة مباشرة بعد فحص مستوى واحد، فلو بقي لأتاح اعتماد خطة لم تراجعها المالية أو فيها طلبات لم يُفصل فيها — أي لجعل الدورة كلها قابلة للتجاوز من ترويسة الخطة.

- **ثلاث ثغرات RLS حقيقية اكتُشفت بالتشغيل الفعلي لا بالمراجعة**: (1) `20260807000003` — رئيس القسم لم يرَ أي وحدة تنظيمية فكان النموذج المبني له تحديدًا غير قابل للاستخدام (`org_units`/`job_titles`/`competencies` تتطلب مجالات لا يملكها)، أُصلحت بنفس شكل إصلاحَي migration 14 و`20260719000008`، مع إبقاء فرع الوحدات مقيَّدًا بالنطاق فما يظهر في القائمة هو بالضبط ما ستقبله سياسة الكتابة (تطابق بالبناء لا بالصدفة)؛ (2) `20260807000004` — الدمج أنتج بندًا بتكلفة NULL رغم وجود صف حقيقي في `salary_scale`: منطق الاشتقاق سليم لكنه كان يقرأ جدولًا لا يراه صاحب الصلاحية، وأُضيف الفرع عند مستوى `recommend` تحديدًا لا `view` لأن أرقام الرواتب أكثر حساسية فلا يراها رئيس القسم؛ (3) `20260807000005` — ثغرة في تصميم `20260807000002` نفسه: أُعطي المراجع المالي وصولًا إلى الطلبات دون الخطة، فما كان ليقرأ الخطة التي يراجعها ولا يسجّل مراجعته (0 صفوف في القراءة والكتابة معًا).

- **الإشعارات**: `notifications` كان مذكورًا في §6 ولم يُبنَ قط، وجرس الشريط العلوي كان زخرفيًا بلا `onClick` ولا جدول خلفه منذ بداية المشروع (نفس حالة زر الدخول المُصلَحة 2026-07-18)، والمواصفة تقول «استخدم النظام إن وُجد» — فبُني النظام الحقيقي خلف الجرس نفسه لا بجانبه. الخصوصية أشد من بقية المخطط عمدًا: **لا فرع إشرافي إطلاقًا في القراءة** (لا أحد يطّلع على صندوق غيره ولو كان super_admin) و**لا سياسة INSERT للمستخدمين البتة**، فلا يمكن تلفيق إشعار يزعم اعتماد طلب؛ الكتابة من الخادم بصلاحية الخدمة كأثر جانبي لإجراء مُصرَّح أصلًا. المستقبِلون يُحدَّدون بالصلاحية ومن جدول التحويلات نفسه، فأي قاعدة تُضاف لاحقًا تصل لأصحابها بلا تعديل. `audit_log` نال سياسة ثانية ضيقة مقصورة على كيانَي التوظيف فقط بنفس شكل سابقتها للترقيات والمكافآت (`20260719000010`)، وكل الأنواع الأخرى تبقى غير مقروءة.

- **قرارات موثّقة بما لم يُبنَ ولماذا**: لا عمود `total_estimated_cost` مخزَّن (يُحسب عند القراءة فلا ينحرف إن نسي مسار كتابة مستقبلي إعادة الحساب — وهذا يحقق قاعدة المواصفة نفسها أقوى من عمود مخزَّن)، ولا جدول `ApprovalAuthority` (النظام يوفّر التهيئة أصلًا عبر `recruitmentPlan = approve` المنقولة من `/admin`)، ولا مكتبة PDF (`sru-print.css` موجود لهذا الغرض وكل شاشات المشروع تطبع عبر المتصفح)، ولا شاشة اعتماد منفصلة (صفحة تفاصيل الخطة هي شاشة الملخص المطلوبة نفسها). قواعد `@media print` لإخفاء الشريط والقائمة الجانبية أُضيفت في ذيل `globals.css` لا بتعديل `sru-print.css` (كتب الهوية لا تُعدَّل، §7)، وتحرّر أيضًا `height`/`overflow` قشرة التطبيق وإلا لطُبعت أول شاشة فقط من خطة طويلة — وهذا ما سمح لصفحة الطباعة بالبقاء تحت `(app)` محتفظةً ببوابة المصادقة المركزية الواحدة بدل إعادة تنفيذها خارجها.

- **التحقق**: 338 اختبارًا، وكل مرحلة من الست تحققت حيًّا على قاعدة الإنتاج بحسابات مؤقتة حقيقية للأدوار الأربعة — أهمها أن منعَي الاعتماد أُثبتا على الخادم لا نظريًا («لا يمكن الاعتماد قبل إتمام المراجعة المالية» ثم «الخطة تحتوي طلبات لم يُفصل فيها» حتى استُوفيا)، وأن صاحب الاعتماد عند حالة «مرفوعة للمراجعة» لا يملك أي زر إطلاقًا فالخطة لا تتخطى المالية بنيويًا، وأن المراجع المالي بلا أي منح على `recruitmentPlan` يرى الطلبات وأسماء المسميات والوحدات بلا خانات فارغة وبلا أي زر إجراء، وتعديله يصيب 0 صف.

- **ملاحظة تشغيلية مهمة**: الإشعارات تتوزع على أصحاب الصلاحية **الحقيقيين**، فاختبار واحد كتب في صندوق حساب إنتاجي فعلي (سلوك صحيح لا خلل) — أي اختبار مستقبلي يجب أن يحذف `notifications` بالمعرّف الدقيق للكيان، وقد أُضيف ذلك لسكربت التنظيف.

- **القراران المعلَّقان أُغلقا في اليوم نفسه (2026-08-07)**: أُنشئ دور `finance_manager` («مدير الشؤون المالية»، مخصّص لا نظامي) ومُنح `recruitmentBudget = recommend` — لم يكن هناك دور مالي قبله البتة؛ وفُصلت التوصية عن الاعتماد بخفض `hr_admin` من `approve` إلى `recommend` ورفع `super_admin` من `view` إلى `approve` (سلطة مؤقتة، المقصود نقلها للرئيس التنفيذي). كل ذلك بيانات تشغيل لا هجرات، لأن الأدوار تُنشأ من `/admin` بحكم التصميم. متحقَّق بمحاكاة الأدوار حيًّا: `hr_admin` صار `approve=false` محتفظًا بـ`recommend`/`prepare` (ما زال ينشئ الخطط ويدمج ويرى سلم الرواتب للتسعير)، و`super_admin` صار `approve=true`، والدور المالي يقرأ الخطة ويسجّل مراجعته دون أي منح على `recruitmentPlan` ولا يرى سلم الرواتب ولا ينشئ خطة.

- **أثر أصيل مسجَّل**: رفع دور إلى `approve` يمنحه تلقائيًا كل ما دونه، فصاحب الاعتماد صار يرى سلم الرواتب أيضًا — نموذج VPRA سُلَّمي ولا وجود لـ«اعتماد بلا ما دونه»؛ معتمِدٌ لا يرى الرواتب يحتاج مجالًا منفصلًا للاعتماد وهو قرار أكبر لم يُتخذ.

- **ولا تُعامَل هذه المستويات كحقيقة عن اليوم**: `role_permissions` قابل للتعديل من `/admin` وقت التشغيل، وقد ضُلِّل هذا المشروع مرتين بلقطة موثّقة تقادمت — أي قرار يعتمد على «من يملك ماذا» يجب أن يُعيد الاستعلام من الجدول. التوثيق الكامل في `docs/recruitment-module.md` بما فيه الخطافات المستقبلية الثلاث (الإعلان الداخلي ثم الخارجي، سدّ الاحتياج بترقية داخلية بدل تعيين، ربط المشاريع الاستراتيجية) مع الخطوة التالية المقترحة لكل منها.

## الجزء الثالث (2026-08-07 → 2026-08-29)
